Electron- K K -Phonon Interaction In Twisted Bilayer Graphene
Chao-Xing Liu ∗, Yulin Chen, Ali Yazdani, B. Andrei Bernevig ∗

Abstract
We develop an analytic theory to describe the interaction between electrons and K K -phonons and study its influence on superconductivity in the bare bands of twisted bilayer graphene (TBG). We find that, due to symmetry and the two-center approximation, only one optical K K phonon ( ∼ 160 ​ m ​ e ​ V \sim 160meV ) of graphene is responsible for inter-valley electron-phonon interaction. This phonon has recently been found in angular-resolved photo-emission spectroscopy to be responsible for replicas of the TBG flat bands. By projecting the interaction to the TBG flat bands, we perform the full symmetry analysis of phonon-mediated attractive interaction and pairing channels in the Chern basis, and show that several channels are guaranteed to have gapless order parameters. From the linearized gap equations, we find that the highest T c T_{c} pairing induced by this phonon is a singlet gapped s-wave inter-Chern-band order parameter, followed closely by a gapless nematic d-wave intra-Chern-band order parameter. We justify these results analytically, using the topological heavy fermion mapping of TBG which has allowed us to obtain an analytic form of phonon-mediated attractive interaction and to analytically solve the linearized and T = 0 T=0 gap equations. For the intra-Chern-band channel, the nematic state with nodes is shown to be stabilized in the chiral flat band limit. While the flat band Coulomb interaction can be screened sufficiently enough - around Van-Hove singularities - to allow for electron-phonon based superconductivity, it is unlikely that this effect can be maintained in the lower density of states excitation bands around the correlated insulator states.

Based on a recent experiment that suggest a strong coupling between the graphene
K
-phonon and the flat bands in TBG
che
, we perform a comprehensive analysis of the electron-
K
-phonon (e-K-ph) interaction and the resulting phonon-mediated superconductivity on the bare flat bands of TBG. We develop an exhaustive numerical, analytical, and symmetry based description of the e-K-ph interaction in TBG and the symmetry classifications of the order parameter, and find the competing singlet gapped inter-Chern-band channel and nematic gapless intra-Chern-band channel. Armed with the heavy-fermion description of TBG
Song and Bernevig 2022
;
Călugăru et al. 2023
;
Hu et al. 2023a
;
Hu et al. 2023b
;

Datta et al. 2023
;
Yu et al. 2023
;
Chou and Sarma 2022
;
Zhou and Song 2023
, the form factors of the
K
-phonon induced attractive interaction can be analytically computed and matched well to a full numerical calculations. An analysis of the Coulomb screening shows that, due to the high density of states (DOS) of flat bands, the Coulomb interaction might be strongly renormalized down near the Van Hove singularities. However, it remains unclear if the Hartree-Fock bands of the correlated insulator, with the lower DOS, can provide a similar result.

Model Hamiltonian for electron-phonon interaction in TBG -

We consider the deformation potential type of theory, described by a tight-binding (TB) model for the electron Hamiltonian with the hopping parameters
depending on the atom positions
\tilde{\bf R}_{\alpha}^{l}={\bf R}^{l}+{\bf\tau}_{\alpha}^{l}+{\bf u}^{l}({\bf R}^{l}_{\alpha})
with a displacement field

{\bf u}^{l}({\bf R}^{l}_{\alpha}={\bf R}^{l}+{\bf\tau}_{\alpha}^{l})
, where
{\bf R}^{l}
and
{\bf\tau}_{\alpha}^{l}
label the lattice vector and
the sublattice atom position (
\alpha=A,B
) at the layer
l
, respectively. By treating
{\bf u}
as a perturbation, we expand the intra-layer Hamiltonian up to the linear order in
\mathbf{u}
(Supplementary Material (SM) Sec. II
sm2
). We only keep
\mathbf{u}
-independent terms for the inter-layer Hamiltonian for TBG, thus focusing on intra-layer electron-phonon (e-ph) interaction in this work.
As only the Dirac bands appear around the Fermi energy close to
\pm\mathbf{K}_{D}=\pm\frac{4\pi}{3a_{0}}(1,0)
in Brillouin zone (BZ) with lattice constant
a_{0}
in graphene, we also expand the Hamiltonian
around
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}
(
\eta=\pm
labelling two valleys) and focus on Dirac electrons around two valleys. Our full Hamiltonian consists of three parts

\displaystyle H=H_{el}+H_{ph}+H_{eph}.

(1)

Here
H_{el}
describes the Dirac electrons located at valley
\eta=\pm
momentum
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}
that are coupled through inter-layer tunnelings and is given by the Bistritzer-MacDonald (BM) model
Bistritzer and MacDonald 2011
(SM Sec. V
sm2
),

{\hat{H}}_{\rm el}=\sum_{\eta s}\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in{\rm MBZ}}\sum_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}h_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha,\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{k})c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,\eta,s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},\eta,s}

(2)

where
c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,\eta,s}
is the fermion annihilation operator
\mathbf{k}
is a momentum in the Moiré Brillouin zone (MBZ) (Fig.
1
b),
\alpha
is the sublattice index, and
s
is spin. The vector
\mathbf{Q}
belongs to the lattice set

\mathcal{Q}_{l\eta}=\{l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+n_{1}\mathbf{b}_{M1}+n_{2}\mathbf{b}_{M2}\ |\ n_{1,2}\in\mathbb{Z}\}
, where
l
is the layer index,

\mathbf{q}_{2}=k_{\theta}(\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},\frac{1}{2})
,
\mathbf{b}_{M1}=k_{\theta}(\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},\frac{3}{2})
,
\mathbf{b}_{M2}=k_{\theta}(-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},\frac{3}{2})

and
k_{\theta}=2|\mathbf{K}_{D}|\sin\frac{\theta}{2}
with
\theta
the twist angle.
h_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha,\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{k})
is given in SM Sec. V.A
sm2
.

{\hat{H}}_{\rm el}
exhibits
C_{6v}
and time-reversal symmetries, generated by valley-switching
\pi/6
-rotation along z axis (
\hat{C}_{6z}
), time reversal (
\hat{T}
), and
\pi
-rotation along y axis (
C_{2y}
), and valley-preserving
\pi
-rotation along x axis (
\hat{C}_{2x}
), and the composite anti-unitary
C_{2z}T
. In addition,
{\hat{H}}_{\rm el}
has a unitary particle-hole (
\hat{P}
) symmetry,
as well as a chiral symmetry
\hat{C}
in the limit with vanishing
AA
region hopping (
w_{0}=0
)
Vafek and Kang 2020
. A full discussion of symmetry of BM model
Bernevig et al. 2021
;
Song et al. 2021
is found in SM Sec.V.B
sm2
.

H_{ph}
describes the intra-layer in-plane phonon modes. Out-of-plane phonon modes are decoupled from Dirac electrons for intra-layer e-ph interaction.
The dynamical matrix for a single layer graphene is derived in SM Sec.III
sm2
based both on symmetry considerations and the microscopic model, up to the next nearest neighbor interaction. The resulting in-plane phonon dispersion in Fig.
1
a reproduces that in literature
Neto et al. 2009
;
Sahoo and Mishra 2012
;
Thingstad et al. 2020
;
Maultzsch et al. 2004
;
Mohr et al. 2007
(SM Sec.III and IV.B
sm2
).
The phonon modes at
\Gamma
and
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}
can induce intra-valley and inter-valley e-ph interactions, respectively. In this paper we focus on the
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}
phonons; the
\Gamma
phonons will be derived in
liu
.
At
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}
, we have one
A_{1}
(
\sim 160meV
), one
A_{2}
(
\sim 140meV
) and one 2D
E
mode (
\sim 150meV
) of
C_{3v}
group. Based on the deformation potential theory, we derive the e-ph interaction
H_{eph}
by expanding the TB Hamiltonian treating both the momentum
and phonon displacement field
\mathbf{u}
as perturbations. For e-ph interaction, we only keep the dominant zeroth-order in momentum for the
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}
phonons.
We find, due to both symmetry and the two-center approximation (SM Sec.II.E
sm2
), that only the
A_{1}
phonons at
\mathbf{K}_{D}
can scatter an electron from
\mathbf{K}_{D}

to
-\mathbf{K}_{D}

Mohr et al. 2007
. The corresponding Hamiltonian reads

\displaystyle H_{inter-vall}^{op,A_{1}}\approx\frac{\gamma_{3}}{\sqrt{2N_{G}M\omega_{A1}}}\sum_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}},\tilde{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},\eta,\alpha\beta}(b_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\tilde{\mathbf{k}}-\tilde{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},A_{1}}+b_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}-\tilde{\mathbf{k}}+\tilde{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},A_{1}}^{\dagger})c^{\dagger}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha}(\sigma_{x})_{\alpha\beta}c_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\beta},

(3)

where
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}
is the electron momentum away from
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}
,
N_{G}
is the number of atomic unit cells,
M
is the atomic mass,
\omega_{A1}
is the
A_{1}
phonon frequency, and
b
and
c
are phonon and electron annihilation operators. The material dependent parameter

\gamma_{3}
can be derived from the hopping potential as
\gamma_{3}=2i\sum_{\mathbf{G}}e^{i(\tau_{A}-\tau_{B})\cdot\mathbf{G}}(\mathbf{G}+\mathbf{K}_{D})_{y}t(\mathbf{G}+\mathbf{K}_{D},0)\approx 17\text{eV/ \AA}
, where
\mathbf{G}
is the reciprocal lattice vector and
t(\mathbf{q})
is the Fourier transform of the
\pi
-bond hopping function
between two carbon
p_{z}
orbitals in graphene (Eq. 6 in SM Sec.I
sm2
). Our next step is to re-write the electron momentum
\tilde{k}
into the MBZ by

\tilde{\mathbf{k}}=\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}
with
\mathbf{k}\in\text{MBZ}
, so that
c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta},\alpha,\eta,s}=c_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}^{l}+\tilde{\mathbf{k}},\alpha,l,s}

and
\sum_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\rightarrow\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\text{MBZ}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}}
, where we have added the spin index
s
and layer index
l
.
Finally, we project the e-ph interaction
H_{inter-vall}^{op,A_{1}}
into the flat bands of the BM Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle H_{inter-vall}^{op,A_{1}}\approx\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\sum G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta nn^{\prime}l}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta,s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n^{\prime},-\eta,s}

\displaystyle(b_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta},l,A_{1}}+b_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}-\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta},l,A_{1}}^{\dagger})

(4)

where the summation includes
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n,n^{\prime},\eta,s,l,\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}
,
\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta,s}^{\dagger}=\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha}u_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha;n\eta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\eta,\alpha s}^{\dagger}

with
u^{n}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta},\alpha,\eta}
the eigenstates of
h_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha,\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{k})
.
The matrix element

\displaystyle G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta nn^{\prime}l}=\frac{\gamma_{3}}{\sqrt{2M\omega_{A_{1}}}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta},\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle u^{n\star}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta},\alpha,\eta}\sigma^{x}_{\alpha\beta}u^{n^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta}-\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta},\beta,-\eta}

(5)

characterizes the e-ph interaction strength for TBG and can be evaluated numerically (and later analytically), as shown in SM Sec. VI.F
sm2
.
We focus on two flat bands (per valley per spin) of TBG, labelled by
n=\pm
. Instead of the eigen-state basis, we work on the so-called ”Chern-band” basis, defined by

u^{e_{Y}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,\eta}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(u^{n=+}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,\eta}+ie_{Y}u^{n=-}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,\eta})

(6)

with
e_{Y}=\pm 1
.
u^{e_{Y}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,\eta}
carries the Chern number
\pm 1
.
On the Chern-band basis, the expressions for e-ph interaction can be obtained by replacing the
n,n^{\prime}
indices in Eqs. (
Electron-
K
-Phonon Interaction In Twisted Bilayer Graphene
) and (
Electron-
K
-Phonon Interaction In Twisted Bilayer Graphene
) with

e_{Y},e^{\prime}_{Y}
indices and
u^{n}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,\eta}
in Eq. (
Electron-
K
-Phonon Interaction In Twisted Bilayer Graphene
) with
u^{e_{Y}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,\eta}
.
Discrete symmetries can constrain the form of the function

G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta e_{Y}e_{Y}^{\prime}l}
, as discussed in SM Sec.VI.D
sm2
.
In particular, in the chiral limit
w_{0}=0
one can show that
G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta e_{Y}e_{Y}^{\prime}l}=\delta_{e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta e_{Y}e_{Y}l}
has diagonal form on the Chern-band basis.

Phonon-mediated Electron-electron Interaction and Symmetry Classification of Superconducting Pairing Channels -

We next apply the Schrieffer-Wolff transformation
Schrieffer and Wolff 1966
to integrate out the phonon modes and obtain the phonon-mediated electron-electron (el-el) interaction
Wu et al. 2018
;
Yu et al. 2022a
. We focus on the Cooper pair channel of the attractive interaction, which takes the form

\displaystyle H_{ee}=-\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},s,s_{1},e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\gamma_{\mathbf{k}e_{Y}\eta s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k}e^{\prime}_{Y},-\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}e^{\prime}_{Y},\eta s_{1}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}e_{Y},-\eta s},

(7)

where

V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{N_{0}\omega_{A_{1}}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}_{M},l}G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{\eta,e_{Y},l}G_{-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}-\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{-\eta,e_{Y}^{\prime},l}

with
\mathbf{G}_{M}
the Moiré reciprocal lattice vectors,
N_{M}
the number of Moiré unit cells and
N_{0}
the number of atomic unit cells in one Moiré unit cell (
N_{G}=N_{0}\times N_{M}
). Discrete symmetries constrain the form of the interaction parameter
V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}
.
The ones leaving the momentum
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})
unchanged are: (1)

\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{P}
:
V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{-\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}
; (2)
\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{T}
:

V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{\eta,-e_{Y},-e_{Y}^{\prime}\star}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}
; and (3) the combination of index reshuffling and
\hat{P}
symmetry:

V^{\eta,e_{Y}e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{-\eta,e_{Y}^{\prime}e_{Y}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}
. These three symmetry operations reduce the number of the independent components
of the
V
-function for a fixed
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})
from 8 complex parameters to 1 real (
V^{+,+-}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}
) and 1 complex parameter (
V^{+,++}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}
). Other discrete symmetries, including
\hat{P}
, reshuffling, hermicity,
\hat{C}_{3z}
and
\hat{C}_{2z}
, relate the
V
-function at different
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})
. In particular,
\hat{C}_{3z}
guarantees
V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}}_{\mathbf{K}_{M},0}=0
for the intra-Chern-band channels. The projected Coulomb interaction into the flat bands of the BM model possesses a large

U(4)\times U(4)
spin-valley continuous symmetry
Bernevig et al. 2021
;
Kang and Vafek 2019
;
Bultinck et al. 2020
. the el-el interaction (
Electron-
K
-Phonon Interaction In Twisted Bilayer Graphene
) breaks this symmetry down to the
U(2)_{e_{Y}=+}\times U(2)_{e_{Y}=-}
in the chiral limit and further to a total spin
SU(2)
together with a valley charge
U(1)\otimes U(1)
(SM Sec.VI.E
sm2
).

At the mean field level, the attractive interaction (
Electron-
K
-Phonon Interaction In Twisted Bilayer Graphene
) is decomposed into the fermion bilinear form

H_{\Delta}=\hat{\Delta}+\hat{\Delta}^{\dagger}
with

\hat{\Delta}=\sum\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y_{1}},\eta,s_{1}}^{\dagger}\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{1}}s_{1},e_{Y_{2}}s_{2}}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k},e_{Y_{2}},-\eta,s_{2}}^{\dagger},

(8)

where the summation above includes the indices
\mathbf{k},e_{Y_{1}},e_{Y_{2}},s_{1},s_{2},\eta
and the gap function

\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{1}}s_{1},e_{Y_{2}}s_{2}}=-\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}V_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}^{\eta e_{Y_{1}}e_{Y_{2}}}\langle\gamma_{-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}e_{Y_{2}}\eta s_{2}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}e_{Y_{1}}-\eta s_{1}}\rangle.

(9)

Since the interaction
V
-function does not involve spin, we can decompose
\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{1}}s_{1},e_{Y_{2}}s_{2}}=\sum_{S,M}\Delta^{\eta,SM}_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{1}}e_{Y_{2}}}{\mathcal{S}}^{SM}_{s_{1}s_{2}}
, where
S=0
for spin singlet and
S=1
(
M=-S,...,S
) for spin triplet (SM Sec.VI.G.1
sm2
).

The gap function can be classified according to the discrete symmetries. The
C_{6v}
group includes four 1D irreducible representations (irreps), e.g.
A_{1,2}
and
B_{1,2}
, and two 2D irreps,
E_{1,2}
. 1D irreps
A_{1,2}
and
B_{1,2}
channels differ by their
\hat{C}_{2z}
eigen-values,
\lambda_{C_{2z}}=+1
for
A_{1,2}
and
\lambda_{C_{2z}}=-1
for
B_{1,2}
. Combining
\hat{C}_{2z}
and reshuffling symmetries leads to
\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{1}},e_{Y_{2}}}=\lambda_{C_{2z}}\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{2}},e_{Y_{1}}}
for spin singlet and
\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{1}},e_{Y_{2}}}=-\lambda_{C_{2z}}\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{2}},e_{Y_{1}}}
for spin triplet. Thus, for intra-Chern-band pairing (
e_{Y_{1}}=e_{Y_{2}}
), the
A_{1,2}
channel must be spin singlet while the
B_{1,2}
channel must be spin triplet. Furthermore, the rotation
\hat{C}_{3z}
ensures the existence of nodes at
\mathbf{K}_{M}
for the gap function of any 1D irrep intra-Chern-band channel (
\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{K}_{M};e_{Y},e_{Y}}=0
), while the inter-Chern-band channel does not have such constraint. The 2D irreps
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
have different
\hat{C}_{2z}
eigen-values,
\lambda_{C_{2z}}=+1
for
E_{2}
and
\lambda_{C_{2z}}=-1
for
E_{1}
, similarly to the 1D irrep case. Consequently, the
E_{2}
channel must be spin singlet while the
E_{1}
channel must be spin triplet for intra-Chern-band pairings.
\hat{C}_{3z}
guarantees nodes at
\Gamma_{M}
for both intra- and inter-Chern-band channels, and it requires additional nodes at
\mathbf{K}_{M}
for the inter-Chern-band channels for both 2D
E_{1,2}
pairings. Besides discrete
symmetries, the continuous
U(2)_{e_{Y}=1}\times U(2)_{e_{Y}=-1}
spin symmetry in the chiral limit guarantees the singlet and triplet pairings of inter-Chern-band channel to be degenerate in the chiral flat band limit. The full symmetry analysis of the gap functions can be found in SM Sec.VI.G
sm2
.

(a) Phonon dispersion of graphene. The irreps for phonon modes at

and

|\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}|
for the inter-Chern-band
A_{1}
singlet (or the

triplet) channel and intra-Chern-band pairing 2D
E_{2}
singlet channel, respectively.

along the dashed line
k_{y}=0
for both the inter-Chern-band (red) and intra-Chern-band (blue) channels.

{\bf\Gamma}_{M},\mathbf{K}_{M},{\bf M}_{M}
are labelled in MBZ in (b) and (d).

Gap Equations and Self-consistent Solution of Pairing Channels -

The linearized gap equation (LGE) for the attractive interaction (
Electron-
K
-Phonon Interaction In Twisted Bilayer Graphene
) can be derived by evaluating
\langle\gamma_{-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}e_{Y_{2}}\eta s_{2}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}e_{Y_{1}}-\eta s_{1}}\rangle
in Eq. (
9
) and expanding it to
linear order of the gap function. In the chiral flat band limit, e.g. the band width is much smaller than the critical temperature
T_{c}
,
the LGE is derived as

2k_{B}T\Delta^{\eta,SM}_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{1}}e_{Y_{2}}}=\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}V^{\eta e_{Y_{1}}e_{Y_{2}}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\Delta^{-\eta,SM}_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime};e_{Y_{1}}e_{Y_{2}}}.

(10)

This is eigen-equation problem for the matrix
V^{\eta e_{Y_{1}}e_{Y_{2}}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}
: the
T_{c}
is determined by the largest
eigen-value and the symmetry of the gap function is determined by that of its eigenvector. As mentioned, the only two independent components of the
V
-function (complex
V^{+++}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}
and real
V^{++-}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}
) leads to two independent LGEs for the intra- and inter-Chern-band channels, respectively.
The form of the LGE suggests that all the gap functions are doubly degenerate at
T_{c}
in the flat-band limit. They belong either to two degenerate 1D irreps or one 2D irrep. We first numerically solve these two LGEs from Eq. (
358
), and find the forms of the gap functions with the largest eigen-values, as shown in Fig.
1
.
Our numerical calculations show
k_{B}T_{c}\sim 0.21meV
for the inter-Chern-band channel, slightly larger than
k_{B}T_{c}\sim 0.16meV
for the intra-Chern-band channel. For the inter-Chern-band channels, the gap function is almost a constant in Fig.
1
a, featuring a fully gapped s-wave pairing with even
\hat{C}_{2z}
-parity (
A_{1}
or
A_{2}
irrep).
In the chiral flat-band limit, spin singlet and triplet pairings are degenerate, as required by the continuous
U(2)\times U(2)
spin symmetry
(SM Sec.VI.E
sm2
). Including kinetic energy splits this degeneracy and makes the spin singlet
A_{1}
irrep channel to have the highest
T_{c}
.
For the intra-Chern-band channel, one can see nodes appear at the
\Gamma_{M}
in Fig.
1
b. As our previous symmetry analysis shows that the gap function should have nodes at
\mathbf{K}_{M}
for the 1D irrep (
A_{1,2},B_{1,2}
) and
\Gamma_{M}
for the 2D irrep (
E_{1,2}
), numerical results should correspond to a 2D irrep. Numerically analyzing the symmetry property of the gap function suggests that the intra-Chern-band channel belongs to the 2D
E_{2}
irrep with spin singlet. Full numerical results are discussed in SM Sec.VI.H.2 and 3
sm2
.

Our results for the intra-Chern-band channels reveal a d-wave character of the gap. Using the heavy fermion formalism of TBG
Song and Bernevig 2022
;
liu
, we analytically obtain
V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}
:

\displaystyle V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=U^{*}_{e_{Y},\mathbf{k}}U_{e_{Y},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}};\;\;U_{e_{Y},\mathbf{k}}=\frac{\sqrt{V_{0}}}{k^{2}+b^{2}}k_{e_{Y}}^{2},

(11)

with
k_{e_{Y}}=k_{x}+ie_{Y}k_{y}
(
e_{Y}=\pm
). This interaction allows us to solve the LGE analytically to obtain the
T_{c}
,

k_{B}T_{c}=\frac{\tilde{V}_{0}}{2},\;\;\tilde{V}_{0}=\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}V_{0}\frac{k^{4}}{(k^{2}+b^{2})^{2}},

(12)

where
V_{0}
and
b
are material dependent parameters. The corresponding self-consistent gap function takes the d-wave form

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\Delta^{+,00}_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y}e_{Y}}\\
\Delta^{-,00}_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y}e_{Y}}\end{array}\right)=\Delta_{e_{Y}}\frac{k_{-e_{Y}}^{2}}{k^{2}+b^{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
1\end{array}\right)

with
e_{Y}=\pm
and
\Delta_{e_{Y}}
a parameter to be determined. Time reversal, if exists, requires

\left(\Delta^{-,00}_{\mathbf{k};--},\Delta^{+,00}_{\mathbf{k};--}\right)=\left(\Delta^{+,00}_{\mathbf{k};++},\Delta^{-,00}_{\mathbf{k};++}\right)^{*}
.
The d-wave nature of the gap function suggests the possibility of the nodal superconductivity. However, one should note that the single-particle
Hamiltonian is
not
diagonal in the Chern-band basis. The Bogoliubov-de Gennes (BdG) spectrum must be checked with kinetic energy added. The BdG Hamiltonian for the intra-Chern-band pairing is block diagonal and one block
\mathcal{H}_{BdG}^{+,+}
on the basis

(\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y}=\pm,+,s=\uparrow},\gamma_{-\mathbf{k},e_{Y}=\pm,-,s=\downarrow})
reads

\displaystyle H_{BdG}^{+,+}(\mathbf{k})=\begin{pmatrix}h_{+}(\mathbf{k})&\Delta^{+}_{\mathbf{k}}\\
(\Delta^{+}_{\mathbf{k}})^{\dagger}&-h_{-}^{\star}(-\mathbf{k})\end{pmatrix}

(18)

with
h_{\eta}(\mathbf{k})=(d_{0,\eta}(\mathbf{k})-\mu)\zeta^{0}+d_{x,\eta}(\mathbf{k})\zeta^{x}
and
\Delta^{+}_{\mathbf{k}}=Diag[\Delta^{+,00}_{\mathbf{k},++},\Delta^{+,00}_{\mathbf{k},--}]
.
Here
d_{0,\eta}(\mathbf{k})=(\epsilon_{+,\eta}(\mathbf{k})+\epsilon_{-,\eta}(\mathbf{k}))/2
and
d_{x,\eta}(\mathbf{k})=(\epsilon_{+,\eta}(\mathbf{k})-\epsilon_{-,\eta}(\mathbf{k}))/2
,
where
\epsilon_{\pm,\eta}(\mathbf{k})
are the eigen-energies for the two low-energy flat bands (per valley per spin) of the BM model
{\hat{H}}_{\rm el}
(
2
). The corresponding energy spectrum can possess nodes when the pairing amplitudes of two Chern-band channels are equal,
|\Delta_{e_{Y}=+}|=|\Delta_{e_{Y}=-}|=\Delta_{0}
, which corresponds to the Euler pairing discussed in Ref.
Yu et al. 2022b
;
Yu et al. 2022a
.
Point nodes appear at the location defined by two conditions (1)
\cos((\Phi_{\mathbf{k},-}-\Phi_{\mathbf{k},+})/2)=0
,
where
\Phi_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y}}=\varphi_{e_{Y}}-2e_{Y}\theta_{\mathbf{k}}
with
\Delta_{e_{Y}}=\Delta_{0}e^{i\varphi_{e_{Y}}}
and
k_{e_{Y}}=ke^{ie_{Y}\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}
;
and (2)
d_{x,\mathbf{k}}^{2}=(d_{0,\mathbf{k}}-\mu)^{2}+\Delta_{0,\mathbf{k}}^{2}
with
\Delta_{0,\mathbf{k}}=\Delta_{0}\frac{k^{2}}{k^{2}+b^{2}}
, as discussed in SM Sec.VI.H.5
sm2
. The first condition determines the momentum angle for the nodes while the second gives the momentum amplitude, thus together fixing the location of point nodes in the 2D momentum space.
We next solve the self-consistent gap equation at zero temperature for the interaction form
(
414
). With the gap function ansatz
\Delta_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y}}=\Delta_{e_{Y}}\frac{k^{2}_{-e_{Y}}}{k^{2}+b^{2}}
, we find a self-consistent gap equation

\Delta_{e_{Y}}=\frac{V_{0}}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},e_{Y_{1}}}\frac{{k^{\prime}}^{2}_{e_{Y}}}{k^{\prime 2}+b^{2}}u_{-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},e_{Y}e_{Y_{1}}}w^{*}_{-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},e_{Y}e_{Y_{1}}},

(19)

where
\psi_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y_{1}}}=(u_{\mathbf{k},\pm,e_{Y_{1}}},w_{\mathbf{k},\pm e_{Y_{1}}})
(
e_{Y_{1}}=\pm
) are the eigen-wave functions with the positive eigen-energies of the BdG Hamiltonian
H_{BdG}^{+,+}(\mathbf{k})
(
18
). Fig.
2
A shows the chemical potential dependence of the gap functions and the condensation energy. The Euler pairing
|\Delta_{+}|=|\Delta_{-}|
is always energetically favored for a non-flat band width
\sim 0.3meV
, quite different from chiral d-wave pairing in doped graphene
Black-Schaffer and Honerkamp 2014
;
Nandkishore et al. 2012
;
Wu 2019
. For the chemical potential
\mu
below
0.1meV
, a nodal superconductor phase with four point nodes (Fig.
2
B) located at the positions determined by two conditions discussed above
Yu et al. 2022a
. With increasing
\mu
, four nodes move towards
\Gamma_{M}
and eventually a gapped superconductor phase (Fig.
2
C) appears for
\mu>0.1meV
.

Figure 2:
(A) The superconductor order parameter amplitudes
|\Delta_{\pm}|
(red circles and blue crosses) and the ground state energy (black dots) as a function of
\mu
. The superconducting phase has nodes in the shadowed regime. (B) and (C) show the BdG spectrum with and without nodes at
\mu=0.04meV
and
0.14meV
, respectively. The single-particle bandwidth is set around
0.3meV
.

The energy scale of the Coulomb interaction in TBG is
\sim 24meV

Bernevig et al. 2021
, much larger than the estimated energy scale of e-K-ph mediated attractive interaction
\sim 0.3meV

not
. Near the Van-Hove singularities of flat bands, the screening can significantly reduce the Coulomb interaction to a similar order as e-K-ph mediated interaction due to the large DOS (SM Sec.VI.H.7
sm2
), thus making superconductivity from this mechanism possible. If, however, the DOS is that of the Hartree-Fock bands of correlated insulators, the screening might not be enough to reduce the Coulomb interaction. Hence, superconductivity from this
K
-phonon flat bare band mechanism could appear only when the correlated insulator states are suppressed.

Conclusion -

In conclusion, we develop a theory for the projected e-K-ph interaction of the flat bands and the resulting superconductor pairing channels in TBG. We find the inter-Chern-band s-wave singlet pairing and the intra-Chern-band d-wave nematic singlet pairing have highest
T_{c}
, and the
T_{c}
of inter-Chern-band channel is slightly higher than the intra-Chen-band channel. The intra-Chen-band channel can have nodes in a large parameter regime. From the estimate of the screened Coulomb interaction, we argue that this mechanism requires the correlated insulators to be suppressed.

Supplementary Material for ”Electron-
K
-Phonon Interaction In Twisted Bilayer Graphene”

I
Notation

In this section, we will first describe our notations for the whole paper.
We denote
{\bf a}_{1}^{l},{\bf a}_{2}^{l}
as the un-strained, microscopic lattice vectors of the graphene layer
l
(
l=\pm
for the top and bottom layers),
while
{\bf G}_{1,2}^{l}
are the corresponding reciprocal lattice vectors.
{\bf G}_{a}^{l}\cdot{\bf a}_{b}^{l}=2\pi\delta_{ab}
.
In the layer
l
, there are two sub-lattice positions
{\bf\tau}_{A}^{l},{\bf\tau}_{B}^{l}
. We assume the graphene layer
l
is rotated by an in-plane angle

l\theta/2
followed by a shift
l\frac{l_{0}}{2}\hat{z}
along the z direction,
so any vector
{\bf v}^{l}
is related to the un-rotated vector
{\bf v}
by
{\bf v}^{l}=\hat{R}^{l}_{\theta/2}{\bf v}+l\frac{l_{0}}{2}\hat{z}=l\frac{\theta}{2}\hat{z}\times{\bf v}+l\frac{l_{0}}{2}\hat{z}
, where
\hat{R}^{l}_{\theta/2}
labels the rotation operator,

\hat{z}
is the out-of-plane unit vector and
l_{0}
is the equilibrium distance between two graphene layers.
Thus, we only need to give the values of all the un-rotated vectors below.
Two primitive lattice vectors are
{\bf a}_{1}=a_{0}(\frac{-1}{2},\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},0)
and
{\bf a}_{2}=-a_{0}(\frac{1}{2},\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},0)
,
where
a_{0}=2.46\AA

is the lattice constant. The two primitive reciprocal lattice vectors spanning the Brillouin zone (BZ) are

{\bf G}_{1}=\frac{4\pi}{\sqrt{3}a_{0}}(-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},\frac{1}{2});\;\;{\bf G}_{2}=\frac{4\pi}{\sqrt{3}a_{0}}(-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},-\frac{1}{2}).

(20)

Since the Fermi surface in graphene is around two
K
-valley, we also label them as
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}
with
\mathbf{K}_{D}=\frac{4\pi}{3a_{0}}(1,0)
and
\eta=\pm
.
The atoms are at positions

{\bf\tau}_{A}=\frac{a_{0}}{2}(1,-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}),\;\;{\bf\tau}_{B}=\frac{a_{0}}{2}(1,\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}).

(21)

For two layers
l=\pm
, the atoms on position
\alpha=A,B
in the top/bottom later reside at

{\bf R}^{l}_{\alpha}=\hat{R}_{\theta/2}^{l}{\bf R}+l\frac{l_{0}}{2}\hat{z}+{\bf\tau}_{\alpha}^{l}={\bf R}^{l}+{\bf\tau}_{\alpha}^{l}

(22)

where
{\bf R}=m{\bf a}_{1}+n{\bf a}_{2}
is a unit cell vector.

The hopping between two atomic centers at a distance
r
is

\displaystyle t({\bf r}_{\parallel},z)=V_{pp\pi}(1-(\frac{{\bf r}\cdot\hat{z}}{r})^{2})e^{-(r-\frac{a}{\sqrt{3}})/r_{0}}+V_{pp\sigma}(\frac{{\bf r}\cdot\hat{z}}{r})^{2}e^{-\frac{r-l_{0}}{r_{0}}}

(23)

where
{\bf r}_{\parallel}=(x,y)
,
r_{0}
characterizes the decaying length of atomic orbitals of carbon atoms,

a=a_{0}/\sqrt{3}
is the distance between the
A
and
B
atoms, and
V_{pp\pi}
and
V_{pp\sigma}
are the hybridization parameters of
\pi

and
\sigma
bonds. We performed the Fourier transform to the in-plane coordinate of
t({\bf r}_{\parallel},z)
,

t_{{\bf q},z}=\frac{1}{S_{0}}\int d^{2}{\bf r}_{\parallel}e^{-i{\bf q}\cdot{\bf r}_{\parallel}}t({\bf r}_{\parallel},z)

(24)

where
S_{0}
is the unit cell area.

Figure 3:
Lattice and Brillouin zone for graphene.

In the discussion below, we mainly focus on the in-plane hopping term, and direct calculation gives

t_{{\bf q},0}=\frac{V_{pp\pi}e^{\frac{a_{0}}{\sqrt{3}r_{0}}}}{S_{0}}\frac{2\pi r_{0}^{2}}{(1+(qr_{0})^{2})^{3/2}}

(25)

In the calculation we have used

r_{0}=0.184a_{0};\;V_{pp\pi}=-2.7eV;\;S_{0}=\frac{\sqrt{3}a_{0}^{2}}{2};\;|\mathbf{K}_{D}|=1.7028\AA^{-1}

(26)

In the presence of a phonon field, the atom position should include the displacement field
{\bf u}^{l}({\bf R}^{l}_{\alpha})
as

{\bf R}_{\alpha}^{l}={\bf R}^{l}+{\bf\tau}_{\alpha}^{l}\rightarrow\tilde{\bf R}_{\alpha}^{l}={\bf R}^{l}+{\bf\tau}_{\alpha}^{l}+{\bf u}^{l}({\bf R}^{l}_{\alpha})

(27)

where

{\bf u}^{l}({\bf R}^{l}+\tau_{\alpha}^{l})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\sum_{\bf p}{\bf u}_{\bf p}^{l,\alpha}e^{i{\bf p}\cdot({\bf R}^{l}+\tau_{\alpha}^{l})}

(28)

where the sum over
{\bf p}
extends of the graphene BZ and the
N_{G}
are the total number of atomic unit cells in graphene.
Another important property of the phonon field is its periodic gauge. With the form of inversion Fourier transform

\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{l,\alpha}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{u}^{l}(\mathbf{R}^{l}+\tau_{\alpha}^{l})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot(\mathbf{R}^{l}+\tau_{\alpha}^{l})},

(29)

we can see

\mathbf{u}^{\alpha}_{\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G}}=e^{-i\mathbf{G}\cdot\tau_{\alpha}}\mathbf{u}^{l\alpha}_{\mathbf{q}}

(30)

which is called the ”periodic gauge”.

Here we only introduce our notation for the single layer graphene, and the notation for the Moiré lattice for twisted bilayer graphene (TBG)
will be introduced in Sec.
V
.

II
Electron-phonon interaction in a single layer graphene

On the basis states

\left|\psi_{{\bf p},\alpha}^{l}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\sum_{{\bf R}^{l}}e^{i{\bf p}\cdot({\bf R}^{l}+{\bf\tau}_{\alpha}^{l})}\left|\psi({\bf R}_{\alpha}^{l}+{\bf u}^{l}({\bf R}^{l}+{\bf\tau}_{\alpha}^{l})\right\rangle,

(31)

the matrix Hamiltonian between the momentum
{\bf p},{\bf p}^{\prime}
in layers
a,b=1,2
and orbitals
\alpha,\beta=A,B
is given by

\displaystyle H_{ll^{\prime}}^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}({\bf pp}^{\prime})=\frac{1}{N_{G}}\sum_{{\bf R}^{l},{\bf R}^{l^{\prime}}}t(\tilde{\bf R}_{\alpha}^{l}-\tilde{\bf R}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l^{\prime}})e^{-i({\bf R}_{\alpha}^{l}\cdot{\bf p}-{\bf R}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l^{\prime}}\cdot{\bf p}^{\prime})}

(32)

or

H_{ll^{\prime}}^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}({\bf pp}^{\prime})=\frac{S_{0}}{(2\pi)^{2}N_{G}}\int d^{2}{\bf q}\left(\sum_{{\bf R}^{l},{\bf R}^{l^{\prime}}}e^{-i({\bf R}_{\alpha}^{l}\cdot({\bf p-q})-{\bf R}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l^{\prime}}\cdot({\bf p}^{\prime}-{\bf q}))}e^{i{\bf q}\cdot({\bf u}({\bf R}_{\alpha}^{l})-{\bf u}({\bf R}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l^{\prime}}))}\right)t({\bf q},z_{ll^{\prime}})

(33)

where
z_{ll^{\prime}}
is the height difference between the layers
l
and
l^{\prime}
. In this section, we first discuss the electron-phonon (e-ph) interaction in the same layer
l=l^{\prime}
, and may drop the layer index
l
in some expressions for
simplicity.

II.1
Zero Displacement

We have

\frac{1}{N_{G}}\sum_{{\bf R}^{l}}e^{-i{\bf R}^{l}\cdot({\bf p-q})}=\sum_{{\bf G}^{l}}\delta_{{\bf p-q},{\bf G}^{l}}

(34)

where
{\bf G}^{l}
is a reciprocal lattice vector in the layer
l
.

For the layer
l=l^{\prime}
, we have (since
z_{ll}=0
)

\displaystyle H_{0,ll}^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}({\bf pp}^{\prime})=\frac{S_{0}N_{G}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int d^{2}{\bf q}\sum_{{\bf G}_{a}^{l},{\bf G}_{b}^{l}}\left(\delta_{{\bf p-q},-{\bf G}_{a}^{l}}\delta_{{\bf p^{\prime}-q},-{\bf G}_{b}^{l}}e^{-i{\bf G}_{b}^{l}\cdot{\bf\tau}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l}+i{\bf G}_{a}^{l}\cdot{\bf\tau}_{\alpha}^{l}}\right)t({\bf q},0)

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\bf G}_{a}^{l},{\bf G}_{b}^{l}}\delta_{{\bf p}+{\bf G}_{a}^{l},{\bf p}^{\prime}+{\bf G}_{b}^{l}}e^{-i{\bf G}_{b}^{l}\cdot{\bf\tau}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l}+i{\bf G}_{a}^{l}\cdot{\bf\tau}_{\alpha}^{l}}t({\bf p+G}_{a}^{l},0)

(35)

where
{\bf G}_{a}^{l}
,
{\bf G}_{b}^{l}
are two sets of reciprocal lattice vectors in the BZ of the layer
l
.
The summation in Eq. (
35
) respects the symmetries of the problem. When we restrict
{\bf p,p}^{\prime}
to the same (first) BZ of graphene,
we have
\delta_{{\bf p+G}_{a}^{l},{\bf p^{\prime}+G}_{b}^{l}}=\delta_{\bf p,p^{\prime}}\delta_{{\bf G}_{a}^{l},{\bf G}_{b}^{l}}
and hence

H_{0ll}^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}({\bf pp}^{\prime})=\delta_{\bf p,p^{\prime}}\sum_{{\bf G}_{a}^{l}}e^{-i{\bf G}_{a}^{l}\cdot({\bf\tau}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l}-{\bf\tau}_{\alpha}^{l})}t({\bf p+G}_{a}^{l},0)

(36)

We then have the tight-binding Hamiltonian for the single layer graphene

t_{\bf p}^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}=\sum_{{\bf G}_{a}^{l}}e^{-i{\bf G}_{a}^{l}\cdot({\bf\tau}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l}-{\bf\tau}_{\alpha}^{l})}t({\bf p+G}_{a}^{l},0),

(37)

which has a node at the
{\bf K}_{D}
point.

II.2
Finite Out of-Plane Displacement

For the out-of-plane displacement, we can use hopping
t({\bf q},z=u^{z}({\bf R}_{\alpha}^{l})-u^{z}({\bf R}_{\alpha}^{\prime l}))
.
With the small displacement approximation, we can linearize the expression in small
u
, and sum over graphene lattice,
leading to the intra-layer Hamiltonian

\displaystyle H_{\perp ll}^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}({\bf pp}^{\prime})=\frac{S_{0}}{(2\pi)^{2}N_{G}}\int d^{2}{\bf q}\left(\sum_{{\bf R}_{1}^{l},{\bf R}_{2}^{l}}e^{-i({\bf R}_{1\alpha}^{l}({\bf p-q})-{\bf R}_{2\alpha^{\prime}}^{l}({\bf p^{\prime}-q}))}(u^{z}({\bf R}_{\alpha}^{l})-u^{z}({\bf R}_{\alpha}^{\prime l}))\partial_{z}t({\bf q},z)|_{z=0}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{1/2}_{G}}\sum_{{\bf p}_{1}\in BZ}\sum_{{\bf G}_{a}^{l},{\bf G}_{b}^{l}}e^{i(\tau_{\alpha}^{l}\cdot{\bf G}_{a}^{l}-\tau_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l}\cdot{\bf G}_{b}^{l})}\delta_{{\bf p^{\prime}+p_{1}+G}_{b}^{l},{\bf p+G}_{a}^{l}}

\displaystyle\left(u_{{\bf p}_{1}}^{zl,\alpha}\partial_{z}t({\bf G}_{a}^{l}+{\bf p-p_{1}},z)|_{z=0}-u_{\bf p_{1}}^{zl,\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{z}t({\bf G}_{b}^{l}+{\bf p^{\prime}+p_{1}},z)|_{z=0}\right).

(38)

One can show that

\frac{\partial t({\bf r},t)}{\partial z}|_{z=0}=0

(39)

and hence there is no out-of-plane phonon coupling to the graphene electrons (this will also be derived by symmetry), and thus

H_{\perp ll}^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}({\bf pp}^{\prime})=0

(40)

Thus, we only focus on in-plane phonon coupling below.

II.3
Finite In-Plane Displacement

For the in-plane displacement, we can use the Fourier transform form of the hopping term,
t({\bf q},z=0)
.
With the small displacement approximation, we can linearize
{\bf u}
, and sum over graphene lattice, leading to the intra-layer Hamiltonian

\displaystyle H_{\parallel ll}^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}({\bf pp}^{\prime})=\frac{S_{0}}{(2\pi)^{2}N_{G}}\int d^{2}{\bf q}\left(\sum_{{\bf R}_{1}^{l},{\bf R}_{2}^{l}}e^{-i({\bf R}_{1\alpha}^{l}\cdot({\bf p-q})-{\bf R}_{2\alpha^{\prime}}^{l}({\bf p^{\prime}-q}))}i{\bf q}\cdot({\bf u}({\bf R}_{1\alpha}^{l})-{\bf u}({\bf R}_{2\alpha^{\prime}}^{l}))\right)t({\bf q},0)

\displaystyle=\frac{S_{0}N^{1/2}_{G}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int d^{2}{\bf q}\times t({\bf q},0)i{\bf q}\cdot

\displaystyle\sum_{{\bf p}_{1}\in BZ}\sum_{{\bf G}_{a}^{l},{\bf G}_{b}^{l}}\left(\delta_{{\bf-p_{1}+p-q},-{\bf G}_{a}^{l}}\delta_{{\bf p^{\prime}-q},-{\bf G}_{b}^{l}}e^{i({\bf\tau}_{\alpha}^{l}\cdot{\bf G}_{a}^{l}-{\bf\tau}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l}\cdot{\bf G}_{b}^{l}))}{\bf u}_{{\bf p}_{1}}^{l,\alpha}-\delta_{{\bf p_{1}+p^{\prime}-q},-{\bf G}_{b}^{l}}\delta_{{\bf p-q},-{\bf G}_{a}^{l}}e^{i({\bf\tau}_{\alpha}^{l}\cdot{\bf G}_{a}^{l}-{\bf\tau}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l}\cdot{\bf G}_{b}^{l})}{\bf u}_{{\bf p}_{1}}^{l,\alpha^{\prime}}\right)

(41)

where
{\bf G}_{a}^{l},{\bf G}_{b}^{l}
are two sets of reciprocal lattice vectors in the the BZ of layer

l
. Transforming the
\delta
functions

\displaystyle\delta_{{\bf-p_{1}+p-q},-{\bf G}_{a}^{l}}\delta_{{\bf p^{\prime}-q},-{\bf G}_{b}^{l}}=\delta_{{\bf q},\frac{{\bf G}_{a}^{l}+{\bf G}_{b}^{l}+{\bf p+p^{\prime}-p}_{1}}{2}}\delta_{{\bf p^{\prime}+p}_{1}+{\bf G}_{b}^{l},{\bf p+G}_{a}^{l}}

\displaystyle\delta_{{\bf p_{1}+p^{\prime}-q},-{\bf G}_{b}^{l}}\delta_{{\bf p-q},-{\bf G}_{a}^{l}}=\delta_{{\bf q},\frac{{\bf G}_{a}^{l}+{\bf G}_{b}^{l}+{\bf p+p^{\prime}+p_{1}}}{2}}\delta_{{\bf p^{\prime}+p_{1}+G}_{b}^{l},{\bf p+G}_{a}^{l}},

(42)

we find the matrix elements of the intra-layer Hamiltonian,

\displaystyle H_{\parallel l}^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}({\bf pp}^{\prime})=\frac{1}{N^{1/2}_{G}}\sum_{{\bf p}_{1}\in BZ}\sum_{{\bf G}_{a}^{l},{\bf G}_{b}^{l}}e^{i({\bf\tau}_{\alpha}^{l}\cdot{\bf G}_{a}^{l}-{\bf\tau}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l}\cdot{\bf G}_{b}^{l})}\delta_{{\bf p^{\prime}+p_{1}+G}_{b}^{l},{\bf p+G}_{a}^{l}}

\displaystyle\left(i\frac{{\bf G}_{a}^{l}+{\bf G}_{b}^{l}+{\bf p+p^{\prime}-p_{1}}}{2}\cdot{\bf u}_{{\bf p}_{1}}^{l,\alpha}t(\frac{{\bf G}_{a}^{l}+{\bf G}_{b}^{l}+{\bf p+p^{\prime}-p_{1}}}{2},0)-i\frac{{\bf G}_{a}^{l}+{\bf G}_{b}^{l}+{\bf p+p^{\prime}+p_{1}}}{2}\cdot{\bf u}_{{\bf p}_{1}}^{l,\alpha^{\prime}}t(\frac{{\bf G}_{a}^{l}+{\bf G}_{b}^{l}+{\bf p+p^{\prime}+p_{1}}}{2},0)\right)=

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{1/2}_{G}}\sum_{{\bf p}_{1}\in BZ}\sum_{{\bf G}_{a}^{l},{\bf G}_{b}^{l}}e^{i({\bf\tau}_{\alpha}^{l}\cdot{\bf G}_{a}^{l}-{\bf\tau}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l}\cdot{\bf G}_{b}^{l})}\delta_{{\bf p^{\prime}+p_{1}+G}_{b}^{l},{\bf p+G}_{a}^{l}}

\displaystyle\left(i({\bf G}_{a}^{l}+{\bf p-p_{1}})\cdot{\bf u}_{{\bf p}_{1}}^{l,\alpha}t({\bf G}_{a}^{l}+{\bf p-p_{1}},0)-i({\bf G}_{b}^{l}+{\bf p^{\prime}+p_{1}})\cdot{\bf u}_{{\bf p}_{1}}^{l,\alpha^{\prime}}t({\bf G}_{b}^{l}+{\bf p^{\prime}+p_{1}},0)\right)

(43)

It should be noted that
{\bf p}_{1}
summation in the above expression is over one BZ. For convenience, we can choose it within the first BZ. From the
\delta
-function, we have
{\bf p_{1}}={\bf p+G}_{a}^{l}-{\bf p^{\prime}-G}_{b}^{l}
, so if we keep the summation over
{\bf G}_{b}^{l}
, only one
{\bf G}_{a}^{l}
is allowed to make

{\bf p}_{1}
in the first BZ.
Actually the choice of
{\bf G}_{a}^{l}
will not influence the results. To see that, let’s assume the choice of
{\bf G}_{a0}^{l}
can make
{\bf p}_{10}={\bf p+G}_{a0}^{l}-{\bf p^{\prime}-G}_{b}^{l}
within the first BZ, and now we consider another choice
{\bf G}_{a}^{l}={\bf G}_{a0}^{l}+\Delta{\bf G}^{l}
, so
{\bf p_{1}=p+G}_{a}^{l}-{\bf p^{\prime}-G}_{b}^{l}={\bf p}_{10}+\Delta{\bf G}^{l}
is outside the first BZ. For the first term in Eq. (
43
), we have

\displaystyle e^{i(\tau_{\alpha}^{l}\cdot{\bf G}_{a}^{l}-\tau_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l}\cdot{\bf G}_{b}^{l})}i({\bf G}_{a}^{l}+{\bf p-p_{1}})\cdot{\bf u}_{{\bf p}_{1}}^{l,\alpha}t({\bf G}_{a}^{l}+{\bf p-p_{1}},0)

\displaystyle=e^{i(\tau_{\alpha}^{l}\cdot({\bf G}_{a0}^{l}+\Delta{\bf G}^{l})-\tau_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l}\cdot{\bf G}_{b}^{l})}i({\bf G}_{a0}^{l}+{\bf p-p_{10}})\cdot{\bf u}_{{\bf p}_{10}+\Delta{\bf G}^{l}}^{l,\alpha}t({\bf G}_{a0}^{l}+{\bf p-p_{10}},0)

\displaystyle=e^{i(\tau_{\alpha}^{l}\cdot{\bf G}_{a0}^{l}-\tau_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l}\cdot{\bf G}_{b}^{l})}i({\bf G}_{a0}^{l}+{\bf p-p}_{10})\cdot{\bf u}_{{\bf p}_{10}}^{l,\alpha}t({\bf G}_{a0}^{l}+{\bf p-p}_{10},0)

(44)

where we have used the periodic gauge
\mathbf{u}^{l,\alpha}_{\mathbf{p}_{10}+\Delta\mathbf{G}^{l}}=e^{-i\tau_{\alpha}^{l}\cdot\Delta{\bf G}^{l}}{\bf u}_{\mathbf{p}_{10}}^{l,\alpha}
.
From the above expression, one can see that the first term in Eq. (
43
) remains the same if one changes from the summation over
{\bf p}_{10}
to that over
{\bf p}_{1}
. This means one just needs to pick one
{\bf G}_{a}^{l}
(e.g. one possible choice
{\bf G}_{a}^{l}={\bf G}_{b}^{l}
), while which choice will not influence the results. Similar analysis can also be applied to the second term in Eq. (
43
).

Both in graphene and TBG, the electronic states with the energies around the Fermi energy is only close to the
\eta{\bf K}_{D}
point where
\eta=\pm
is the valley. Thus, one needs to expand the electron-phonon Hamiltonian around
\eta{\bf K}_{D}
. The electron momenta
\mathbf{p}
and
\mathbf{p}^{\prime}
in Eq. (
II.3
) can be expanded around the same valley (
\eta{\bf K}_{D}
for a fixed
\eta
) or opposite valleys (
\eta{\bf K}_{D}
and
-\eta{\bf K}_{D}
), corresponding to intra-valley and inter-valley e-ph interaction, respectively. In this work, we focus on inter-valley e-ph interaction.

II.4
Inter-valley Electron-Phonon Interaction with In-plane Phonons

In this section, we choose
\mathbf{p}=\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}
,
\mathbf{p}^{\prime}=\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}
where
k,k^{\prime}\ll|\mathbf{K}_{D}|
,
and correspondingly, we also need to decompose the phonon momentum into a large value (the difference between the two valleys)
and a small value around that,

\mathbf{p}_{1}=\mathbf{p}+\mathbf{P}_{1},;\;\;p\ll P_{1}

(45)

where
\mathbf{P}_{1}
determines the major part of phonon momentum. The momentum
\delta
-function in
H_{\parallel ll}^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{p}\mathbf{p}^{\prime})
becomes

\delta_{\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{G}_{a}^{l},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{p}_{1}+\mathbf{G}_{b}^{l}}=\delta_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{p}}\delta_{2\eta K_{D}+\mathbf{G}_{a}^{l}-\mathbf{G}_{b}^{l},\mathbf{P}_{1}}.

(46)

For the momentum
\mathbf{p}
in the first BZ, there are
3
possible values for
\mathbf{P}_{1}
momenta

\mathbf{P}_{1}=-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D};-\eta(\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{G}_{1});-\eta(\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{G}_{2}).

(47)

However, as discussed in Sec.
II.3
, the summation over
\mathbf{p}_{1}
in the Hamiltonian (
43
) is within one BZ
and the choice of different BZs will not influence the result due to the periodic gauge of the phonon modes.
Thus, we make the choice of
\mathbf{P}_{1}=-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}
below, which corresponds to the K-phonon, and the Hamiltonian reads

\displaystyle H_{\parallel l}^{\eta\alpha,-\eta\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime})=\frac{i}{N^{1/2}_{G}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}_{a}^{l}}e^{i(\tau_{\alpha}^{l}-\tau_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l})\cdot\mathbf{G}_{a}^{l}}e^{i\tau_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l}\cdot(\mathbf{P}_{1}-2\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})}

\displaystyle\left((\mathbf{G}_{a}^{l}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{P}_{1})\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{P}_{1}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}^{l,\alpha}t(\mathbf{G}_{a}^{l}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{P}_{1},0)-(\mathbf{G}_{a}^{l}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k})\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{P}_{1}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}^{l,\alpha^{\prime}}t(\mathbf{G}_{a}^{l}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k},0)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{N^{1/2}_{G}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}_{a}^{l}}e^{i(\tau_{\alpha}^{l}-\tau_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{l})\cdot\mathbf{G}_{a}^{l}}\left((\mathbf{G}_{a}^{l}+2\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{u}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}^{l,\alpha}t(\mathbf{G}_{a}^{l}+2\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime},0)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-(\mathbf{G}_{a}^{l}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k})\cdot\mathbf{u}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}^{l,\alpha^{\prime}}t(\mathbf{G}_{a}^{l}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k},0)\right),

(48)

where we have used

\displaystyle 3\mathbf{K}_{D}=\mathbf{G}_{1}+\mathbf{G}_{2}

\displaystyle e^{i\tau_{A}\cdot(\mathbf{G}_{1}+\mathbf{G}_{2})}=e^{i\tau_{B}\cdot(\mathbf{G}_{1}+\mathbf{G}_{2})}=1.

(49)

We next parameterize the above expression and define

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}=\sum_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}t(\mathbf{G}_{a}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},0),

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}=\sum_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}(\widehat{\mathbf{G}_{a}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}})_{x}(\mathbf{G}_{a}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})_{x}\frac{\partial{t(\mathbf{q},0)}}{\partial q}|_{q=|\mathbf{K}_{D}+\eta\mathbf{G}_{a}|},

(50)

\gamma_{2}=\sum_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}e^{i(\tau_{A}-\tau_{B})\cdot\mathbf{G}_{a}}(\widehat{\mathbf{G}_{a}+\mathbf{K}_{D}})_{x}(\mathbf{G}_{a}+\mathbf{K}_{D})_{x}\frac{\partial{t(\mathbf{q},0)}}{\partial q}|_{q=|\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{G}_{a}|}

(51)

and

\gamma_{3}=2i\sum_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}e^{i(\tau_{A}-\tau_{B})\cdot\mathbf{G}_{a}}(\mathbf{G}_{a}+\mathbf{K}_{D})_{y}t(\mathbf{G}_{a}+\mathbf{K}_{D},0).

(52)

We also define the acoustic and optical basis for the displacement field as

\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{p}}^{l,\alpha}=\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{p}}^{l,ac}+(-1)^{\alpha}\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{p}}^{l,op}

(53)

where we define
(-1)^{\alpha=A(B)}=-1(+1)
.

With the above definitions, we can simplify the inter-valley e-ph Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle H_{\parallel l}^{\eta,-\eta}(\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime})=-\frac{i}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}(\gamma_{0}+\gamma_{1})(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\cdot(\mathbf{u}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}^{l,ac}\sigma_{0}-\mathbf{u}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}^{l,op}\sigma_{z})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\gamma_{3}\sigma_{x}(-u^{l,op}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},y}-i\eta u^{l,ac}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},x})

\displaystyle+\frac{i}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\gamma_{2}[(-(k_{x}-k^{\prime}_{x})\sigma_{x}+\eta(k_{y}+k^{\prime}_{y})\sigma_{y})u^{l,ac}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},x}+((k_{y}-k^{\prime}_{y})\sigma_{x}+\eta(k_{x}+k^{\prime}_{x})\sigma_{y})u^{l,ac}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},y}]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\gamma_{2}[((k_{x}+k^{\prime}_{x})\sigma_{y}+\eta(k_{y}-k_{y}^{\prime})\sigma_{x})u^{l,op}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},x}+(-(k_{y}+k^{\prime}_{y})\sigma_{y}+\eta(k_{x}-k_{x}^{\prime})\sigma_{x})u^{l,op}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},y}],

(54)

where the Pauli matrix
\sigma
is for the sublattice basis (
(\sigma_{i})_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}
with
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}=A,B
).

In addition, we also define

v_{F}=\left|\sum_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}e^{i\mathbf{G}_{a}\cdot(\tau_{A}-\tau_{B})}\widehat{(\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{G}_{a})}_{x}\frac{\partial{t(\mathbf{q},0)}}{\partial q}|_{q=|\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{G}_{a}|}\right|

(55)

for the Fermi velocity of graphene.

All these parameters can be numerically evaluated and their values are listed in Table (
1
) for both the
3

\mathbf{G}_{a}
and for 2000 (infinite)
\mathbf{G}_{a}
’s.
One can see a large difference for the obtained values for all the parameters when keeping only
3

\mathbf{G}_{a}
, as compared to keeping 2000 (infinite)
\mathbf{G}_{a}
’s.
For example, when keeping of order 2000
G_{a}
’s, we find
\gamma_{1}=63.506
and
\gamma_{0}=-61.4306
, which almost cancel each other, and hence

\gamma=\gamma_{0}+\gamma_{1}=2.07565
. For the first
3

\mathbf{G}_{a}
’s approximation,
\gamma_{0}=-22.7898
and
\gamma_{1}=12.7393
, and hence
\gamma=-10.0505
,
which is larger and of opposite sign as compared to keeping all
\mathbf{G}_{a}
.
Here
\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
is in the unit of eV while
\gamma_{3}
is in the unit of
eV/\AA
.
