II.4
). Here
\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
is in the unit of eV while
\gamma_{3}
is in the unit of
eV/\AA
.

In the above Hamiltonian (
II.4
), the term in the second line (
\gamma_{3}
term) is zeroth order in
\mathbf{k}
while all the other terms linearly depend on
\mathbf{k}
.
Thus, we below only keep the zeroth-order term for e-ph interaction for K-phonons.

II.4.1
Physical Meaning of the phonon Modes at
\mathbf{K}_{D}

We now focus on the leading term of the inter-valley electron-phonon Hamiltonian

\displaystyle H=\frac{\gamma_{3}}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\eta,\alpha\beta}(-u^{l,op}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},y}-i\eta u^{l,ac}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},x})c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha}(\sigma_{x})_{\alpha\beta}c_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\beta}

(56)

where the Hamiltonian is hermitian once the summation over
\eta
is performed. We take the Fourier transform form of the phonon field

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{\alpha}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}e^{i\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}\cdot(\mathbf{R}+\tau_{\alpha})}e^{-i(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\cdot(\mathbf{R}+\tau_{\alpha})}\mathbf{u}_{\alpha}(\mathbf{R}+\tau_{\alpha}).

(57)

We can see that the term
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}\cdot\mathbf{R}
is not periodic in
\mathbf{R}
, and this problem can be fixed by enlarging the unit cell.
By choosing
\mathbf{R}=n\mathbf{a}_{1}+m\mathbf{a}_{2}
, we have

\mathbf{K}_{D}\cdot\mathbf{R}=-\frac{2\pi}{3}(m+n).

(58)

By choosing

m+n=3n_{1}+s\;\;\;

(59)

with
s=0,1,2
and

\sum_{\mathbf{R}}=\sum_{m,n}=\sum_{n_{1},m,s},

(60)

We have

\mathbf{R}^{\prime}=3n_{1}\mathbf{a}_{1}+m(\mathbf{a}_{2}-\mathbf{a}_{1}),\;\;\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{R}^{\prime}+s\mathbf{a}_{1},

(61)

so

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{\alpha}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\sum_{s,\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}e^{i\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}\cdot(s\mathbf{a}_{1}+\tau_{\alpha})}e^{-i(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\cdot(\mathbf{R}^{\prime}+s\mathbf{a}_{1}+\tau_{\alpha})}\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime}+s\mathbf{a}_{1}+\tau_{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\sum_{s}e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}\eta(1-s)}\mathbf{u}_{\alpha s}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime})

(62)

where we have defined the
\mathbf{u}
modes in the larger unit cell as

\mathbf{u}_{\alpha s}(\mathbf{q})=\sqrt{\frac{3}{N_{G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot(\mathbf{R}^{\prime}+s\mathbf{a}_{1}+\tau_{\alpha})}\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime}+s\mathbf{a}_{1}+\tau_{\alpha}).

(63)

For our choices of vectors, we note that

\tau_{A}\cdot\mathbf{K}_{D}=\tau_{B}\cdot\mathbf{K}_{D}=\frac{2\pi}{3}

(64)

The fact that they have the same value allows us to separate them once again into optical and acoustic combinations

u_{\alpha s}=u_{ac,s}+(-1)^{\alpha}u_{op,s}

(65)

and to have

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{ac/op}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\sum_{s}e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}\eta(1-s)}\mathbf{u}_{ac/op,s}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime})

(66)

And the Hamiltonian becomes

\displaystyle H=\frac{\gamma_{3}}{\sqrt{3N_{G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\eta,\alpha\beta}\sum_{s=1,2,3}e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}\eta(1-s)}(-u_{op,s,y}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime})-i\eta u_{ac,s,x}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}))c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha}(\sigma_{x})_{\alpha\beta}c_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\beta}

(67)

We may separate the valley index from electron momentum and introduce the field operator

\hat{\Psi}_{\mathbf{k},\alpha}=(c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha},c_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha})^{T}

(68)

and the Pauli matrix
\tau
for the valley index. The Hamiltonian is then written as

\displaystyle H=\frac{\gamma_{3}}{2\sqrt{3N_{G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\hat{\Psi}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}}\sigma_{x}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\sum_{\alpha,s}(\mathbf{d}^{r}_{\alpha s}\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\alpha,s}-i\mathbf{d}^{i}_{\alpha s}\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\alpha,s})\\
\sum_{\alpha,s}(\mathbf{d}^{r}_{\alpha s}\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\alpha s}+i\mathbf{d}^{i}_{\alpha s}\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\alpha,s})&0\end{array}\right)\hat{\Psi}_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{3}}{2\sqrt{3N_{G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\alpha,s}\hat{\Psi}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}}\sigma_{x}\left((\mathbf{d}^{r}_{\alpha s}\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\alpha,s})\tau_{x}+(\mathbf{d}^{i}_{\alpha s}\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\alpha,s})\tau_{y}\right)\hat{\Psi}_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}},

(72)

where we have used

\displaystyle\sum_{s}e^{i\eta\theta_{s}}(-u_{op,s,y}-i\eta u_{ac,s,x}))=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s}e^{i\eta\theta_{s}}(-(u_{B,s,y}-u_{A,s,y})-i\eta(u_{A,s,x}+u_{B,s,x}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\alpha,s}(\mathbf{d}^{r}_{\alpha s}\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\alpha s}+i\eta\mathbf{d}^{i}_{\alpha s}\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\alpha,s})

(73)

with

\displaystyle\mathbf{d}^{r}_{A,s}=\cos{\theta_{s}}\hat{e}_{y}+\sin{\theta_{s}}\hat{e}_{x},\;\;\mathbf{d}^{r}_{B,s}=-\cos{\theta_{s}}\hat{e}_{y}+\sin{\theta_{s}}\hat{e}_{x},

\displaystyle\mathbf{d}^{i}_{A,s}=\sin{\theta_{s}}\hat{e}_{y}-\cos{\theta_{s}}\hat{e}_{x},\;\;\mathbf{d}^{i}_{B,s}=-\sin{\theta_{s}}\hat{e}_{y}-\cos{\theta_{s}}\hat{e}_{x},

(74)

and
\theta_{s}=\frac{2\pi}{3}(1-s)
. Here
\mathbf{u}_{\alpha,s}
is a function of
\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
. The above e-ph Hamiltonian
takes the same form as in Ref.
Wu et al. 2018
.

Now let’s focus on the vibration mode at
\mathbf{K}_{D}
(
\mathbf{k}\sim\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\sim 0
), so
\mathbf{u}_{\alpha,s}(0)=\sqrt{3/N_{G}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime}+s\mathbf{a}_{1}+\tau_{\alpha})
.
We can view
\mathbf{d}^{r(i)}_{\alpha s}
as the vector, along which the
(\alpha,s)
-atom vibrates. Explicitly, the
\mathbf{d}^{r}
-vectors are given by

\mathbf{d}^{r}_{A,0}=-\frac{1}{2}\hat{e}_{y}+\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\hat{e}_{x}
,
\mathbf{d}^{r}_{B,0}=\frac{1}{2}\hat{e}_{y}+\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\hat{e}_{x}
,

\mathbf{d}^{r}_{A,1}=\hat{e}_{y}
,
\mathbf{d}^{r}_{B,1}=-\hat{e}_{y}
,
\mathbf{d}^{r}_{A,2}=-\frac{1}{2}\hat{e}_{y}-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\hat{e}_{x}

and
\mathbf{d}^{r}_{B,2}=\frac{1}{2}\hat{e}_{y}-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\hat{e}_{x}
, which can be depicted by the green arrows in Fig.
4
b
and correspond to the
A_{1}
phonon mode. The vibration configuration of each atoms for the
\mathbf{d}^{i}
phonon mode is shown
in Fig.
4
c and corresponds to the
A_{2}
mode (Here
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
corresponds to the irreducible representations of the
C_{3v}
group).

Figure 4:
(a) Enlarged unit cell for phonon modes at
\mathbf{K}_{D}
. (b) and (c) are for the
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
phonon modes at
\mathbf{K}_{D}
.

II.5
Symmetry properties of the Electron-Phonon Interaction

In this section, we will present a construction of the electron-phonon Hamiltonian from the symmetry principle.
We now write all the symmetry operators on the electron and phonon modes (we consider the phonon as a field and expand it around
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}
):

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{3}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha}\hat{C}_{3}^{-1}=e^{i\eta\tau_{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{G}_{1}}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\hat{C}_{3}\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha}

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{y}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha}\hat{M}_{y}^{-1}=\sum_{\beta}(\sigma_{x})_{\beta\alpha}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\hat{M}_{y}\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\beta}

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{x}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha}\hat{M}_{x}^{-1}=\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\hat{M}_{x}\mathbf{k}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha}

\displaystyle\hat{T}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha}\hat{T}^{-1}=\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{-\mathbf{k}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha}

\displaystyle\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G},\alpha}=e^{i\tau_{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{G}}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\alpha}

\displaystyle u_{\alpha i}(\mathbf{p}+\mathbf{G})=e^{-i\tau_{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{G}}u_{\alpha i}(\mathbf{p})

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{3}u_{\alpha i}(\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})\hat{C}_{3}^{-1}=e^{-i\eta\tau_{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{G}_{1}}\sum_{i^{\prime}}u_{\alpha i^{\prime}}(\hat{C}_{3}\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})C_{3i^{\prime}i}

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{y}u_{\alpha i}(\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})\hat{M}_{y}^{-1}=\sum_{i^{\prime}\beta}(\sigma_{x})_{\beta\alpha}u_{\beta i^{\prime}}(\hat{M}_{y}\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})M_{yi^{\prime}i}

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{x}u_{\alpha i}(\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})\hat{M}_{x}^{-1}=\sum_{i^{\prime}}u_{\alpha i^{\prime}}(\hat{M}_{x}\mathbf{k}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})M_{xi^{\prime}i}

\displaystyle\hat{T}u_{\alpha i}(\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})\hat{T}^{-1}=u_{\alpha i}(-\mathbf{k}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})

(75)

In the above, the matrix representations are defined as

\displaystyle C_{3}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-1/2&-\sqrt{3}/2\\
\sqrt{3}/2&-1/2\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle M_{x}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-1&0\\
0&1\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle M_{y}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&0\\
0&-1\end{array}\right).

We now use the above symmetry transformation to construct the inter-valley electron-phonon Hamiltonian.

The generic form of the intra-valley electron-phonon Hamiltonian reads

\displaystyle H_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\eta;\alpha\beta\theta;j}F_{\alpha\beta\theta;j}^{\eta}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha}u_{\theta j}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})c_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\beta}

(85)

where
F_{\alpha\beta\theta;j}^{\eta}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})
is the generic inter-valley electron-phonon interaction parameter.
We consider the case of
F_{\alpha\beta\theta;j}^{\eta}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})
independent of
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
in order to obtain the leading term.
However, we first obtain the symmetry conditions considering the entire functional dependence.
Under the generic
\hat{g}
-symmetry transformations, we have

\displaystyle\hat{g}c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha}\hat{g}^{-1}=\sum_{\eta^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}c^{\dagger}_{\hat{g}\mathbf{k}+\eta^{\prime}\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha^{\prime}}D_{c}(\hat{g})_{\eta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime};\eta\alpha}

\displaystyle\hat{g}u_{\alpha i}(\mathbf{p}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})\hat{g}^{-1}=\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}i^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}}u_{\alpha^{\prime}i^{\prime}}(\hat{g}\mathbf{p}+\eta^{\prime}\mathbf{K}_{D})S_{i^{\prime}i}^{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime};\alpha\eta}(\hat{g})

(86)

where
S_{i^{\prime}\eta^{\prime};i\eta}^{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}
is a combination of the space rotation and sublattice and valley rotation. The equation
\hat{g}H\hat{g}^{-1}=H
gives

\displaystyle\sum_{\eta\alpha\beta\theta i}D_{c}(\hat{g})_{\eta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime};\eta\alpha}S_{i^{\prime},i}^{\theta^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime};\theta-\eta}D^{\star}_{c}(\hat{g})_{-\eta^{\prime}\beta^{\prime};-\eta\beta}F_{\alpha\beta\theta;i}^{\eta}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})=F_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}\theta^{\prime};i^{\prime}}^{\eta^{\prime}}(\hat{g}\mathbf{k},\hat{g}\mathbf{k}^{\prime})

(87)

We now write the explicit form of the matrices and the constraints

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{y}:D_{c\eta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime};\eta\alpha}=\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\eta}\sigma^{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha},\;\;S_{i^{\prime}i}^{\theta^{\prime}\eta^{\prime};\theta\eta}=\sigma^{x}_{\theta^{\prime}\theta}(-1)^{i+1}\delta_{i^{\prime}i}\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\implies F_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}\bar{\theta};i}^{\eta}(\hat{M}_{y}\mathbf{k},\hat{M}_{y}\mathbf{k}^{\prime})=(-1)^{i+1}F_{\alpha\beta\theta;i}^{\eta}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{x}:D_{c\eta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime};\eta\alpha}=\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\bar{\eta}}\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha},\;\;S_{i^{\prime}i}^{\theta^{\prime}\eta^{\prime};\theta\eta}=\delta_{\theta^{\prime}\theta}(-1)^{i}\delta_{i^{\prime}i}\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\bar{\eta}}

\displaystyle\implies F_{\alpha\beta\theta;i}^{\bar{\eta}}(\hat{M}_{x}\mathbf{k},\hat{M}_{x}\mathbf{k}^{\prime})=(-1)^{i}F_{\alpha\beta\theta;i}^{\eta}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{3}:D_{c\eta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime};\eta\alpha}=\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\eta}\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}e^{i\eta\tau_{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{G}_{1}};\;\;S_{i^{\prime}i}^{\theta^{\prime}\eta^{\prime};\theta\eta}=e^{-i\eta\tau_{\theta}\cdot\mathbf{G}_{1}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\eta}\delta_{\theta^{\prime}\theta}C_{3i^{\prime}i}

\displaystyle\implies F_{\alpha\beta\theta;i^{\prime}}^{\eta}(\hat{C}_{3}\mathbf{k},\hat{C}_{3}\mathbf{k}^{\prime})=e^{i\eta(\tau_{\alpha}+\tau_{\beta}+\tau_{\theta})\cdot\mathbf{G}_{1}}\times\sum_{i}F_{\alpha\beta\theta;i}^{\eta}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})C_{3i^{\prime}i}

\displaystyle\hat{T}:D_{c\eta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime};\eta\alpha}=\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\bar{\eta}}\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha},\;\;S_{i^{\prime}i}^{\theta^{\prime}\eta^{\prime};\theta\eta}=\delta_{\theta^{\prime}\theta}\delta_{i^{\prime}i}\delta_{\eta^{\prime},\bar{\eta}}

\displaystyle\implies F_{\alpha\beta\theta;i}^{\bar{\eta}}(-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{k}^{\prime})=F_{\alpha\beta\theta;i}^{\eta\star}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})

(88)

The Hermiticity of the
H_{2}
Hamiltonian requires

\text{Hermiticity:}\;\;\;F_{\alpha\beta\theta;i}^{\eta}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})=F_{\beta\alpha\theta;i}^{\bar{\eta}\star}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{k})

(89)

We are aided by the following identity

e^{i\eta(\tau_{\alpha}+\tau_{\beta}+\tau_{\theta})\cdot\mathbf{G}_{1}}=e^{i(-1)^{\eta}((-1)^{\alpha}+(-1)^{\beta}+(-1)^{\theta})\frac{2\pi}{3}}

(90)

This is a simple system of linear equations to solve, giving rise to the
two
possible solutions. The first solution is given by

\displaystyle F^{\eta}_{\alpha\beta\theta x}=-\frac{\gamma_{3}}{2}\eta\sigma^{x}_{\alpha\beta},\;\;F^{\eta}_{\alpha\beta\theta y}=-\frac{\gamma_{3}}{2}\sigma^{x}_{\alpha\beta}(-1)^{\theta}

(91)

which corresponds to the inter-valley phonon contribution that we have obtained,
-\gamma_{3}(i\eta u^{ac}_{x}+u^{op}_{y})\sigma_{x}
.

The second solution is the missing term whose only nonzero components are

F_{\alpha\alpha\bar{\alpha};x}^{\eta}=i\eta;\;\;F_{\alpha\alpha\bar{\alpha};y}^{\eta}=(-1)^{\alpha}

(92)

which would correspond to a new term in the Hamiltonian

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\eta}c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha}c_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}\alpha}\times(i\eta u_{\bar{\alpha}x}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})+(-1)^{\alpha}u_{\bar{\alpha}y}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}))

from which we can see two crucial properties of the extra terms

•

First, it is diagonal in the electron sublattice index, hence it can only come from second nearest neighbor, or larger.

•

Crucially, the sublattice index of the electron
\alpha
and that of the phonon
\bar{\alpha}
are opposite. It means that the phonon on one sublattice couples to the electron annihilation and creation operators on the other sublattice. Hence it is now clear why this term does not appear in our Hamiltonian or
in any other two-center approximation
. The two center approximation reads

t(R_{\alpha}+u_{\alpha}-R_{\beta}-u_{\beta})c_{R_{\alpha}}^{\dagger}c_{R_{\beta}}

(93)

and hence the phonon sublattice index has to be either that of
c^{\dagger}
or that of
c
. It cannot be neither that of
c^{\dagger}
nor that of
c
. We hence see that the absence of this term is due to the two-center approximation of our Hamiltonian (and of most tight-binding Hamiltonians).

III
Phonons in a Single Layer Graphene

In this section, we will discuss the phonon dynamical matrix and phonon eigen-modes. In real-space, in any dimensions, the dynamica matrix
D
reads

(D_{\mathbf{R}\alpha,\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}})_{ij}

(94)

where
\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}
are the unit cells, the
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
are the atoms, and the
i,j\in x,y,z
are the components in 3D. The presence of stability (the free energy is invariant to adding any constant vector
\vec{a}
to the displacements:
\mathbf{u}_{r\alpha}\rightarrow\mathbf{u}_{r\alpha}+\mathbf{a}
) gives

\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}(D_{\mathbf{R}\alpha,\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}})_{ij}=0

(95)

Which gives, in a spring model,

\sum_{\beta}D_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{q}=0)=0,\forall\alpha;\;\sum_{\alpha}D_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{q}=0)=0,\forall\beta

(96)

Which guarantees the presence of an acoustic phonon mode.

III.1
Dynamical Matrix

The dynamical matrix can be obtained from the potential
V
of the system

(D_{\mathbf{R}\alpha,\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}})_{ii^{\prime}}=\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial u_{\mathbf{R}\alpha i}\partial u_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}i^{\prime}}}

(97)

As such, if there exists a symmetry
\hat{g}
of the system with representation matrix
G_{ij}
, we have the relation

D_{\hat{g}\mathbf{R},\hat{g}\alpha;\hat{g}\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\hat{g}\alpha^{\prime}}=GD_{\mathbf{R}\alpha;\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}G^{-1}

(98)

Next let’s consider the constraint on the dynamical matrix from the symmetry operators,
\hat{C}_{3},\hat{M}_{x}

\hat{M}_{y}
and
\hat{T}
, with their
representation matrices given in the above. Due to translation symmetry,
(D_{\mathbf{R}\alpha,\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}})_{ii^{\prime}}=(D_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}_{\alpha}-\mathbf{R}^{\prime}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))_{ii^{\prime}}

only depends
\mathbf{R}_{\alpha}-\mathbf{R}^{\prime}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
. We only consider the nearest neighbor (nn) and next nearest neighbor (nnn) interaction.
Three nn vectors are labelled as
\delta^{nn}_{AB,\mu=1,2,3}=(a_{0}/2,a_{0}/2\sqrt{3}),(-a_{0}/2,a_{0}/2\sqrt{3}),(0,-a_{0}/\sqrt{3})
that satisfies
\hat{C}_{3}\delta^{nn}_{i}=\delta^{nn}_{i+1}
,
and six nnn vectors are
\delta^{nnn}_{\alpha\alpha,\mu=1,2,3}=-\mathbf{a}_{1}-\mathbf{a}_{2},\mathbf{a}_{1},\mathbf{a}_{2}
and
-\delta^{nnn}_{\alpha\alpha,\mu=1,2,3}
with
\hat{C}_{3}\delta^{nnn}_{i}=\delta^{nnn}_{i+1}
.
In the above expressions, we use the convention
\delta^{nn}_{4}=\delta^{nn}_{1}
and
\delta^{nnn}_{4}=\delta^{nnn}_{1}
.

For the nn interaction that couples the A and B atoms,
\delta^{nn}_{AB,3}=(0,-a_{0}/\sqrt{3})
remains the same under
\hat{M}_{x}
and thus

M_{x}D_{AB}(\delta^{nn}_{3})M_{x}^{-1}=D_{AB}(\delta^{nn}_{3}),

(99)

which requires the diagonal form of
D_{AB}(\delta^{nn}_{3})
, given by

D_{AB}(\delta^{nn}_{3})=-\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}K_{0t}&0\\
0&K_{0r}\end{array}\right).

(100)

Other nn interaction terms can be related to
D_{AB}(\delta^{nn}_{3})
through
C_{3}
rotation,

D_{AB}(\delta^{nn}_{i+1})=C_{3}D_{AB}(\delta^{nn}_{i})C_{3}^{-1}.

(101)

\hat{M}_{y}
reverses the sign of
\delta^{nn}_{AB,\mu}
and interchanges A and B sublattice, and thus

D_{BA}(-\delta^{nn}_{i})=M_{y}D_{AB}(\delta^{nn}_{i})M_{y}^{-1}.

(102)

For the nnn interaction, we first consider the matrix
D_{AA}(\delta^{nnn}_{1})
. Mirror
\hat{M}_{x}
requires

D_{AA}(-\delta^{nnn}_{1})=M_{x}D_{AA}(\delta^{nnn}_{1})M_{x}^{-1}

(103)

.
Furthermore, the symmetric condition for the dynamical matrix gives rise to

D_{AA}(\delta^{nnn}_{1})=(D_{AA}(-\delta^{nnn}_{1}))^{T}.

(104)

Thus, we have the condition

(D_{AA}(\delta^{nnn}_{1}))^{T}=M_{x}D_{AA}(\delta^{nnn}_{1})M_{x}^{-1},

(105)

which leads to the form

D_{AA}(\delta^{nnn}_{1})=-\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}K_{r1}&K_{2}\\
-K_{2}&K_{t1}\end{array}\right).

(106)

D_{AB}(\delta^{nnn}_{2,3})
can be related to
D_{AB}(\delta^{nnn}_{1})
from
C_{3}
rotation,

D_{AB}(\delta^{nnn}_{i+1})=C_{3}D_{AB}(\delta^{nnn}_{i})C_{3}^{-1},

(107)

while
D_{AB}(-\delta^{nnn}_{i})
can be related to
D_{AB}(\delta^{nnn}_{i})
from mirror
\hat{M}_{x}
,

D_{AB}(-\delta^{nnn}_{i})=M_{x}D_{AB}(\delta^{nnn}_{i})M_{x}^{-1}.

(108)

Finally, time reversal
\hat{T}
requires all the five parameters

K_{t0},K_{r0},K_{t1},K_{r1},K_{2}
to be real.
With all the components in the real space, we can transform the dynamical matrix to the momentum space, which is given by

D_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})=\sum_{\mathbf{R}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}\cdot(\mathbf{R}+\tau_{\alpha}-\tau_{\alpha^{\prime}})}D_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}+\tau_{\alpha}-\tau_{\alpha^{\prime}})

(109)

The explicit form of the dynamical matrix under the basis
(u_{Bx},u_{By},u_{Ax},u_{Ay})^{T}
is given by

\displaystyle D(\mathbf{k})=d_{ij}(k_{x},k_{y})\sigma_{i}\otimes\xi_{j}

\displaystyle d_{00}(k_{x},k_{y})=\left(K_{\text{r1}}+K_{\text{t1}}\right)\left(-2\cos\left(\frac{k_{x}}{2}\right)\cos\left(\frac{\sqrt{3}k_{y}}{2}\right)-\cos\left(k_{x}\right)+3\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(3K_{\text{r0}}+3K_{\text{t0}}\right)

\displaystyle d_{01}(k_{x},k_{y})=\sqrt{3}\sin\left(\frac{k_{x}}{2}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\sqrt{3}k_{y}}{2}\right)\left(K_{\text{r1}}-K_{\text{t1}}\right)

\displaystyle d_{03}(k_{x},k_{y})=-\left(K_{\text{r1}}-K_{\text{t1}}\right)\left(\cos\left(k_{x}\right)-\cos\left(\frac{k_{x}}{2}\right)\cos\left(\frac{\sqrt{3}k_{y}}{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle d_{11}(k_{x},k_{y})=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{3}\left(K_{\text{r0}}-K_{\text{t0}}\right)\sin\left(\frac{k_{x}}{2}\right)\sin\left(\frac{k_{y}}{2\sqrt{3}}\right)

\displaystyle d_{21}(k_{x},k_{y})=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{3}\left(K_{\text{r0}}-K_{\text{t0}}\right)\sin\left(\frac{k_{x}}{2}\right)\cos\left(\frac{k_{y}}{2\sqrt{3}}\right)

\displaystyle d_{10}(k_{x},k_{y})=-\frac{1}{2}\left(K_{\text{r0}}+K_{\text{t0}}\right)\left(2\cos\left(\frac{k_{x}}{2}\right)\cos\left(\frac{k_{y}}{2\sqrt{3}}\right)+\cos\left(\frac{k_{y}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle d_{20}(k_{x},k_{y})=\frac{1}{2}\left(K_{\text{r0}}+K_{\text{t0}}\right)\left(2\cos\left(\frac{k_{x}}{2}\right)\sin\left(\frac{k_{y}}{2\sqrt{3}}\right)-\sin\left(\frac{k_{y}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle d_{13}(k_{x},k_{y})=-\frac{1}{2}\left(K_{\text{r0}}-K_{\text{t0}}\right)\left(\cos\left(\frac{k_{x}}{2}\right)\cos\left(\frac{k_{y}}{2\sqrt{3}}\right)-\cos\left(\frac{k_{y}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle d_{23}(k_{x},k_{y})=\frac{1}{2}\left(K_{\text{r0}}-K_{\text{t0}}\right)\sin\left(\frac{k_{y}}{2\sqrt{3}}\right)\left(\cos\left(\frac{k_{x}}{2}\right)+2\cos\left(\frac{k_{y}}{2\sqrt{3}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle d_{32}(k_{x},k_{y})=4K_{2}\sin\left(\frac{k_{x}}{2}\right)\left(\cos\left(\frac{k_{x}}{2}\right)-\cos\left(\frac{\sqrt{3}k_{y}}{2}\right)\right)

(110)

where
\sigma
operates in the
A,B
sub-lattices while
\xi
operates in the
x,y
components.
The eigen-equation of dynamical matrix is given by

D(\mathbf{k})\epsilon_{\mathbf{k}}=M\omega^{2}\epsilon_{\mathbf{k}}.

(111)

Below we focus on the dynamical matrix and the corresponding phonon modes at
\mathbf{K}_{D}
.

III.2
Dynamical Matrix and Phonon Modes at
\mathbf{K}_{D}

Close to the
K_{D}
point, the dynamical matrix is not solvable, so we first consider the eigen-states at the
\mathbf{K}_{D}
point

\displaystyle D(\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})=\frac{3}{2}\left(K_{\text{r0}}+3K_{\text{r1}}+K_{\text{t0}}+3K_{\text{t1}}\right)\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}+

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{4}\left(K_{\text{r0}}-K_{\text{t0}}\right)(\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{3}+\eta\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{1})-

\displaystyle-\eta 3\sqrt{3}K_{2}\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3}

(112)

Let’s define

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}=\frac{3}{2}\left(K_{\text{r0}}+3K_{\text{r1}}+K_{\text{t0}}+3K_{\text{t1}}\right)

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}=\frac{3}{2}\left(K_{\text{r0}}-K_{\text{t0}}\right)

\displaystyle\alpha_{3}=3\sqrt{3}K_{2}

(113)

and the eigenvalues and eigenstates are

\displaystyle E_{E_{-}}=\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{3};\;E_{E_{+}}=\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{3}

(114)

\displaystyle E_{A_{1}}=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}

\displaystyle E_{A_{2}}=\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\eta K_{D}}^{E_{-}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(0,0,\eta i,1)^{T},\;\;\epsilon_{\eta K_{D}}^{E_{+}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(-\eta i,1,0,0)^{T},

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\eta K_{D}}^{A_{1}}=\frac{1}{2}(-\eta i,-1,-\eta i,1)^{T},\;\;\epsilon_{\eta K_{D}}^{A_{2}}=\frac{1}{2}(\eta i,1,-\eta i,1)^{T}

and the eigen-frequencies are related to eigenvalues by

\omega_{r}=\sqrt{E_{r}/M},\;\;r=E_{\pm},A_{2},A_{1}.

(115)

The projected Dynamical matrix into the basis of the doublet
E_{\pm}
is

\displaystyle\left\langle\epsilon_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}}^{E_{\mp}}\right|D(\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})\left|\epsilon_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}}^{E_{\mp}}\right\rangle=

\displaystyle=E_{E_{-}}\sigma_{0}+\frac{\alpha_{2}}{2\sqrt{3}}(k_{y}\sigma_{y}-\eta k_{x}\sigma_{x})

(116)

The symmetry properties of these states are:

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{y}\epsilon_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}}^{A_{1}}=\epsilon_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}}^{A_{1}};\;\;\hat{M}_{y}\epsilon_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}}^{A_{2}}=-\epsilon_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}}^{A_{2}}

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{y}\epsilon_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}}^{E_{-}}=-\epsilon_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}}^{E_{+}},\;\;\;\hat{M}_{y}\epsilon_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}}^{E_{+}}=-\epsilon_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}}^{E_{-}}

(117)

The energies of the phonon bands away from
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}
are

\displaystyle\left\langle\epsilon_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}}^{A_{1,2}}\right|D(\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})\left|\epsilon_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}}^{A_{1,2}}\right\rangle=

\displaystyle=E_{A_{1,2}}+\frac{k^{2}}{8}(-6\sqrt{3}K_{2}-K_{r0}-3K_{r1}+K_{t0}-3K_{t1})

(118)

Hence the effective masses of phonons away from
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}
are the same for the
A_{1,2}
modes.

IV
Electron and Phonon-Band Projected Electron-Phonon Hamiltonian

Since the inter-valley phonons always have a finite frequency, we keep only the zeroth-order terms of momenta and neglect the rest for the inter-valley electron-phonon Hamiltonian

\displaystyle H_{\parallel l}^{\eta,-\eta}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\gamma_{3}\sigma_{x}(-u^{l,op}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},y}-i\eta u^{l,ac}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},x})

(119)

For the electron Dirac Hamiltonian
v_{f}(\eta k_{x}\sigma_{x}+k_{y}\sigma_{y})
, we have the energies
E_{\pm}=\pm v_{f}k
and the corresponding eigen-states

\displaystyle\psi^{\pm}_{\mathbf{k},\eta}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\pm\eta e^{-i\eta\phi}\\
1\end{array}\right],\;\;\;e^{i\phi}=\frac{k_{x}+ik_{y}}{k}

The electron operator can also be expanded on the eigen-states with

c_{\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha}=\sum_{m=1,2}\psi^{m}_{\mathbf{k},\eta\alpha}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},\eta}^{m}.

(123)

For the phonon part, with the eigenstates of the dynamical matrix
\epsilon^{n}_{\mathbf{q}\alpha}
,
we have that the displacements
b_{\mathbf{q}n}
become decomposed in phonon band basis

u_{\mathbf{q}t}=\sum_{n=1,2,3,4}\epsilon_{\mathbf{q}t}^{n}\frac{b_{\mathbf{q}n}+b_{-\mathbf{q}n}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2M\omega_{\mathbf{q}n}}}

(124)

where
t=1,2,3,4
corresponds to
u_{Bx},u_{By},u_{Ax},u_{Ay}
, respectively, and
n
labels the energy eigenstates (from lower energy
to higher energy).

Below we will project the electron-phonon Hamiltonian into the eigen-state basis of both electrons and phonons.

IV.1
Projected Inter-Valley Electron-Phonon Interaction

We next consider the inter-valley e-ph interaction and focus on the
k
-independent terms.

\displaystyle H=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\gamma_{3}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\eta,\alpha\beta}(-u^{l,op}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},y}-i\eta u^{l,ac}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},x})c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha}(\sigma_{x})_{\alpha\beta}c_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\beta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\sqrt{N_{G}}}\gamma_{3}\sum_{m,n=\pm}\sum_{r=1}^{4}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\eta,\alpha\beta}\frac{b_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},r}+b_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}-\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime},r}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2M\omega_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},r}}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},\eta}^{n\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-\eta}^{m}

(125)

\displaystyle((\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},2}-\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},4})+i\eta(\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},1}+\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},3}))\psi_{\mathbf{k},\eta,\alpha}^{n\star}(\sigma_{x})_{\alpha\beta}\psi_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-\eta,\beta}^{m}

Using the phonon eigen-states obtained at
\mathbf{K}_{D}
, we can obtain the approximate form of the couplings for
\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
as

\displaystyle r=E_{-}:\;\;((\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},2}-\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},4})+i\eta(\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},1}+\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},3}))=0

\displaystyle r=E_{+}:\;\;((\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},2}-\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},4})+i\eta(\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},1}+\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},3}))=0

\displaystyle r=A_{2}:\;\;((\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},2}-\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},4})+i\eta(\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},1}+\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},3}))=0

\displaystyle r=A_{1}:\;\;((\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},2}-\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},4})+i\eta(\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},1}+\epsilon^{r}_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},3}))=-2

(126)

Hence the electrons at the
\mathbf{K}_{D}
point only couple with the
A_{1}
phonons, while the couplings to all other 3 phonon modes are proportional to
the momentum
\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
, being zero when
\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
. With

\sum_{\alpha\beta}\psi^{n\star}_{\mathbf{k}\eta\alpha}\sigma^{x}_{\alpha\beta}\psi^{m}_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\eta\beta}=\frac{\eta}{2}(ne^{i\eta\phi}-me^{i\eta\phi^{\prime}})

(127)

We then have the inter-valley electron-phonon coupling

\displaystyle H^{op}_{inter-vall}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\gamma_{3}\sum_{m,n=\pm}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\eta,\alpha\beta}\frac{b_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},A_{1}}+b_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}-\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime},A_{1}}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2M\omega_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},A_{1}}}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},\eta}^{n\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-\eta}^{m}\psi_{\mathbf{k},\eta,\alpha}^{n\star}(\sigma_{x})_{\alpha\beta}\psi_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-\eta,\beta}^{m}

\displaystyle\approx\frac{1}{2\sqrt{N_{G}}}\gamma_{3}\sum_{m,n=\pm}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\eta}\frac{b_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},A_{1}}+b_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}-\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime},A_{1}}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2\sqrt{M(\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3})}}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},\eta}^{n\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-\eta}^{m}\eta(ne^{i\eta\phi}-me^{i\eta\phi^{\prime}})

(128)

where
\phi,\phi^{\prime}
are the angles of the momenta
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
.

•

Intra-band (
m=n
) electrons have the matrix element

m=n:\eta n(e^{i\eta\phi}-e^{i\eta\phi^{\prime}})

(129)

this is zero for
\phi=\phi^{\prime}
, i.e. for
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
being parallel, and is maximum for
\phi=\pi+\phi^{\prime}
, i.e. for
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
being anti-parallel.

•

Inter-band (
m\neq n
) electrons have the matrix element

m=-n:\eta n(e^{i\eta\phi}+e^{i\eta\phi^{\prime}})

(130)

this is zero for
\phi=\pi+\phi^{\prime}
, i.e. for
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
antiparallel and is maximum for
\phi=\phi^{\prime}
, i.e. for
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
parallel.

For further reference, we will also need the electron-phonon interaction on the sublattice basis,
not
projected into the graphene bands -
as we will need to project it into the TBG bands, which are different from the graphene bands.
This form of the inter-valley electron-phonon interaction reads

\displaystyle H_{inter-vall}^{op}\approx\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\gamma_{3}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\eta,\alpha\beta}\frac{b_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},A_{1}}+b_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}-\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime},A_{1}}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2\sqrt{M(\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3})}}}c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\alpha}(\sigma_{x})_{\alpha\beta}c_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},\beta}.

(131)

IV.2
Numerical Calculations of Electron-Phonon Interaction in a Single-Layer Graphene

In this section, we will describe our numerical calculations of energy spectrum for both electrons and phonons in a single layer graphene.
The energy spectrum for electrons and phonons are shown in Fig.
5
(a) and (b), respectively,
which are consistent with the literature
Neto et al. 2009
;
Sahoo and Mishra 2012
;
Thingstad et al. 2020
;
Maultzsch et al. 2004
;
Mohr et al. 2007
.
The parameters for the calculation of energy spectrum of electrons and phonons are summarized in table
2
.
For the phonon dispersion, we label four in-plane phonon modes as the modes 1, 2, 3 and 4, with their irreducible representations (irreps) at high symmetry momenta shown in table
3
.
Here we only consider the
C_{6v}
symmetry group for the in-plane modes of graphene, as the z-direction symmetry will be lost when considering TBG.
The phonon modes 1 and 2 are degenerate have zero energy at
{\bf\Gamma}
, so they describe the acoustic phonon modes with
E_{1}
irrep of
C_{6v}
, while
the phonon modes 3 and 4 are degenerate at
{\bf\Gamma}
and describe two optical phonon modes with
E_{2}
irrep.
These degeneracies are split away from
\Gamma
. At
\mathbf{K}_{1}
(the same as
\mathbf{K}_{D}
), the modes 2 and 3 become degenerate, forming the
E
irrep, while the mode 1 belongs to
A_{2}
irrep and the mode 4 to
A_{1}
irrep, as shown in Fig.
5
(b).

is just
K_{D}
defined
in the main text. (b) Phonon dispersion in single-layer graphene.

\Gamma
and

in single-layer graphene.

For the numerical evaluation of electron-phonon interaction, we introduce the interaction parameter
g
as
(Since we only consider a single-layer graphene here, we have dropped the layer index)

\displaystyle H_{ep}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{p}\mathbf{p}^{\prime},\alpha\beta r}g^{r}_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{p},\alpha}c_{\mathbf{p}^{\prime},\beta}(b_{\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{p}^{\prime},r}+b^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{p}^{\prime}-\mathbf{p},r})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{p}\mathbf{p}^{\prime},nm,r}g^{s}_{nm}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})\gamma^{n\dagger}_{\mathbf{p}}\gamma^{m}_{\mathbf{p}^{\prime}}(b_{\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{p}^{\prime},r}+b^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{p}^{\prime}-\mathbf{p},r})

(132)

where
N_{G}
is the number of unit cells,
\alpha,\beta=A,B
,
n,m
label the eigen-states of electrons and
s
labels the eigen-state of phonon modes.
Here
g^{r}_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})
is in the sub-lattice basis for electrons while
g^{r}_{nm}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})
is in the eigen-state basis.
Explicitly,
g^{r}_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})
is given by

\displaystyle g^{r}_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})=i\sqrt{\frac{\hbar}{2M\omega_{\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{p}^{\prime},r}}}\sum_{G}e^{i\mathbf{G}\cdot(\tau_{\alpha}-\tau_{\beta})}\left(t(\mathbf{p}^{\prime}+\mathbf{G})(\mathbf{p}^{\prime}+\mathbf{G})\cdot{\bf\epsilon}^{r}_{\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{p}^{\prime},\alpha}-t(\mathbf{p}+\mathbf{G})(\mathbf{p}+\mathbf{G})\cdot{\bf\epsilon}^{r}_{\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{p}^{\prime},\beta}\right)

(133)

which can be directly obtained from substituting the phonon eigen-mode expansion Eq. (
124
) into Eq. (
43
).
Here we have recovered the constant
\hbar
for the correct unit.

\displaystyle g^{r}_{nm}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})=\sum_{\alpha\beta}\psi^{n*}_{\alpha,\mathbf{p}}g^{r}_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})\psi^{m}_{\beta,\mathbf{p}^{\prime}}.

(134)

With the expansion of the momentum around
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}
, one can directly relate the function
g
to the parameters
\gamma_{1,2,3}
defined in the
early sections. We can give an estimate of the magnitude of
g
from the value of
\gamma_{3}
as
|\gamma_{3}|\sqrt{\frac{\hbar}{2M\omega_{op,K}}}=|\gamma_{3}|a\sqrt{\frac{\hbar^{2}}{2m_{e}a_{B}^{2}}\frac{1}{\hbar\omega_{op,K}}\frac{m_{e}}{M}\frac{a_{B}^{2}}{a^{2}}}
, where
\frac{\hbar^{2}}{2m_{e}a_{B}^{2}}
is Rydberg
energy,
\hbar\omega_{op,K}
is optical phonon energy at
K
,

m_{e}/M
is the mass ration between electron and carbon atom,

a_{B}
is the Bohr radius and
a=a_{0}/\sqrt{3}
is the NN distance between two carbon atoms in graphene. With
|\gamma_{3}|\sim 17eV/
Å,

a\sim 1.42\AA
and the dimensionless quantity
\sqrt{\frac{\hbar^{2}}{2m_{e}a_{B}^{2}}\frac{1}{\hbar\omega_{op,\Gamma}}\frac{m_{e}}{M}\frac{a_{B}^{2}}{a^{2}}}\sim 0.02
, we find the magnitude of
g
is around
0.56
eV.
To evaluate
g
numerically, it is more efficient to perform the calculation in the real space instead of the momentum space since the hopping function
t

decays exponentially in the real space, but only in power-law in the momentum space. To get a real space expression, we can use the identity

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{G}}e^{i\mathbf{G}\cdot(\tau_{\alpha}-\tau_{\beta})}i(\mathbf{p}+\mathbf{G})t(\mathbf{p}+\mathbf{G})=\sum_{\mathbf{R}}e^{-i\mathbf{p}\cdot(\mathbf{R}+\tau_{\alpha}-\tau_{\beta})}\nabla t(\mathbf{r})|_{\mathbf{r}=\mathbf{R}+\tau_{\alpha}-\tau_{\beta}},

(135)

and obtain

\displaystyle g^{r}_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})=\sqrt{\frac{\hbar}{2M\omega_{r,\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{p}^{\prime}}}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\left(e^{-i\mathbf{p}^{\prime}\cdot\delta_{\alpha\beta}}{\bf\epsilon}^{r}_{\alpha,\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{p}^{\prime}}-e^{-i\mathbf{p}\cdot\delta_{\alpha\beta}}{\bf\epsilon}^{r}_{\beta,\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{p}^{\prime}}\right)\cdot\left.\nabla t(\mathbf{r})\right|_{\mathbf{r}=\delta_{\alpha\beta}}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{\frac{\hbar}{2M\omega_{r,\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{p}^{\prime}}}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\frac{t(\delta_{\alpha\beta})}{r_{0}}\left(e^{-i\mathbf{p}^{\prime}\cdot(\mathbf{R}+\tau_{\alpha}-\tau_{\beta})}{\bf\epsilon}^{r}_{\alpha,\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{p}^{\prime}}-e^{-i\mathbf{p}\cdot(\mathbf{R}+\tau_{\alpha}-\tau_{\beta})}{\bf\epsilon}^{r}_{\beta,\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{p}^{\prime}}\right)\cdot\frac{\delta_{\alpha\beta}}{|\delta_{\alpha\beta}|},

(136)

where
\delta_{\alpha\beta}=\mathbf{R}+\tau_{\alpha}-\tau_{\beta}
. This is the expression for our numerical simulations.

Figure 6:
The electron-phonon interaction parameter
g^{r}_{nm}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})
as a function of
p^{\prime}
for a fixed
\mathbf{p}=\mathbf{K}_{1}+\delta\mathbf{p}
,
where
\delta\mathbf{p}=\frac{1}{a_{0}}(0.001,0)
. (a), (b), (c)
and (d) are for four phonon modes
r=1,2,3,4
, respectively, and
n,m=\pm
. The red hexagon shows the Brillouin zone.
Here the momenta
\mathbf{p}
and
\mathbf{p}^{\prime}
are in the unit of
1/a_{0}
with
a_{0}=2.46
Å.

Figure 7:
Zoom in for the electron-phonon parameter
g(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})
as a function of
\mathbf{p}^{\prime}
with a fixed
\mathbf{p}=\mathbf{K}_{1}+\delta\mathbf{p}
(
\delta\mathbf{p}=\frac{1}{a_{0}}(0.001,0)
)
for the phonon mode
r=4
(inter-valley scattering).
(a) and (b) show the zoom in of
g^{r=4}_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})
(
\alpha,\beta=A,B
) and
g^{r=4}_{nm}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})
(
n,m=\pm
) for
\mathbf{p}^{\prime}
around

\mathbf{K}_{2}^{\prime}
. (c) shows the analytical solutions for the electron-phonon parameters
g^{r=4}_{nm}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})
(
n,m=\pm
) for
\mathbf{p}^{\prime}
around
\mathbf{K}_{2}^{\prime}
for the phonon mode 4 from Eq. (
IV.1
). Here the momenta
\mathbf{p}
and
\mathbf{p}^{\prime}
are in the unit of
1/a_{0}
with
a_{0}=2.46
Å.

Next let’s discuss our numerical results in Figs.
6
and
7
based on the full expression Eq. (
136
). Fig.
6
shows
g^{r}_{nm}(\mathbf{p}=\mathbf{K}_{1}+\delta\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})
(
\mathbf{K}_{1}=\mathbf{K}_{D}
) as a function of
\mathbf{p}^{\prime}
in
the whole Brillouin zone for different phonon modes (a small
\delta\mathbf{p}=\frac{1}{a_{0}}(0.001,0)
is chosen to get rid of the degeneracy of electron states at
the Dirac point).
Since electron Fermi surface is only around
\pm\mathbf{K}_{D}
, we can focus on the corners of the Brillouin zone (BZ), which is shown by the red hexagon in Fig.
6
.
Furthermore, we focus on the inter-valley e-ph interaction, which means
\mathbf{p}^{\prime}
around
\mathbf{K}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{K}_{2}^{\prime},\mathbf{K}_{3}^{\prime}
, as we fix
\mathbf{p}=\mathbf{K}_{1}+\delta\mathbf{p}
and the phonon momentum is
\mathbf{q}=\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{p}^{\prime}
.
We first notice that the e-ph interaction for the phonon modes 1, 2 and 3 are approaching zero when the momentum
\mathbf{p}^{\prime}
is close to
\mathbf{K}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{K}_{2}^{\prime},\mathbf{K}_{3}^{\prime}
, which is consistent with our analytical solution (See Eq.
IV.1
) in Sec.
IV.1
. Thus, only phonon modes 4 couple to electron for the
K
-phonons.
The phonon mode 4 belongs to
E_{2}
irrep of
C_{6v}
at
{\bf\Gamma}
and
A_{1}
irrep of
C_{3v}
at
\mathbf{K}_{D}
, and we show the zoom-in for
\mathbf{p}^{\prime}
around
\mathbf{K}_{2}^{\prime}
(inter-valley scattering) in Fig.
7
(a) and (b). Here (a) and (b) correspond to the parameter
g
in the sublattice basis and the eigen-state basis, respectively, for the electron part.
On the sublattice basis, we find
g
is non-zero for the inter-sublattice components, while its value approaches zero for the intra-valley components, consistent with the analytical results shown in Eq. (
131
). On the eigen-state basis, we find
g
shows a singular behavior at
\mathbf{K}_{2}^{\prime}
for the inter-valley scattering. In Fig.
7
(c), we also show the analytical results of inter-valley scattering from Eq. (
IV.1
), which is consistent with the numerical results in Fig.
7
(b) on the eigen-state basis.

V
Review of Bistritzer-MacDonald model for Twisted Bilayer Graphene

Here we first briefly review the Bistritzer-MacDonald (BM) model
Bistritzer and MacDonald 2011
of magic-angle twisted bilayer graphene (MATBG)
by introducing our notations. Readers may refer to the supplementary materials of Ref.
Song et al. 2019
for more details.

V.1
Basis and Single-particle Hamiltonian

As shown in Fig.
8
(a), the low-energy states around the Dirac points at
\mathbf{K}_{D}
of the two graphene layers form the Moiré Brillouin zone (MBZ) in the valley
\mathbf{K}_{D}
. Similarly, those at the
\mathbf{K}_{D}^{\prime}
momenta of the two layers form another MBZ in the valley
\mathbf{K}_{D}^{\prime}
.
Thus, in the BM model there are two independent sets of basis from two valleys.
We use the index
\eta
(
=+
for
\mathbf{K}_{D}
and
-
for
\mathbf{K}_{D}^{\prime}
) to label the two graphene valleys.
We denote the basis as
c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,\eta,s}
, where
\mathbf{k}
is a momentum in the MBZ,
\mathbf{Q}
takes values in the lattice shown in Fig.
8
(b) (illustrated below),
\alpha=1,2
represents the graphene sublattice,
\eta=\pm
represents the graphene valley, and
s=\uparrow,\downarrow

is the spin index. To construct the MBZ and the
\mathbf{Q}
lattices in Fig.
8
(b), we define

{\bf q}_{1}={\bf K}_{D}^{-}-{\bf K}_{D}^{+}=k_{\theta}(0,-1),{\bf q}_{2}=\hat{C}_{3z}{\bf q}_{1}={\bf K}_{D}^{-}+{\bf G}_{1}^{2}-{\bf K}_{D}^{+}-{\bf G}_{1}^{1}=k_{\theta}(\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},\frac{1}{2}),{\bf q}_{3}=C_{3z}^{2}{\bf q}_{1}={\bf K}_{D}^{-}+{\bf G}_{2}^{2}-{\bf K}_{D}^{+}-{\bf G}_{2}^{1}=k_{\theta}(-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},\frac{1}{2})
, where
k_{\theta}=2|{\bf K}_{D}|\sin\frac{\theta}{2}
and
\theta
is the twist angle between two layers. Here
\mathbf{K}_{D}^{+}
and
\mathbf{K}_{D}^{-}
are the
\mathbf{K}_{D}
momentum of the top and bottom layers
There are two types of
\mathbf{Q}
lattices in Fig.
8
(b): the blue lattice
\mathcal{Q}_{+}=\{\mathbf{q}_{2}+n_{1}\mathbf{b}_{M1}+n_{2}\mathbf{b}_{M2}\ |\ n_{1,2}\in\mathbb{Z}\}
and the red lattice
\mathcal{Q}_{-}=\{-\mathbf{q}_{2}+n_{1}\mathbf{b}_{M1}+n_{2}\mathbf{b}_{M2}\ |\ n_{1,2}\in\mathbb{Z}\}
, where
\mathbf{b}_{M1},\mathbf{b}_{M2}
are Moiré reciprocal lattice basis

\mathbf{b}_{M1}=\mathbf{q}_{2}-\mathbf{q}_{1}=k_{\theta}(\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},\frac{3}{2}),\qquad\mathbf{b}_{M2}=\mathbf{q}_{3}-\mathbf{q}_{1}=k_{\theta}(-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},\frac{3}{2}).

(137)

For
\mathbf{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{+}
, the basis is defined as

(\mathbf{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{+})\qquad c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,\eta,s}^{\dagger}=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{\rm G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathrm{top}}e^{i(\mathbf{K}_{D}^{+}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})\cdot(\mathbf{R}+\tau_{\alpha})}c_{\mathbf{R},\alpha,s}^{\dagger}\qquad&\text{if}\;\eta=+\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{\rm G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{bottom}}e^{i(-\mathbf{K}_{D}^{-}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})\cdot(\mathbf{R}^{\prime}+\tau_{\alpha}^{\prime})}c_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\alpha,s}^{\dagger}\qquad&\text{if}\;\eta=-\end{cases},

(138)

and for
\mathbf{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{-}
, the basis is defined as

(\mathbf{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{-})\qquad c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,\eta,s}^{\dagger}=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{\rm G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{bottom}}e^{i(\mathbf{K}_{D}^{-}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})\cdot(\mathbf{R}^{\prime}+\tau_{\alpha}^{\prime})}c_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\alpha,s}^{\dagger}\qquad&\text{if}\;\eta=+\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{\rm G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathrm{top}}e^{i(-\mathbf{K}_{D}^{+}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})\cdot(\mathbf{R}+\tau_{\alpha})}c_{\mathbf{R},\alpha,s}^{\dagger}\qquad&\text{if}\;\eta=-\end{cases}\ .

(139)

Here
N_{\rm G}
is the number of graphene unit cells in each layer,
\mathbf{R}
and
\mathbf{R}^{\prime}
index graphene lattices in the the top and bottom layers, respectively,
\tau_{\alpha}
and
\tau_{\alpha}^{\prime}
are the sublattice vectors of the two layers, respectively,
\mathbf{K}_{D}^{+}
and
\mathbf{K}_{D}^{-}
are the
\mathbf{K}_{D}
momentum of the top and bottom layers, respectively, and
c_{\mathbf{R},\alpha,s}
(
c_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\alpha,s}
) is the fermion operator with spin
s
at the atom site
\mathbf{R}+\tau_{\alpha}
(
\mathbf{R}^{\prime}+\tau_{\alpha}^{\prime}
).
The
\mathcal{Q}_{+}
(
\mathcal{Q}_{-}
) lattice is defined in such a way that
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}^{+}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q}
(
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}^{-}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q}
) with
\mathbf{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{+}
(
\mathbf{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{-}
) is the Dirac point position
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}^{+}
(
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}^{-}
) when
\mathbf{k}
equals to the high symmetry point
\eta\mathbf{K}_{M}
(
\eta\mathbf{K}_{M}^{\prime}
) of the MBZ, as shown in Fig.
8
(b).

Figure 8:
The moiré BZ and the
\mathbf{Q}
lattice. (a) The blue and red hexagons represent the BZ’s of the top and bottom graphene layers, respectively. The blue and red dots represent the Diract points (
\mathbf{K}_{D}
momentum) from the two layers. The vectors
\mathbf{q}_{1},\mathbf{q}_{2},\mathbf{q}_{3}
connecting the Dirac points of the two layers span the moiré BZ in the valley
\mathbf{K}_{D}
(shaded with light green). Similarly, the Dirac points at the
\mathbf{K}_{D}^{\prime}
momentum form another Moiré BZ in the valley
\mathbf{K}_{D}^{\prime}
(shaded with light yellow). (b) We denote the blue and red
\mathbf{Q}
lattices as
\mathcal{Q}_{+}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{-}
respectively. In the BM model, the states in the valley
\mathbf{K}_{D}
(
\mathbf{K}_{D}^{\prime}
) of the top layer and in the valley
\mathbf{K}_{D}^{\prime}
(
\mathbf{K}_{D}
) of the bottom layer contribute to the lattice
\mathcal{Q}_{+}
(
\mathcal{Q}_{-}
).

The BM model is given by

{\hat{H}}_{\rm 0}=\sum_{\eta s}\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in{\rm MBZ}}\sum_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}h_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha,\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{k})c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,\eta,s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},\eta,s}

(140)

where the single particle Hamiltonian reads

h_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha,\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}^{(+)}(\mathbf{k})=v_{F}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})\cdot\bm{\sigma}\delta_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}[T_{j}]_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\pm\mathbf{q}_{j}},\qquad h_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha,\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}^{(-)}(\mathbf{k})=h_{-\mathbf{Q}\alpha,-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}^{(+)*}(-\mathbf{k}),

(141)

T_{j}=w_{0}\sigma_{0}+w_{1}\sigma_{x}\cos\frac{2\pi(j-1)}{3}+w_{1}\sigma_{y}\sin\frac{2\pi(j-1)}{3}\ .

(142)

Here
w_{0}
and
w_{1}
are the interlayer couplings in the AA-stacking and AB-stacking regions, respectively.
In this work, we adopt the parameters
v_{F}=5.944\mathrm{eV\cdot\mathring{A}}
,
k_{\theta}=0.0312\mathrm{\mathring{A}^{-1}}
,
w_{1}=110\mathrm{meV}
.
The relation
h_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha,\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}^{(-)}(\mathbf{k})=h_{-\mathbf{Q}\alpha,-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}^{(+)*}(-\mathbf{k})
is due to the time-reversal symmetry that transform the two valleys to each other.
The single-particle Hamiltonian (upon to a unitary transformation
Song et al. 2019
) is periodic with respect to the reciprocal lattice vectors
\mathbf{b}_{M1}
,
\mathbf{b}_{M2}
. The MBZ and high symmetry momenta are defined in Fig.
8
(b).
The corresponding real space unit cell and maximal Wyckoff positions are shown in Fig.
8
(c).
The
1a
and
2c
positions correspond to the AA-stacking and AB-stacking regions, respectively.
We denote the real space lattice basis as
\mathbf{a}_{M1}
,
\mathbf{a}_{M2}
, they satisfy
\mathbf{a}_{Mi}\cdot\mathbf{b}_{Mj}=2\pi\delta_{ij}
.
We plot the band structures in the valley
\eta=+
for different twisted angles around the magic angle in the chiral limit
w_{0}=0
in Fig.
9
.

The fact that the band structure is labeled by
\mathbf{k}
in the MBZ implies that
\mathbf{k}
labels the eigenvalues of translation operators of the Moiré lattice.
We hence
define
the translation operator
T_{\mathbf{R}}
as

T_{\mathbf{R}}c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,\eta,s}^{\dagger}T_{\mathbf{R}}^{-1}=e^{-i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{R}}c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,\eta,s}^{\dagger}\ ,

(143)

where
\mathbf{R}=n_{1}\mathbf{a}_{M1}+n_{2}\mathbf{a}_{M2}
, with
n_{1,2}\in\mathbb{Z}
, is a Moiré lattice vector.
One should not confuse
T_{\mathbf{R}}
with the time-reversal symmetry (
\hat{T}
) defined in the early section.
We now verify Eq. (
143
) at the commensurate twist angles,
where
\mathbf{a}_{M1}
and
\mathbf{a}_{M2}
are integer linear combinations of the microscopic graphene lattice vectors
\mathbf{a}_{1},\mathbf{a}_{2}
.

T_{\mathbf{R}}
can be defined as translation acting on the atomic orbitals:
\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\to\mathbf{R}^{\prime}+\mathbf{R}
, leading to

\displaystyle T_{\mathbf{R}}c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,\eta,s}^{\dagger}T_{\mathbf{R}}^{-1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{\rm G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in l}e^{i(\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}^{l}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})\cdot(\mathbf{R}^{\prime}+\tau_{\alpha})}c_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}+\mathbf{R},\alpha,s}^{\dagger}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{\rm G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in l}e^{-i(\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}^{l}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})\cdot\mathbf{R}}e^{i(\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}^{l}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})\cdot(\mathbf{R}^{\prime}+\tau_{\alpha})}c_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\alpha,s}^{\dagger}\ ,

(144)

where
l=\eta
(
-\eta
) for
\mathbf{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{+}
(
\mathcal{Q}_{-}
).
For commensurate angle,
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}^{l}-\mathbf{Q}
is a Moiré reciprocal lattice and hence
e^{-i(\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}^{l}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})\cdot\mathbf{R}}=e^{-i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{R}}
.
Then Eq. (
143
) is justified at commensurate angles.
We emphasize that Eq. (
143
) is also well-defined even at non-commensurate angles.

Figure 9:
The band structure in the valley
\eta=+
of TBG near the magic angle in the chiral limit (
w_{0}=0
). (a), (b) and (c) are for three twist angles
\theta=1.063152^{\circ}
,
\theta=1.06^{\circ}
and
\theta=1.05^{\circ}
. (d), (e) and (f) show the zoom in for the dispersion of the flat bands for these three angles. One can see the profiles of the dispersion look similar for these three angles, while the band width changes
0.02
meV to 2 meV. The other parameters of the BM model are given by
v_{F}=5.944\mathrm{eV}\cdot\mathring{A}
,
|\mathbf{K}|=1.703\mathrm{\mathring{A}^{-1}}
,
w_{1}=110\mathrm{meV}
.

V.2
Symmetries of the BM model

The single-particle Hamiltonian in each valley has the symmetry of the magnetic space group
P6^{\prime}2^{\prime}2
(# 177.151 in the BNS setting),
which is generated by
\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{T}
,
\hat{C}_{3z}
,
\hat{C}_{2x}
and translation symmetries.
Since the two valleys are related by time-reversal symmetry
\hat{T}
, the total system also has the
\hat{C}_{2z}
symmetry
(product of
\hat{C}_{2z}T
and
\hat{T}
). The full crystalline symmetries of the two valleys form the space group
P622
(#177),
which is generated by
\hat{C}_{6z}
,
\hat{C}_{2x}
, and translation symmetries. Thus, these symmetry operators satisfy

[\hat{C}_{3z},H_{0}]=[\hat{C}_{2z},H_{0}]=[\hat{C}_{2x},H_{0}]=[\hat{T},H_{0}]=0,

(145)

and we write the symmetry action on the fermion operators as

\hat{g}c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\eta,\alpha,s}^{\dagger}\hat{g}^{-1}=\sum_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}}c_{\hat{g}\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s}^{\dagger}[D(\hat{g})]_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}\eta\alpha}\ .

(146)

The
D
matrices for the above discrete symmetries are given by

[D(C_{3z})]_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\beta,\mathbf{Q}\eta\alpha}=\delta_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime},C_{3z}\mathbf{Q}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime},\eta}(e^{i\eta\frac{2\pi}{3}\sigma_{z}})_{\beta\alpha}\;\;\;[D(C_{2x})]_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\beta,\mathbf{Q}\eta\alpha}=\delta_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime},C_{2x}\mathbf{Q}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime},\eta}(\sigma_{x})_{\beta\alpha},

(147)

[D(C_{2z})]_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\beta,\mathbf{Q}\eta\alpha}=\delta_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime},-\mathbf{Q}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime},-\eta}(\sigma_{x})_{\beta\alpha},\;\;\;[D(T)]_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\beta,\mathbf{Q}\eta\alpha}=\delta_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime},-\mathbf{Q}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime},-\eta}\delta_{\beta,\alpha,}

(148)

[D(C_{2z}T)]_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\beta,\mathbf{Q}\eta\alpha}=[D(C_{2z})D(T)]_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\beta,\mathbf{Q}\eta\alpha}=\delta_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime},\eta}(\sigma_{x})_{\beta,\alpha}.

(149)

Here and below we will use
\sigma_{x,y,z}
(
\sigma_{0}
) and
\tau_{x,y,z}
(
\tau_{0}
) for the Pauli (identity) matrices in the sub-lattice and valley spaces.
For the sublattice, we choose
\alpha=A,B
to be
1,2
for
\sigma
matrix.

Besides the crystalline symmetries, the BM model also has a unitary particle-hole (PH) symmetry
\hat{P}
that anti-commutes with the single-particle Hamiltonian,

\{\hat{P},H_{0}\}=0
.
\hat{P}
transforms
\mathbf{k}
to
-\mathbf{k}
and the corresponding
D
matrix reads

Pc^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\eta,\alpha,s}P^{-1}=\sum_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\beta}[D(P)]_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\beta,\mathbf{Q}\eta\alpha}c^{\dagger}_{-\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\beta,s},\;\;\;\quad[D(P)]_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\beta,\mathbf{Q}\eta\alpha}=\delta_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime},-\mathbf{Q}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime},\eta}\delta_{\beta,\alpha}\zeta_{\mathbf{Q}}\ ,

(150)

where
\zeta_{\mathbf{Q}}=\pm 1
for
\mathbf{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\pm}
, respectively.
Note that
P
transforms creation operators to creation operators (rather than annihilation operators), and maps sites
\mathbf{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\pm}
into
-\mathbf{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mp}
. The PH transformation
P
satisfies

P^{2}=-1,\qquad[P,C_{3z}]=0,\qquad\{P,C_{2x}\}=0,\qquad\{P,C_{2z}\}=0,\qquad\{P,T\}=0,\qquad[P,C_{2z}T]=0.

(151)

The particle-hole symmetry will be broken if the
\theta
-dependence of the single-layer Hamiltonian or quadratic terms in
\mathbf{k}
of the single-layer Hamiltonian are taken into account.

Besides the above discrete symmetry, the BM model also possesses the continuous U(2)
\times
U(2) spin-charge rotation symmetry,
and the corresponding symmetry operators are given by

\hat{S}^{ab}=\sum_{\mathbf{k}}(\tau^{a})_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}(s^{b})_{ss^{\prime}}c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\eta,\alpha,s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\eta^{\prime},\alpha,s^{\prime}}\ ,\qquad(a=0,z,\quad b=0,x,y,z)\ ,

(152)

where
\tau
and
s
are Pauli matrices for valley and spin degree of freedom.

If, furthermore,
w_{0}=0
, the model acquires an effective chiral symmetry
\hat{C}
that anti-commute with the single-particle Hamiltonian
Tarnopolsky et al. 2019
.
\hat{C}
is referred to as the first chiral symmetry in Refs.
Song et al. 2021
;
Bernevig et al. 2021
.

\hat{C}
leaves
\mathbf{k}
invariant and its
D
matrix reads

[D(\hat{C})]_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}\alpha\eta}=\delta_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}}[\sigma_{z}]_{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}[\tau_{0}]_{\eta^{\prime}\eta}\ .

(153)

In the first-quantized formalism the algebra between
C
and other symmetries are given by

C^{2}=1,\qquad[C,T]=0,\qquad[C,C_{3z}]=0,\qquad\{C,C_{2x}\}=0,\qquad\{C,C_{2z}T\}=0,\qquad[C,P]=0\ .

(154)

VI
Intra-Layer Inter-Valley Electron-phonon Interaction in Twisted Bilayer Graphene

VI.1
Projected Intra-Layer Inter-Valley Electron-Phonon Interaction for the Flat Bands

We define the short-hand notation for the
\mathbf{Q}
lattice in the layer
l=\pm
(top/bottom) and valley
\eta=\pm
as

\mathcal{Q}_{l\eta}=\{l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+n_{1}\mathbf{b}_{M1}+n_{2}\mathbf{b}_{M2}\ |\ n_{1,2}\in\mathbb{Z}\},

(155)

which is consistent with the definition in the previous Sec.
V.1
.
We have the equivalence between the operators
c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,\eta,s}
in Eq. (
138
) in the MBZ
and the ones
c_{\mathbf{k},\alpha,l,s}
in a single layer graphene,

c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta},\alpha,\eta,s}=c_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}^{l}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta},\alpha,l,s},\;\;\;k\in MBZ

(156)

where
\mathbf{K}_{D}^{l}
is the
K
momentum in the layer
l
.

With the electron-phonon Hamiltonian now summed over the two layers
l=\pm
, and with
\mathbf{K}_{D}^{l}
being the graphene Dirac momentum in the layer
l
,
we have

\displaystyle H_{inter-vall}^{op}\approx\frac{\gamma_{3}}{\sqrt{2N_{G}{\sqrt{M(\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3})}}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\eta,\alpha\beta,l,s}(b_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},l,A_{1}}+b_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}-\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime},l,A_{1}}^{\dagger})c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},l,\alpha,s}(\sigma_{x})_{\alpha\beta}c_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},l,\beta,s}

where we have added the layer
l=\pm
index as well as the spin index in the electrons.
In this summation,
\mathbf{k}
is summed over the vicinity of the Dirac node, over a radius much larger than the first MBZ
(but much smaller smaller than
\mathbf{K}_{D}
). We hence break the sum over the momentum into

\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\rightarrow\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in MBZ}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}}

(158)

where the lattice
\mathbf{Q}_{l,\eta}
depends on
\eta,l
for electrons around the
\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}
point of the
l
layer,
and find

\displaystyle H_{inter-vall}^{op}\approx\frac{\gamma_{3}}{\sqrt{2N_{G}{\sqrt{M(\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3})}}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\in MBZ}\sum_{l,\eta}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{-l\eta}}\sum_{\alpha\beta,s}

\displaystyle(b_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}+\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{-l\eta},l,A_{1}}+b_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}-\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{-l\eta},l,A_{1}}^{\dagger})c^{\dagger}_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta},l,\alpha s}(\sigma_{x})_{\alpha\beta}c_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{-l\eta},l,\beta,s}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{3}}{\sqrt{2N_{G}{\sqrt{M(\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3})}}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\in MBZ}\sum_{l,\eta}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{-l\eta}}\sum_{\alpha\beta,s}

\displaystyle(b_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}+\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{-l\eta},l,A_{1}}+b_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}-\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{-l\eta},l,A_{1}}^{\dagger})c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta},\alpha,\eta,s}(\sigma_{x})_{\alpha\beta}c_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{-l\eta},\beta,-\eta,s}

(159)

We next project the above Hamiltonian into the flat bands of the BM model with the expansion

c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta},\alpha,\eta,s}=\sum_{n=1,2}u^{n}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta},\alpha,\eta}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}n\eta s}

(160)

where
u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta},\alpha,\eta}
is the eigenstate of the BM model. This gives

\displaystyle H_{inter-vall}^{op}\approx\frac{\gamma_{3}}{\sqrt{2N_{G}{\sqrt{M(\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3})}}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\in MBZ}\sum_{n,n^{\prime}=1,2}\sum_{s}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta,s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n^{\prime},-\eta,s}

\displaystyle\sum_{l,\eta}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{-l\eta}}(b_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}+\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{-l\eta},l,A_{1}}+b_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}-\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{-l\eta},l,A_{1}}^{\dagger})\sum_{\alpha\beta}u^{n\star}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta},\alpha,\eta}\sigma^{x}_{\alpha\beta}u^{n^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{-l\eta},\beta,-\eta}.

(161)

By defining

\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{-l\eta}=\mathbf{Q}^{\prime\prime}_{-l\eta},

(162)

the Inter-valley Hamiltonian becomes

\displaystyle H_{inter-vall}^{op}\approx\frac{\gamma_{3}}{\sqrt{2N_{G}{\sqrt{M(\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3})}}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\in MBZ}\sum_{n,n^{\prime}=1,2}\sum_{\eta,s}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta,s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n^{\prime},-\eta,s}

\displaystyle\sum_{l}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime\prime}_{-l\eta}}(b_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime\prime}_{-l\eta},l,A_{1}}+b_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}-\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}^{\prime\prime}_{-l\eta},l,A_{1}}^{\dagger})\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}}\sum_{\alpha\beta}u^{n\star}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta},\alpha,\eta}\sigma^{x}_{\alpha\beta}u^{n^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime\prime}_{-l\eta},\beta,-\eta}.

(163)

VI.2
Phonon-Mediated Electron-Electron Interaction for the Flat Bands

We rewrite

\displaystyle H_{inter-vall}^{op}\approx\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\in MBZ}\sum_{n,n^{\prime}=1,2}\sum_{\eta,s}\sum_{l=\pm}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}\times

\displaystyle\times G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta nn^{\prime}l}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta,s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n^{\prime},-\eta,s}(b_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta},l,A_{1}}+b_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}-\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta},l,A_{1}}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta nn^{\prime}l}=\frac{\gamma_{3}}{\sqrt{2M\omega_{A_{1}}}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta}}\sum_{\alpha\beta}u^{n\star}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta},\alpha,\eta}\sigma^{x}_{\alpha\beta}u^{n^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta}-\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta},\beta,-\eta}

\displaystyle\omega_{A1}=\sqrt{(\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3})/M}

(164)

In the above expression,
\mathbf{Q}_{\pm l\eta}=\pm l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}_{M}
and the summation is made over the
\mathbf{G}_{M}
.

With the electron Hamiltonian

H_{el}=\sum_{m,\mathbf{k}\in MBZ,\eta,s}E_{m\mathbf{k}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}m\eta s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}m\eta s}

(165)

and phonon Hamiltonian for the
A_{1}
mode

H_{ph}=\sum_{\mathbf{q},l}\omega_{A1}b_{\mathbf{q}l,A_{1}}^{\dagger}b_{\mathbf{q}l,A_{1}}

(166)

we apply the transformation
S

-H_{inter-vall}^{op}=[H_{el}+H_{ph},S]

(167)

and obtain

\displaystyle S=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\in MBZ}\sum_{n,n^{\prime}=1,2}\sum_{\eta,s}\sum_{l=\pm}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}\times

\displaystyle\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta,s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n^{\prime},-\eta,s}(A_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta nn^{\prime}l}b_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta},l,A_{1}}-A_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{-\eta n^{\prime}nl\star}b_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}-\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta},l,A_{1}}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle A_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta nn^{\prime}l}=-\frac{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta nn^{\prime}l}}{E_{n\mathbf{k}}-E_{n^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}-\omega_{A_{1}}}

(168)

One should note that it is important to keep
-\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}
in
A_{k^{\prime},k,-\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{-\eta n^{\prime}nl\star}
and not rewrite it as
\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}
since

-\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}=l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}-\mathbf{G}_{M}
whereas
\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}=l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}_{M}
. They hence differ by a Moiré reciprocal lattice vector,
but since this vector is what we sum over, it is important to keep the correct form.

We then obtain the phonon-mediated electron-electron interaction (we now expand explicitly the
\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}
and perform the summation over
\mathbf{G}_{M}
)

\displaystyle H_{el-el}=\frac{1}{N_{G}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}_{1}\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{G}_{M},l,s,s_{1}}\frac{G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{\eta nn^{\prime}l}G_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}-\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{-\eta n_{1}n_{1}^{\prime}l}\omega_{A_{1}}}{(E_{n_{1}\mathbf{k}_{1}}-E_{n_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}})^{2}-\omega_{A_{1}}^{2}}\delta_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta,s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1},n_{1},-\eta,s_{1}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},n_{1}^{\prime},\eta,s_{1}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n^{\prime},-\eta,s}

Since the optical phonon frequency
\omega_{A1}
is much larger than the bandwidth of the flat bands in TBG, we neglect the latter.
In the Cooper channel
\mathbf{k}_{1}=-\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}=-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle H_{el-el}=-\frac{1}{N_{G}}\frac{1}{\omega_{A_{1}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{G}_{M},l,s,s_{1}}G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{\eta nn^{\prime}l}G_{-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}-\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{-\eta n_{1}n_{1}^{\prime}l}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta,s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k},n_{1},-\eta,s_{1}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n_{1}^{\prime},\eta,s_{1}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n^{\prime},-\eta,s}.

(170)

If one wants to include more bands in the system, one can extend the indices
n,n^{\prime}
, although due to the larger dispersion of the remote bands, one might need to keep the kinetic energies.

It should be noted as the summation over
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
is within the MBZ, so we should split the factor
N_{G}
(which represents the total number of atomic unit cells) into two parts,
N_{G}=N_{M}\times N_{0}
, with
N_{M}
the total number of Moiré unit cell and
N_{0}
the number of atomic unit cells in one Moiré unit cell.
Consequently, we can rewrite the above Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle H_{el-el}=-\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},s,s_{1}}V^{\eta nn^{\prime},n_{1}n_{1}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta,s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k},n_{1},-\eta,s_{1}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n_{1}^{\prime},\eta,s_{1}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n^{\prime},-\eta,s}

(171)

where

V^{\eta nn^{\prime},n_{1}n_{1}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{N_{0}}\frac{1}{\omega_{A_{1}}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}_{M}}\sum_{l}G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{\eta nn^{\prime},l}G_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}-\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{-\eta n_{1}n_{1}^{\prime},l}

(172)

represents the effective attractive electron-electron interaction mediated by phonons in TBG.

VI.3
Gauge Fixing for Eigen-state Basis and Chern Band Basis

VI.3.1
Eigen-state Basis

The eigen-energy basis of the bands in the TBG is given by

\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta,s}^{\dagger}=\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha}u_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha;n\eta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\eta,\alpha s}^{\dagger}\ ,

(173)

where
u_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha;n\eta}(\mathbf{k})
is the eigenstate of energy band
n
of the first quantized single-particle Hamiltonian
h_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)
in valley
\eta

\sum_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime},\beta}[h_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)]_{\alpha\beta}u_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\beta;n\eta}(\mathbf{k})=\epsilon_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})u_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha;n\eta}(\mathbf{k})\ ,

(174)

where
\epsilon_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})
is the single-particle energy of the eigen-state
u_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha;n\eta}(\mathbf{k})
. In each valley and spin, we use integers
n>0
to label the
n
-th conduction band, and use integer
n<0
to label the
|n|
-th valence band (thus
n\neq 0
). The lowest conduction and valence bands in each valley-spin flavor is thus labeled by
n=\pm 1
.

The BM Hamiltonian becomes

\hat{H}_{0}=\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\sum_{n\eta s}\epsilon_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})\gamma_{\mathbf{k}n\eta s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}n\eta s}.

(175)

Bloch periodicity and periodic gauge require

c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{b}_{Mi},\mathbf{Q},\eta\alpha s}^{\dagger}=c_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{b}_{Mi},\eta\alpha s}^{\dagger}\implies u_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha;n\eta}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{b}_{Mi}\right)=u_{\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{b}_{Mi},\alpha;n\eta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)\ \implies\gamma_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{b}_{Mi},n\eta s}^{\dagger}=\gamma_{\mathbf{k}n\eta s}^{\dagger}

(176)

and the
\hat{C}_{2z}
and
\hat{P}
symmetries require

\hat{C}_{2z}:\;\;\epsilon_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})=\epsilon_{n,-\eta}(-\mathbf{k})\ ,\qquad\hat{P}:\;\;\epsilon_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})=-\epsilon_{-n,\eta}(-\mathbf{k})\ .

(177)

VI.3.2
Gauge Fixing for Eigen-state Basis

We fix the gauge for the eigen-state basis of energy bands
\gamma_{\mathbf{k}n\eta s}^{\dagger}
in Eq. (
173
) for the symmetry operators
\hat{C}_{2z},\hat{T}
and
\hat{P}
. We denote the wave function
u_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha;n\eta}(\mathbf{k})
as a column vector
u_{n\eta}(\mathbf{k})
in the space of indices
\{\mathbf{Q},\alpha\}
.
A representation matrix
D(\hat{g})
of an operation
\hat{g}
acts on a wave function
u_{n\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})
, we denote the resulting wave function in the valley
\eta
for short as
[D(g)]_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}u_{n\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})
, the components of which are given by
\sum_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\beta\eta^{\prime}}[D(g)]_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha\eta,\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\beta\eta^{\prime}}u_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\beta;n\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})
.
For the symmetries
\hat{C}_{2z},\hat{T}
and
\hat{P}
, the sewing matrices
B^{\hat{g}}(\mathbf{k})
in the band and valley space are defined by

[D(\hat{C}_{2z})]_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}u_{n\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})=\sum_{m}[B^{C_{2z}}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}u_{m\eta}(-\mathbf{k})\ ,\;\;\;[D(\hat{T})]_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}u_{n\eta^{\prime}}^{*}(\mathbf{k})=\sum_{m}[B^{T}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}u_{m\eta}(-\mathbf{k})\ ,

(178)

[D(\hat{P})]_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}u_{n\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})=\sum_{m}[B^{P}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}u_{m\eta}(-\mathbf{k})\ .

(179)

For non-degenerate wave function
u_{n\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})
in the valley
\eta^{\prime}
, since
\hat{C}_{2z}
and
\hat{T}
commute with the
\hat{H}_{0}
and flips the valley
\eta
, while
\hat{P}
anti-commutes with
\hat{H}_{0}
and preserves the valley
\eta
, we generically have

\begin{split}&[B^{C_{2z}}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}=\delta_{\eta,-\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{m,n}e^{i\varphi^{C_{2z}}_{n,\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})}\ ,\quad[B^{T}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}=\delta_{\eta,-\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{m,n}e^{i\varphi^{T}_{n,\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})}\ ,\\
&[B^{P}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}=\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{-m,n}e^{i\varphi^{P}_{n,\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})}\ .\end{split}

(180)

Accordingly the energy band fermion operators transform as

\hat{g}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta^{\prime},s}^{\dagger}\hat{g}^{-1}=\sum_{m\eta}[B^{g}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}\gamma_{\hat{g}\mathbf{k},m,\eta,s}^{\dagger}\ .

(181)

Since these three symmetries satisfy the relations

C_{2z}^{2}=1\ ,\ T^{2}=1\ ,\ P^{2}=-1\ ,\ \{P,C_{2z}\}=0\ ,\ \{P,T\}=0\ ,\ [C_{2z},T]=0\ ,

(182)

With the above notations, the symmetries
\hat{C}_{2z},\hat{T}
and
\hat{P}
allows us to define

\begin{split}&B^{C_{2z}}(-\mathbf{k})B^{C_{2z}}(\mathbf{k})=B^{T}(-\mathbf{k})B^{T*}(\mathbf{k})=-B^{P}(-\mathbf{k})B^{P}(\mathbf{k})=I\ ,\qquad B^{P}(-\mathbf{k})B^{C_{2z}}(\mathbf{k})=-B^{C_{2z}}(-\mathbf{k})B^{P}(\mathbf{k})\ ,\\
&\qquad\qquad B^{P}(-\mathbf{k})B^{T}(\mathbf{k})=-B^{T}(-\mathbf{k})B^{P*}(\mathbf{k})\ ,\qquad B^{T}(-\mathbf{k})B^{C_{2z}*}(\mathbf{k})=B^{C_{2z}}(-\mathbf{k})B^{T}(\mathbf{k})\ ,\end{split}

(183)

where
B^{g*}(\mathbf{k})
stands for the complex conjugation of matrix
B^{g}(\mathbf{k})
, and
I
is the identity matrix in the
n,\eta
space.
We have two independent symmetry operations
\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{T}
(anti-unitary) and
\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{P}
(unitary), which do not change
\mathbf{k}
. Their sewing matrices are defined by

[D(\hat{C}_{2z})D(\hat{T})]_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}u_{n\eta^{\prime}}^{*}(\mathbf{k})=\sum_{m}[B^{C_{2z}T}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}u_{m\eta}(\mathbf{k})\ ,

(184)

[D(\hat{P})D(\hat{C}_{2z})]_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}u_{n\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})=\sum_{m}[B^{C_{2z}P}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}u_{m\eta}(\mathbf{k})\ .

(185)

For non-degenerate eigenstates at momentum
\mathbf{k}
(non-degenerate within one valley), we have

[B^{C_{2z}T}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}=\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{m,n}e^{i\varphi^{C_{2z}T}_{n,\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})}\ ,\qquad[B^{C_{2z}P}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}=\delta_{-\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{-m,n}e^{i\varphi^{C_{2z}P}_{n,\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})}\ ,

(186)

where by definition we have
\varphi^{C_{2z}T}_{n,\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})=\varphi^{T}_{n,\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})+\varphi^{C_{2z}}_{n,-\eta^{\prime}}(-\mathbf{k})
, and
\varphi^{C_{2z}P}_{n,\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})=\varphi^{C_{2z}}_{n,\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})+\varphi^{P}_{n,-\eta^{\prime}}(-\mathbf{k})
. The sewing matrices of
C_{2z}T
and
C_{2z}P
are subject to the constraint that

(C_{2z}T)^{2}=(C_{2z}P)^{2}=1\ ,\qquad[C_{2z}T,C_{2z}P]=0\ ,

(187)

and thus they satisfy

B^{C_{2z}T}(\mathbf{k})B^{C_{2z}T*}(\mathbf{k})=[B^{C_{2z}P}(\mathbf{k})]^{2}=I\ ,\qquad B^{C_{2z}P}(\mathbf{k})B^{C_{2z}T}(\mathbf{k})=B^{C_{2z}T}(\mathbf{k})B^{C_{2z}P*}(\mathbf{k})\ .

(188)

We will now fixe the gauge of the wave functions and sewing matrices of the
\mathbf{k}
preserving symmetry operations
C_{2z}T
and
C_{2z}P
. By Eqs. (
186
) and (
188
), we make the
\mathbf{k}
-independent choices for the sewing matrices

[B^{C_{2z}T}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}=\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{m,n}\ ,\qquad[B^{C_{2z}P}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}=-\text{sgn}(n)\eta^{\prime}\delta_{-\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{-m,n}\ .

(189)

Accordingly, the symmetry actions on the band basis fermion operators are given by

(C_{2z}T)\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta,s}^{\dagger}(C_{2z}T)^{-1}=\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta,s}^{\dagger}\ ,\qquad(C_{2z}P)\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta,s}^{\dagger}(C_{2z}P)^{-1}=-\text{sgn}(n)\eta\gamma_{\mathbf{k},-n,-\eta,s}^{\dagger}\ .

(190)

This does not yet fix the entire phases of the energy basis at momentum
\mathbf{k}
, since the sewing matrices in Eq. (
189
) are invariant under the unitary transformation of wave functions
u_{n\eta}(\mathbf{k})\rightarrow\text{sgn}(n)\eta u_{n\eta}(\mathbf{k})
at each individual
\mathbf{k}
. To further fix this gauge freedom for different
\mathbf{k}\in\text{MBZ}
, we start by choosing a momentum
\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{k}_{0}
where the eigenstates within one valley are non-degenerate, and choose a gauge fixing of the eigen-state basis at
\mathbf{k}_{0}
satisfying Eq. (
189
). We then fix the band basis of bands
\pm n
at other
\mathbf{k}\neq\mathbf{k}_{0}
by requiring

f_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k})=\left|u^{\dagger}_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q})u_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})-u^{\dagger}_{-n,\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q})u_{-n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})\right|

(191)

to be a continuous function of
\mathbf{k}
and
\mathbf{q}
, and satisfy

\lim_{\mathbf{q}\rightarrow\mathbf{0}}f_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k})=0

(192)

for all
\mathbf{k}
. This fixes the relative sign between wave functions
u_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})
and
u_{-n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})
in a way that is continuous in
\mathbf{k}
. We do not require the wave function
u_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})
itself to be globally continuous in
\mathbf{k}
of the entire MBZ; locally
u_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})
can always be chosen to be continuous in
\mathbf{k}
, provided
u_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})
is non-degenerate at momentum
\mathbf{k}
.

Within the space of each pair of PH symmetric bands with band indices
n=\pm n_{\text{B}}
, if we use
\zeta^{a}
and
\tau^{a}
(
a=0,x,y,z
) to denote the identity and Pauli matrices in the energy band
n=\pm n_{\text{B}}
space and the valley space:

B^{C_{2z}T}(\mathbf{k})=\zeta^{0}\tau^{0}\ ,\qquad B^{C_{2z}P}(\mathbf{k})=-\zeta^{y}\tau^{y}\ .

(193)

For
n_{\text{B}}=1
(i.e., within the lowest conduction and valence bands
n=\pm 1
per spin per valley) when
\mathbf{k}
is at
\mathbf{K}_{M}
or
\mathbf{K}_{M}^{\prime}
point of the MBZ,
the bands
n=+1
and
n=-1
are degenerate. In this case, we can still choose the eigenstate basis at
\mathbf{K}_{M}
or
\mathbf{K}_{M}^{\prime}
point such that Eqs. (
193
) and (
192
) are satisfied.

We further fix the relative gauge between wave functions at momenta
\mathbf{k}
and
-\mathbf{k}
by fixing the sewing matrices of
\hat{C}_{2z}
and
\hat{P}
.
For
\mathbf{k}
not at the
P
-invariant momenta, which are
\Gamma_{M}
and the three equivalent
M_{M}
in TBG, we have

B^{C_{2z}}(\mathbf{k})=\zeta^{0}\tau^{x}\ ,\qquad B^{T}(\mathbf{k})=\zeta^{0}\tau^{x}\ ,\qquad B^{P}(\mathbf{k})=-i\zeta^{y}\tau^{z}\ .

(194)

which are consistent with Eq. (
193
).
With the continous gauge condition, the sewing matrix
B^{P}(\mathbf{k})
must have additional minus signs,
i.e.
,
B^{P}(\mathbf{k})=i\zeta^{y}\tau^{z}
, at an odd (even) number of the four
P
-invariant momenta if the the two bands
n=\pm n_{B}
have an odd (even) topological winding number protected by
C_{2z}T
; and at the other odd (even)
P
-invariant momenta
B^{P}(\mathbf{k})
are
-i\zeta^{y}\tau^{z}
, same as those at generic momenta. Accordingly, the sewing matrices
B^{C_{2z}}(\mathbf{k})
and
B^{T}(\mathbf{k})
also have the additional minus at momenta where
B^{P}(\mathbf{k})
has the minus sign.
In this work, we choose
B^{P}(\mathbf{k}_{\Gamma_{M}})=-i\zeta^{y}\tau^{z}
and
B^{P}(\mathbf{k}_{M_{M}})=i\zeta^{y}\tau^{z}
.

VI.3.3
Gauge Fixing for Chern band basis

After the gauge fixing in Eqs. (
193
) and (
192
), we define a new irrep basis
\gamma^{(n_{\text{B}}){\dagger}}_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}

\gamma^{(n_{\text{B}}){\dagger}}_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}=\frac{\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},n_{\text{B}},\eta,s}+ie_{Y}\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},-n_{\text{B}},\eta,s}}{\sqrt{2}}\ ,\quad(e_{Y}=\pm 1).

(195)

For
n_{\text{B}}=1
, we call them the Chern band basis within the lowest two bands
\gamma^{(1){\dagger}}_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}=\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}
, as given in Eq. (
195
), where
e_{Y}=\pm 1
.

The basis
\gamma^{(n_{\text{B}}){\dagger}}_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}
defines a
band with well-defined Berry curvature; for fixed
