e_{Y},\eta,s
, Eq.(
195
) gives a band with Chern number

C^{n_{\text{B}}}_{e_{Y},\eta,s}=e_{Y}e_{2,n_{\text{B}}}\ ,

(196)

where
e_{2,n_{\text{B}}}\in\mathbb{Z}
is the Wilson loop winding number of the two bands
n=\pm n_{\text{B}}
, provided the pair of bands
n=\pm n_{\text{B}}
are disconnected with other bands. The wave functions of the Chern basis in Eq. (
195
) are given by

u_{e_{Y},n_{\text{B}},\eta}^{\prime}(\mathbf{k})=\frac{u_{+n_{\text{B}},\eta}(\mathbf{k})+ie_{Y}u_{-n_{\text{B}},\eta}(\mathbf{k})}{\sqrt{2}}\ .

(197)

Due to the continuous gauge condition, we see that
\lim_{\mathbf{q}\rightarrow\mathbf{0}}u^{\dagger}_{+n_{\text{B}},\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q})u_{+n_{\text{B}},\eta}(\mathbf{k})=\lim_{\mathbf{q}\rightarrow\mathbf{0}}u^{\dagger}_{-n_{\text{B}},\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q})u_{-n_{\text{B}},\eta}(\mathbf{k})
. Therefore, the Chern band wave functions satisfy the continuous condition

\lim_{\mathbf{q}\to 0}|u_{e_{Y},n_{\text{B}},\eta}^{\prime{\dagger}}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q})u_{e_{Y}^{\prime},n_{\text{B}},\eta}^{\prime}(\mathbf{k})|=\frac{1}{2}\lim_{\mathbf{q}\to 0}|u^{\dagger}_{+n_{\text{B}},\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q})u_{+n_{\text{B}},\eta}(\mathbf{k})+e_{Y}e_{Y}^{\prime}u^{\dagger}_{-n_{\text{B}},\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q})u_{-n_{\text{B}},\eta}(\mathbf{k})|=\delta_{e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}},

(198)

This allows us to define a continuous Berry curvature for the Chern band wave function
u_{e_{Y},n_{\text{B}},\eta}^{\prime}(\mathbf{k})
.

We first focus in the valley
\eta=+
. The sewing matrix for
C_{2z}T
restricted in the valley
\eta=+
is given by
B^{C_{2z}T}(\mathbf{k})=\zeta^{0}
(see Eq. (
193
)). Under this gauge, according to
Ahn et al. 2019
, the non-abelian Berry’s connection

[\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{k})]_{mn}=iu^{\dagger}_{m,+}(\mathbf{k})\partial_{\mathbf{k}}u_{n,+}(\mathbf{k})
will take the form

\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{k})=\begin{pmatrix}0&i\mathbf{a}(\mathbf{k})\\
-i\mathbf{a}(\mathbf{k})&0\end{pmatrix}.

(199)

The sign of wave functions
u_{n,+}(\mathbf{k})
is fixed in such a way that
\mathbf{a}(\mathbf{k})
is globally continuous in the MBZ excluding the Dirac nodes between the two bands
\pm n_{\text{B}}
(recall that we assume the bands
\pm n_{\text{B}}
are disconnected from other bands, and thus there can be Dirac nodes between them only if
n_{B}=1
), which is always possible
Ahn et al. 2019
. In particular, this way of sign fixing is consistent with Eq. (
192
), since the vanishing of the diagonal Berry’s connection requires
\lim_{\mathbf{q}\to 0}|u^{\dagger}_{m,\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q})u_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})|=\delta_{m,n}
.

It is known that the Wilson loop winding number of two bands isolated from other bands is given by the Euler class
Ahn et al. 2019
:

e_{2,n_{\text{B}}}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{i}\oint_{\partial D_{i}}d\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{a}(\mathbf{k})=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathrm{MBZ}-\sum_{i}D_{i}}d^{2}\mathbf{k}\ \Omega(\mathbf{k})\ ,

(200)

where
D_{i}
is a sufficiently small region containing the
i
th Dirac point in the BZ, and
\Omega(\mathbf{k})=\nabla_{\mathbf{k}}\times\mathbf{a}(\mathbf{k})
.

With Eq. (
199
), we can derive the Berry connection of the irrep band basis
\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},+,s}
at
\mathbf{k}
away from Dirac points as

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{e_{Y}}(\mathbf{k})=iu^{\prime{\dagger}}_{e_{Y},n_{\text{B}},+}(\mathbf{k})\partial_{\mathbf{k}}u_{e_{Y},n_{\text{B}},+}^{\prime}(\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{2}[u^{\dagger}_{+n_{\text{B}},+}(\mathbf{k})\partial_{\mathbf{k}}u_{+n_{\text{B}},+}(\mathbf{k})+ie_{Y}u^{\dagger}_{+n_{\text{B}},+}(\mathbf{k})\partial_{\mathbf{k}}u_{-n_{\text{B}},+}(\mathbf{k})-ie_{Y}u^{\dagger}_{-n_{\text{B}},+}(\mathbf{k})\partial_{\mathbf{k}}u_{+n_{\text{B}},+}(\mathbf{k})+u^{\dagger}_{-n_{\text{B}},+}(\mathbf{k})\partial_{\mathbf{k}}u_{-n_{\text{B}},+}(\mathbf{k})]

\displaystyle=e_{Y}\mathbf{a}(\mathbf{k})\ .

(201)

Furthermore, the Berry curvature can be shown to be non-divergent at the Dirac points between the two bands
n=\pm n_{\text{B}}
(see proof in
Song et al. 2021
). If
n_{\text{B}}>1
, there are no Dirac points between bands
n=\pm n_{\text{B}}
). Therefore, by Eq. (
200
), we find the irrep band basis
\gamma^{(n_{\text{B}})\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},+,s}
carries a Chern number given by Eq. (
196
). We also note that the
\hat{C}_{2z}
symmetry maps the irrep band basis
\gamma^{(n_{\text{B}})\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},+,s}
into
\gamma^{(n_{\text{B}})\dagger}_{-\mathbf{k},e_{Y},-,s}
(see Eq. (
194
)). Since
\hat{C}_{2z}
does not change the Chern number, we conclude that the Chern number of the Chern band basis
\gamma^{(n_{\text{B}})\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}
in the MBZ is simply given by Eq. (
196
).

In particular, for the lowest two bands
n_{\text{B}}=1
, the bands are topological and carry a winding number
e_{2}=1

Song et al. 2019
;
Po et al. 2019
;
Ahn et al. 2019
. Therefore, for the Chern band basis (the irrep basis with
n_{\text{B}}=1
)
\gamma^{\dagger}_{-\mathbf{k},e_{Y},-,s}
, we have Chern number

C_{e_{Y},\eta,s}=e_{Y}\ ,

(202)

thus the name “Chern band basis” within the lowest two bands (see
Song et al. 2021
for a more careful treatment at the Dirac points at CNP, which does not change the conclusion).

Now we show that if
e_{2,n_{B}}
is odd, then the sign of the sewing matrix
B^{P}(\mathbf{k})

must
be
\mathbf{k}
-dependent: for
\eta=+
,
B^{P}(\mathbf{k})
can be chosen as
-i\zeta^{y}
at all the momenta except one or three of the
P
-invariant momenta, where
B^{P}(\mathbf{k})
must be
i\zeta^{y}
.
To see this, we assume
B^{P}(\mathbf{k})=-i\chi(\mathbf{k})\zeta^{y}
, where
\chi(\mathbf{k})=\pm 1
, and transform it into the Chern band basis (
197
).
We obtain

B^{P\prime}_{e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})=u^{\prime\dagger}_{e_{Y},n_{B},+}(-\mathbf{k})D(P)u^{\prime}_{e_{Y},n_{B},+}(\mathbf{k})=-i\chi(\mathbf{k})e_{Y}\delta_{e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}.

(203)

Therefore,
\hat{P}
leaves each branch of the Chern band basis, which has the Chern numbers
e_{2,n_{B}}e_{Y}
, invariant.

i\hat{P}
can be equivalently thought as an inversion symmetry for each Chern band since it squares to 1 and changes
\mathbf{k}
to
-\mathbf{k}
.
The “inversion” eigenvalues of the Chern band
e_{Y}
are given by
\chi(\mathbf{k})e_{Y}
for
\mathbf{k}
being the
P
-invariant momentum.
Due to the relation between Chern number and inversion eigenvalues, we have

(-1)^{C^{n_{\text{B}}}_{e_{Y},\eta,s}}=\prod_{K}\chi(K),

(204)

where
K
indexes the four
P
-invariant momenta.
Therefore, the right hand side must be -1 (1) if
C^{n_{\text{B}}}_{e_{Y},\eta,s}
is odd (even), implying
\chi(K)=-1
at one or three (zero, two, or four) of the four
P
-invariant momenta. The sign of
B^{P}(\mathbf{k})
in the other valley
\eta=-
can be obtained from the constraint of
B^{C_{2z}P}(\mathbf{k})
and
B^{P}(\mathbf{k})
.

VI.3.4
The chiral symmetry at
w_{0}=0

At
w_{0}=0
, the single-particle Hamiltonian of TBG acquires an additional unitary chiral symmetry
\hat{C}
,
which satisfies the anti-commutation relation with the full single-particle Hamiltonian
\hat{H}_{0}
in Eq. (
140
)

\{\hat{C},\hat{H}_{0}\}=0;\;\;\;\;\hat{C}c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\eta,\alpha,s}\hat{C}^{-1}=\sum_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\beta}[D(\hat{C})]_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\beta,\mathbf{Q}\eta\alpha}c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\beta,s}\ ,

(205)

with the representation matrix

\quad[D(\hat{C})]_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\beta,\mathbf{Q}\eta\alpha}=\delta_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime},\eta}(\sigma_{z})_{\beta,\alpha}\ .

(206)

Note that
\hat{C}
preserves the electron momentum
\mathbf{k}
. Since
\hat{C}
flips the single-particle Hamiltonian
\hat{H}_{0}
,
it is not a commuting symmetry of TBG, but only reflects a relation between the positive and negative energy spectra. The transformation
\hat{C}
satisfies

\hat{C}^{2}=1,\qquad\{\hat{C},\hat{C}_{2z}\}=0,\qquad[\hat{C},\hat{T}]=0,\qquad[\hat{C},\hat{P}]=0,\qquad\{\hat{C},\hat{C}_{2z}T\}=0,\qquad\{\hat{C},\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{P}\}=0.

(207)

When transformed into the energy band basis, the chiral symmetry
\hat{C}
implies

\epsilon_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})=-\epsilon_{-n,\eta}(\mathbf{k}),\;\;\;[D(\hat{C})]_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}u_{n\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})=\sum_{m}[B^{C}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}u_{m\eta}(\mathbf{k})\ ,

(208)

where

[B^{C}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}=\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{-m,n}e^{i\varphi^{C}_{n,\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})};\;\;\;\hat{C}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta^{\prime},s}^{\dagger}\hat{C}^{-1}=\sum_{m\eta}[B^{C}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},m,\eta,s}^{\dagger}\ .

(209)

By the relations
\{\hat{C},\hat{C}_{2z}T\}=\{\hat{C},\hat{C}_{2z}P\}=0
, the sewing matrix of
C
satisfies

B^{C}(\mathbf{k})B^{C_{2z}T}(\mathbf{k})=-B^{C_{2z}T}(\mathbf{k})B^{C*}(\mathbf{k})\ ,\qquad B^{C}(\mathbf{k})B^{C_{2z}P}(\mathbf{k})=-B^{C_{2z}P}(\mathbf{k})B^{C}(\mathbf{k})\ .

(210)

Under the gauge fixing of Eq. (
193
), we have
B^{C_{2z}T}(\mathbf{k})=\zeta^{0}\tau^{0}
, and
B^{C_{2z}P}(\mathbf{k})=-\zeta^{y}\tau^{y}
. The only
\mathbf{k}
-independent gauge for sewing matrix of
C
in consistency with Eqs. (
209
) and (
210
) within each pair of bands
n=\pm n_{\text{B}}
is then (up to a global minus sign)

B^{C}(\mathbf{k})=\zeta^{y}\tau^{z}\ .

(211)

In particular, this
\mathbf{k}
-independent gauge fixing (
211
) of
C
automatically ensures the continuous gauge fixing condition (
192
), which is crucial for defining the irrep band basis in Eq. (
195
). To see this, note that Eq. (
211
) tells us that
u_{-n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})=i\text{sgn}(n)\eta u_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})
for band
n=\pm n_{\text{B}}
, and thus we have

f_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k})=\left|u^{\dagger}_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q})u_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})-u^{\dagger}_{-n,\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q})u_{-n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})\right|=\left|u^{\dagger}_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q})u_{n,\eta}(\mathbf{k})[1-\text{sgn}(n)^{2}\eta^{2}]\right|=0

(212)

for any
\mathbf{k}
and
\mathbf{q}
, satisfying Eq. (
192
).

We also note that this gauge fixing of
C
is consistent with the gauge fixings of both
C_{2z}
and
P
separately in Eq. (
194
). Basically, the relations
\{\hat{C},\hat{C}_{2z}\}=0
and
[\hat{C},\hat{P}]=0
requires

B^{C}(-\mathbf{k})B^{C_{2z}}(\mathbf{k})=-B^{C_{2z}}(\mathbf{k})B^{C}(\mathbf{k})\ ,\qquad B^{C}(-\mathbf{k})B^{P}(\mathbf{k})=B^{P}(\mathbf{k})B^{C}(\mathbf{k})\ ,

(213)

which is satisfied by Eq. (
211
)

VI.4
Discrete Symmetry Analysis of the Intra-Layer Inter-valley Electron-Phonon Interaction and the phonon-induced electron-electron interaction

We transform the phonon-mediated electron-electron interaction
H_{el-el}
into the Chern band basis and the Hamiltonian is written as

\displaystyle H_{el-el}=-\frac{1}{N_{G}\omega_{A_{1}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{G}_{M},l,s,s_{1}}G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{\eta e_{Y}e_{Y}^{\prime}l}G_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}-\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{-\eta e_{Y_{1}}e_{Y_{1}}^{\prime}l}\delta_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}e_{Y}\eta s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1}e_{Y_{1}}-\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},e_{Y_{1}}^{\prime},\eta,s_{1}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},e_{Y}^{\prime},-\eta,s}

with

G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta e_{Y}e_{Y}^{\prime}l}=\frac{\gamma_{3}}{\sqrt{2M\omega_{A_{1}}}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta}}\sum_{\alpha\beta}u^{e_{Y}\star}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta},\alpha,\eta}\sigma^{x}_{\alpha\beta}u^{e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta}-\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta},\beta,-\eta}.

(215)

Here we consider the chiral limit with
w_{0}=0
and will discuss the constraint on the form of
G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}}^{\eta e_{Y}e_{Y}^{\prime}l}
,
as well as
V^{\eta e_{Y}e_{Y}^{\prime},e_{Y1}e_{Y1}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}
, from different discrete symmetries.

•

Chiral Symmetry
\hat{C}
:

\displaystyle[D(\hat{C})]_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}u_{n\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})=\sum_{m}[B^{C}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}u_{m\eta}(\mathbf{k}),\;\;B^{C}(\mathbf{k})=\zeta^{y}\tau^{z},

\displaystyle[B^{C}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}=-im\delta_{m,-n}\eta\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}},\;\;\quad[D(\hat{C})]_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\beta,\mathbf{Q}\eta\alpha}=\delta_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime},\eta}(\sigma_{z})_{\beta,\alpha}=\delta_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime},\eta}(-1)^{\alpha+1}\delta_{\beta,\alpha}

(216)

which leads to

(-1)^{\alpha+1}u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,n,\eta}=in\eta u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,-n,\eta}\implies\boxed{u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,n,\eta}=-in\eta(-1)^{\alpha}u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,-n,\eta}}

(217)

in the eigen-state basis.
On the Chern band basis, we have

\displaystyle u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,e_{Y},\eta}=\frac{u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,+,\eta}+ie_{Y}u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,-,\eta}}{\sqrt{2}}=\frac{-i\eta(-1)^{\alpha}u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,-,\eta}+ie_{Y}(-i)(-1)\eta(-1)^{\alpha}u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,+,\eta}}{\sqrt{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{-i\eta(-1)^{\alpha}u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,-,\eta}-e_{Y}\eta(-1)^{\alpha}u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,+,\eta}}{\sqrt{2}}=-e_{Y}\eta(-1)^{\alpha}\frac{ie_{Y}u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,-,\eta}+u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,+,\eta}}{\sqrt{2}}=-e_{Y}\eta(-1)^{\alpha}u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,e_{Y},\eta}.

(218)

This shows that, depending on the valley
\eta
and Chern number
e_{Y}
, the Chern band basis is fully sub-lattice polarized (in the chiral limit) and that the Chern number for
u_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{Q}\alpha e_{Y}\eta}
is
e_{Y}=\eta(-1)^{\alpha+1}
.
The sewing matrix in the Chern band basis can be obtained from

B^{g}_{e_{Y_{1}}\eta;e_{Y}\eta^{\prime}}=\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\alpha}u^{\star}_{\hat{g}\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,e_{Y_{1}},\eta}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}[D(\hat{g})]_{\mathbf{Q}\eta\alpha;\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}u_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}e_{Y}\eta^{\prime}}

(219)

which leads to

B^{C}_{e_{Y_{1}}\eta;e_{Y}\eta^{\prime}}=e_{Y}\eta\delta_{e_{Y_{1}},e_{Y}}\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}.

(220)

From the above constraint equation for the wave function, the constraint equation for
G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}}^{\eta e_{Y}e_{Y}^{\prime}l}
is given by

\displaystyle G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta e_{Y}e_{Y}^{\prime}l}=e_{Y}e_{Y}^{\prime}G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta e_{Y}e_{Y}^{\prime}l}

\displaystyle\implies G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta e_{Y}e_{Y}^{\prime}l}=\delta_{e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta e_{Y}e_{Y}l}=:\delta_{e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta e_{Y}l}

(221)

We only keep one
e_{Y}
index in the last equality. This greatly simplifies the form of the electron-electron interaction, which becomes

\displaystyle H_{el-el}=-\frac{1}{N_{G}\omega_{A_{1}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{G}_{M},l,s,s_{1},e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{\eta e_{Y}l}G_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}-\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{-\eta e_{Y1}l}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1},e_{Y_{1}},-\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},e_{Y_{1}},\eta,s_{1}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},e_{Y},-\eta,s}

(222)

with

G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta e_{Y}l}=\frac{\gamma_{3}}{\sqrt{2M\omega_{A_{1}}}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta}}\sum_{\alpha\beta}u^{e_{Y}\star}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta},\alpha,\eta}\sigma^{x}_{\alpha\beta}u^{e_{Y}}_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta}-\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta},\beta,-\eta}.

(223)

•

\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{P}
:

\displaystyle[D(C_{2z}P)]_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}u_{n\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})=\sum_{m}[B^{C_{2z}P}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}u_{m\eta}(\mathbf{k}),\;\;\;B^{C_{2z}P}(\mathbf{k})=-\zeta^{y}\tau^{y}

\displaystyle[B^{C_{2z}P}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta,n\eta^{\prime}}=m\eta\delta_{m,-n}\delta_{\eta,-\eta^{\prime}},\;[D(\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{P})]_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha\eta;\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\beta\eta^{\prime}}=\delta_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\zeta_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\sigma^{x}_{\alpha\beta}\delta_{\eta,-\eta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\implies\delta_{\eta,-\eta^{\prime}}\zeta_{\mathbf{Q}}\sigma^{x}_{\alpha\beta}u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\beta,n\eta^{\prime}}=m\eta\delta_{m,-n}\delta_{\eta,-\eta^{\prime}}u_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{Q}\alpha m\eta}\implies

\displaystyle\boxed{u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,m\eta}=m\eta\zeta_{Q}u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\bar{\alpha},-m,-\eta}}

(224)

On the Chern band basis, this gives

\displaystyle u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,e_{Y},\eta}=-ie_{Y}\eta\zeta_{Q}u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\bar{\alpha},e_{Y},-{\eta}}

(225)

and thus

B^{C_{2z}P}_{e_{Y_{1}}\eta;e_{Y}\eta^{\prime}}=-ie_{Y}\eta^{\prime}\delta_{e_{Y_{1}},e_{Y}}\delta_{\eta,-\eta^{\prime}}.

(226)

\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{P}
can lead to the constraint

\displaystyle G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta e_{Y}l}=G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{-\eta e_{Y}-l}

(227)

where we have used
\zeta_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta}}=l\eta;\;\;\zeta_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta}-\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}=\zeta_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime\prime}_{-l\eta}}=-l\eta
.

•

\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{T}
:

u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\beta,n,\eta}=\sigma^{x}_{\beta\alpha}u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,n,\eta}^{\star}\implies u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,e_{Y},\eta}=u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\bar{\alpha},-e_{Y}\eta}^{\star}

(228)

and the sewing matrix is

B^{C_{2z}T}_{e_{Y_{1}}\eta;e_{Y}\eta^{\prime}}=\delta_{e_{Y_{1}},-e_{Y}}\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}

(229)

This now gives the following transformation property

G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{\eta e_{Y}l}=(G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{\eta-e_{Y}l})^{\star}.

(230)

•

Particle-hole symmetry
\hat{P}
:
we next deal with the particle-hole symmetry, of which the gauge fixing cannot be done with a constant matrix over the entire MBZ as discussed in
Sec.
VI.3.2
and
VI.3.3
.
We write the sewing matrix for
\hat{P}
as

B^{P}(\mathbf{k})=e^{i\phi_{\mathbf{k}}}i\zeta^{y}\tau^{z},

(231)

where
e^{i\phi_{\Gamma_{M}}}=-1
,
e^{i\phi_{M_{M}}}=1
and
e^{i\phi_{\mathbf{k}}}=-1
for a generic
\mathbf{k}
.
With

\sum_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}[D(\hat{P})]_{\mathbf{Q}\eta\alpha;\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}u_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}n\eta^{\prime}}=\sum_{m}B^{P}_{m\eta;n\eta^{\prime}}u_{-\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,m,\eta}

(232)

and
B^{P}_{m\eta;n\eta^{\prime}}=e^{i\phi_{\mathbf{k}}}m\eta\delta_{m,-n}\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}
, we obtain

\zeta_{\mathbf{Q}}u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha n\eta^{\prime}}=e^{i\phi_{\mathbf{k}}}m\eta\delta_{m,-n}\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}u_{-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{Q},\alpha m\eta}\implies u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha-m\eta}=e^{i\phi_{\mathbf{k}}}\zeta_{\mathbf{Q}}m\eta u_{-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{Q},\alpha m\eta}.

(233)

on the eigen-state basis and

\displaystyle u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,e_{Y},\eta}=ie_{Y}e^{i\phi_{\mathbf{k}}}\zeta_{\mathbf{Q}}\eta u_{-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{Q},{\alpha},e_{Y},{\eta}}

(234)

on the Chern band basis. The corresponding sewing matrix on the Chern band basis reads

B^{P}_{e_{Y_{1}}\eta;e_{Y}\eta^{\prime}}=ie_{Y}\eta e^{i\phi_{\mathbf{k}}}\delta_{e_{Y_{1}},e_{Y}}\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}},

(235)

which leads to the constraint

\displaystyle G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}=-l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}}^{\eta e_{Y}l}=e^{i(\phi_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}-\phi_{\mathbf{k}})}G_{-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{l\eta}=l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}-\mathbf{G}}^{\eta e_{Y}-l}.

(236)

•

\hat{C}_{3z}
:
The sewing matrix
B^{C_{3z}}(\mathbf{k})
satisfies the following conditions (we write the
\mathbf{k}
dependence on the sewing matrices of
\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{T}
and
\hat{C}_{2z}P
for completeness, although we know these matrices have been chosen
\mathbf{k}
-independent):

\displaystyle Unitarity:\;\;\;B^{C_{3z}}(\mathbf{k})B^{C_{3z}\dagger}(\mathbf{k})=B^{C_{3z}\dagger}(\mathbf{k})B^{C_{3z}}(\mathbf{k})=\zeta_{0}\tau_{0}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{3z}^{3}=1:\;\;\;B^{C_{3z}}(\hat{C}_{3}^{2}\mathbf{k})B^{C_{3z}}(\hat{C}_{3}\mathbf{k})B^{C_{3z}}(\mathbf{k})=\zeta_{0}\tau_{0}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{T}:\;\;\;B^{C_{3z}}(\mathbf{k})B^{C_{2z}T}(\mathbf{k})=B^{C_{2z}T}(\hat{C}_{3z}\mathbf{k})B^{C_{3z}\star}(\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{P}:\;\;\;B^{C_{3z}}(\mathbf{k})B^{C_{2z}P}(\mathbf{k})=B^{C_{2z}P}(\hat{C}_{3z}\mathbf{k})B^{C_{3z}}(\mathbf{k})

(237)

It is easiest to solve the constraint from
\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{T}
first, since its sewing matrix is identity. Hence, the matrix elements of
B^{C_{3z}}(\mathbf{k})
are all real. We also know
\hat{C}_{3z}
does not change the valley and hence

B^{C_{3z}}(\mathbf{k})_{m\eta;n\eta^{\prime}}=A_{mn}^{\eta}\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}

(238)

with the real matrix
A_{mn}^{\eta}
to be determined. The unitarity condition gives
AA^{T}=1
and hence
A
is a rotation matrix,

[B^{C_{3z}}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta;n\eta^{\prime}}=\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\begin{pmatrix}\cos\theta^{\eta}(\mathbf{k})&-\sin\theta^{\eta}(\mathbf{k})\\
\sin\theta^{\eta}(\mathbf{k})&\cos\theta^{\eta}(\mathbf{k})\end{pmatrix}

(239)

Since
[B^{C_{2z}P}(\mathbf{k})]_{m\eta;n\eta^{\prime}}=m\eta\delta_{m,-n}\delta_{\eta,-\eta^{\prime}}
, the condition becomes

\displaystyle A^{-\eta}_{r,-n}(-n)=rA^{\eta}_{-r,n}\implies A^{-\eta}_{1,1}=A^{\eta}_{2,2},\;\;A^{-\eta}_{1,2}=-A^{\eta}_{2,1}

(240)

where
n,r=\pm
translates into
r=1,2
in the matrix. This condition implies the
C_{3z}
sewing matrix is the same at either of the valleys,
namely

B^{C_{3z}}(\mathbf{k})_{m\eta;n\eta^{\prime}}=\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\begin{pmatrix}\cos\theta(\mathbf{k})&-\sin\theta(\mathbf{k})\\
\sin\theta(\mathbf{k})&\cos\theta(\mathbf{k})\end{pmatrix}

(241)

Finally
\hat{C}_{3z}^{3}=1
implies

\theta(\mathbf{k})+\theta(\hat{C}_{3z}\mathbf{k})+\theta(\hat{C}_{3z}^{2}\mathbf{k})=2\pi n,\;\;\;n\in Z

(242)

where
n
is an integer. On the eigen-state basis, this reads

e^{i\eta\frac{2\pi}{3}(-1)^{\alpha+1}}u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha n\eta}=\sum_{m=1,2}A_{mn}u_{\hat{C}_{3}\mathbf{k},\hat{C}_{3}\mathbf{Q},\alpha m\eta}

(243)

while on the Chern band basis, this then gives

u_{\hat{C}_{3}\mathbf{k},\hat{C}_{3}\mathbf{Q},\alpha e_{Y}\eta}=e^{ie_{Y}\theta(\mathbf{k})}e^{i\eta\frac{2\pi}{3}(-1)^{\alpha+1}}u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha e_{Y}\eta},

(244)

from which we find the sewing matrix of
\hat{C}_{3z}
on the Chern basis is diagonal

B^{C_{3z}}_{e_{Y_{1}}\eta,e_{Y}\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})=e^{-ie_{Y}\theta(\mathbf{k})}\delta_{e_{Y},e_{Y_{1}}}\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}.

(245)

This can be understood from the fact that in the chiral limit, there exists a relation between the Chern number and the sublattice/valley polarization,

e_{Y}=\eta(-1)^{\alpha+1}
.

We can see that
\theta(\mathbf{k})
cannot be constant over the whole MBZ. At the
\hat{C}_{3z}
-invariant points, i.e.
\mathbf{k}=\Gamma_{M}
,
\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{K}_{M}
, or
\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{K}_{M}^{\prime}
,
e^{-ie_{Y}\theta(\mathbf{k})}
is the
\hat{C}_{3z}
-eigenvalue, and there exists a relation between the Chern number and
\hat{C}_{3z}
-eigenvalues,

e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}C}=B(\Gamma_{M})B(\mathbf{K}_{M})B(\mathbf{K}_{M}^{\prime})=e^{-ie_{Y}(\theta(\Gamma_{M})+\theta(\mathbf{K}_{M})+\theta(\mathbf{K}_{M}^{\prime}))},

(246)

in which
C
is the Chern number and it should take
C=e_{Y}
for the Chern band basis. The
\theta
value at
\Gamma_{M}
can be obtained from
the relation (
242
),

\theta(\Gamma_{M})+\theta(\Gamma_{M})+\theta(\Gamma_{M})=2\pi n,\;\;\;n\in Z\implies\theta(\Gamma_{M})=2\pi/3\mod 2\pi.

(247)

So if we assume
\theta
is a constant over the whole MBZ, we will find
e^{-ie_{Y}(\theta(\Gamma_{M})+\theta(\mathbf{K}_{M})+\theta(\mathbf{K}_{M}^{\prime}))}=1
,
which would mean zero Chern number (mod 3) and not
e_{Y}=1
as it is. From the TBG, we know the
\hat{C}_{3z}
-eigenvalues of the flat bands is
1
at
\Gamma
,
and
\exp(\pm 2\pi i/3)
at
\mathbf{K}_{M}
,
\mathbf{K}_{M}^{\prime}
(for the degenerate point). When we separate the bands into Chern band basis, the
\hat{C}_{3z}
-eigenvalue at
\mathbf{K}_{M}
of the Chern band and that at
\mathbf{K}_{M}^{\prime}
have to be the same (if they were opposite, and with eigenvalue
1
at
\Gamma_{M}
, we would get zero Chern number). We hence deduce

\theta(\Gamma_{M})=0\mod 2\pi n;\;\;\;\theta(\mathbf{K}_{M})=\frac{2\pi}{3}\mod 2\pi;\;\;\;\theta(\mathbf{K}_{M}^{\prime})=\frac{2\pi}{3}\mod 2\pi

(248)

which gives

e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}C}=B(\Gamma_{M})B(K_{M})B(K_{M}^{\prime})=e^{-ie_{Y}(\theta(\Gamma_{M})+\theta(K_{M})+\theta(K_{M}^{\prime}))}=e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}e_{Y}},

(249)

leading to
C=e_{Y}
as advertised.

The
\hat{C}_{3z}
symmetry gives a constraint on the
G
function,

G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta e_{Y}l}=e^{ie_{Y}(\theta(\mathbf{k})-\theta(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}))}G_{\hat{C}_{3z}\mathbf{k},\hat{C}_{3z}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\hat{C}_{3z}\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta e_{Y}l}

(250)

•

\hat{C}_{2x}
:
The sewing matrix for
\hat{C}_{2x}
takes the form

B^{C_{2x}}_{m\eta;n\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})=A^{\eta}_{mn}\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}

(251)

and we need to again determine the matrix
A^{\eta}_{mn}
. For
\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{T}
,

\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{T}:\;\;\;B^{C_{2x}}(\mathbf{k})B^{C_{2z}T}(\mathbf{k})=B^{C_{2z}T}(\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{k})B^{C_{2x}\star}(\mathbf{k})\implies A=A^{\star}

(252)

and the unitarity condition requires
AA^{T}=1
. Furthermore, for the chiral symmetry
\hat{C}
,

\displaystyle\{C,C_{2x}\}=0:\;\;B^{C_{2x}}(\mathbf{k})B^{C}(\mathbf{k})=-B^{C}(\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{k})B^{C_{2x}}(\mathbf{k})\implies A^{\eta}(\mathbf{k})=\cos[\alpha^{\eta}(\mathbf{k})]\zeta_{z}+\sin[\alpha^{\eta}(\mathbf{k})]\zeta_{x}

(253)

with
\zeta
being in
n=1,2
band space and
\alpha^{\eta}
being an angle (not to be confused for the orbital
\alpha
). For
\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{P}
,

\displaystyle\{\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{P},\hat{C}_{2x}\}=0:\;\;B^{C_{2x}}(\mathbf{k})B^{C_{2z}P}(\mathbf{k})=-B^{C_{2z}P}(\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{k})B^{C_{2x}}(\mathbf{k}),

\displaystyle A^{-\eta}_{-m,-n}=-mnA^{\eta}_{m,n}\implies\alpha^{\eta}(\mathbf{k})=\alpha^{-\eta}(\mathbf{k})=\alpha(\mathbf{k}).

(254)

For the
\hat{C}_{3z}
rotation,

\displaystyle B^{C_{2x}}(\hat{C}_{3z}^{2}\mathbf{k})B^{C_{3z}}(\hat{C}_{3z}\mathbf{k})B^{C_{3z}}(\mathbf{k})=B^{C_{3z}}(\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{k})B^{C_{2x}}(\mathbf{k})\implies

\displaystyle\alpha_{\hat{C}_{3z}^{2}\mathbf{k}}-\alpha_{\mathbf{k}}-\theta_{\hat{C}_{3z}\mathbf{k}}-\theta_{\mathbf{k}}-\theta_{\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{k}}=0\mod 2\pi

(255)

Putting all the above together, one can find the transformation for the Chern band basis

u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha e_{Y}\eta}=e^{ie_{Y}\alpha(\mathbf{k})}u_{\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{k},\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{Q},\bar{\alpha},-e_{Y},\eta}

(256)

from which we see that

\alpha(\mathbf{k})-\alpha(\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{k})=0\mod 2\pi.

(257)

The sewing matrix is then

B^{C_{2x}}_{e_{Y_{1}}\eta;e_{Y}\eta^{\prime}}=\delta_{e_{Y_{1}},-e_{Y}}e^{ie_{Y}\alpha(\mathbf{k})}\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}.

(258)

\hat{C}_{2x}
imposes the following constraint

\displaystyle G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},Q_{-l\eta}=-l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}}^{\eta,e_{Y},l}=e^{ie_{Y}(\alpha(\mathbf{k}^{\prime})-\alpha(\mathbf{k}))}G_{\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{k},\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}=\hat{C}_{2x}(-l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G})}^{\eta,-e_{Y},-l}

(259)

•

Hermiticity: From the definition of
G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{\eta nn^{\prime}l}
, one can obtain the identity

G_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{k},-l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{\eta,n^{\prime}nl\star}=G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}-\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{-\eta,nn^{\prime}l}

(260)

which is required for the hermiticity condition
H_{el-el}^{\dagger}=H_{el-el}
.

We now define the
F
-function

F^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{N_{0}\omega_{A_{1}}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}_{M},l}G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{\eta,e_{Y},l}G_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}-\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{-\eta,e_{Y}^{\prime},l}

(261)

so that the phonon-mediated electron-electron interaction takes the form

H_{el-el}=-\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},s,s_{1},e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}F^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1},e_{Y_{1}},-\eta,s_{1}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},e_{Y_{1}},\eta,s_{1}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},e_{Y},-\eta,s}

(262)

We need to keep in mind that this term actually came from neglecting the one-body term in the phonon-mediated electron-electron interaction, which is

H_{el-el}=-\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},s,s_{1},e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}F^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},e_{Y},-\eta,s}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1},e_{Y_{1}},-\eta,s_{1}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},e_{Y_{1}},\eta,s_{1}}

(263)

We can apply all the symmetry operators to derive the constraint equations for the
F
-function, which are summarized as follows.

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{P}:\boxed{F^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}=F^{-\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{T}:\boxed{F^{\eta e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}=F^{\eta,-e_{Y},-e_{Y}^{\prime}\star}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle Hermiticiy:\boxed{F^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}=F^{\eta,e_{Y}^{\prime},e_{Y}\star}_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}_{1};\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}}}

\displaystyle Reshuffling:\boxed{F^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}=F^{-\eta,e_{Y}^{\prime},e_{Y}}_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime};\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\hat{P}:\boxed{F^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}=e^{i(\phi_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}-\phi_{\mathbf{k}}+\phi_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}-\phi_{\mathbf{k}_{1}})}F^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{k}^{\prime};-\mathbf{k}_{1},-\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{3z}:\;\;\boxed{F^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}=e^{ie_{Y}(\theta(\mathbf{k})-\theta(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}))+ie_{Y}^{\prime}(\theta(\mathbf{k}_{1})-\theta(\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}))}F^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\hat{C}_{3z}\mathbf{k},\hat{C}_{3z}\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\hat{C}_{3z}\mathbf{k}_{1},\hat{C}_{3z}\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{2x}:\;\;\boxed{F^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}=e^{-ie_{Y}(\alpha(\mathbf{k})-\alpha(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}))-ie_{Y}^{\prime}(\alpha(\mathbf{k}_{1})-\alpha(\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}))}F^{\eta,-e_{Y},-e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{k},\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{k}_{1},\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}}

(264)

Since we have already derived all the symmetry constraint for the
G
-function, all the symmetry constraint on the
F
-function can be derived from
the definition of
F
-function (Eq.
261
). Here we only show an example of reshuffling symmetry.

\displaystyle F^{\eta e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}=\sum_{\mathbf{G}_{M}}\sum_{l}G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{\eta e_{Y}l}G_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}-\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{-\eta e_{Y}^{\prime}l}

\displaystyle F^{-\eta e_{Y}^{\prime},e_{Y}}_{\mathbf{k}_{1}\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime};\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=\sum_{\mathbf{G}_{M}}\sum_{l}G_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{-\eta e_{Y}^{\prime}l}G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}-\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{\eta e_{Y}l}=F^{\eta e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}.

(265)

The above symmetry relations relate different components of the
F
-function and let’s next count the number of independent parameters. Out of
2\times 16=32
complex numbers for each set of
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}
momenta, we are left with
2
complex numbers
F^{+++}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}
,
F^{++-}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}
out of which we can obtain the entire set of values.

Next we consider the phonon-mediated electron-electron interaction in the Cooper channel (similar to Eq.
171
but in the Chern band basis),

\displaystyle H_{el-el}=-\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},s,s_{1},e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}e_{Y}\eta s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k}e_{Y_{1}}-\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},e_{Y_{1}},\eta,s_{1}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}e_{Y},-\eta,s}

(266)

with

\displaystyle V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=F^{\eta e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}.

(267)

From the above constraint equations for the
F
-function, we can easily extract the corresponding relations for
V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}
,
which are listed as follows.

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{P}:\boxed{V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{-\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{T}:\boxed{V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{\eta,-e_{Y},-e_{Y}^{\prime}\star}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle Hermiticiy:\boxed{V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{\eta,e_{Y}^{\prime},e_{Y}\star}_{-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-\mathbf{k}}}

\displaystyle Reshuffling:\boxed{V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{-\eta,e_{Y}^{\prime},e_{Y}}_{-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\hat{P}:\boxed{V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{3z}:\;\;\boxed{V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=e^{ie_{Y}(\theta(\mathbf{k})-\theta(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}))+ie_{Y}^{\prime}(\theta(-\mathbf{k})-\theta(-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}))}V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\hat{C}_{3z}\mathbf{k},\hat{C}_{3z}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{2x}:\;\;\boxed{V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=e^{-ie_{Y}(\alpha(\mathbf{k})-\alpha(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}))-ie_{Y}^{\prime}(\alpha(-\mathbf{k})-\alpha(-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}))}V^{\eta,-e_{Y},-e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{k},\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}}

(268)

We now have the constraint equations for
\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{P}
and
\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{T}
for the
V
potential at the same momentum. In addition,
the combination of reshuffling and particle-hole symmetry also give one more constraint equation that does not change the momentum,

\displaystyle\text{Reshuffling and }\hat{P}:\boxed{V^{\eta,e_{Y}e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{-\eta,e_{Y}^{\prime}e_{Y}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}}.

(269)

Putting the above results together, we can explicitly show the following constraints for different components of the
V
potential at the same momentum

\displaystyle V^{+++}_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{-++}_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{+--\star}_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{---\star}_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle V^{++-}_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{--+}_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{+-+}_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{-+-}_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{+-+\star}_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{-+-\star}_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{++-\star}_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V^{--+\star}_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}

(270)

We notice that there are more constraints for the case of
e_{Y}\neq e^{\prime}_{Y}
, provided by the combination of Reshuffling and
P
. This extra constraint is trivial
for the case of
e_{Y}=e^{\prime}_{Y}
since the constraint from Reshuffling and
P
is the identical to that from
C_{2z}P
.
Hence fundamentally, in the chiral limit, the phonon-mediated electron-electron interaction at every
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
depends only on one complex parameter
V^{+++}_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}
and one
real
parameter
V^{++-}_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}
.

For the above symmetry constraint results, we have two more comments, as discussed below.

•

While we see the change of
e^{i\phi_{\mathbf{k}}}
in the sewing matrix of
\hat{P}
between
k=\Gamma_{M}
and
k=M_{M}
, this does not help to determine the sign changes of the
V
potential. The reason is that, for the Cooper channel (
\mathbf{k}_{1}=-\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}=-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
), we have

F^{\eta e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=e^{i(\phi_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}-\phi_{\mathbf{k}}+\phi_{-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}-\phi_{-\mathbf{k}})}F^{\eta e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}

(271)

We can also find a relation between
\phi_{\mathbf{k}}
and
\phi_{-\mathbf{k}}

\displaystyle u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,e_{Y},\eta}=ie_{Y}e^{i\phi_{\mathbf{k}}}\zeta_{\mathbf{Q}}\eta u_{-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{Q},\bar{\alpha},e_{Y},{\eta}}\implies-ie_{Y}e^{-i\phi_{\mathbf{k}}}\eta=\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\alpha}u_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\alpha,e_{Y},\eta}^{\star}\zeta_{\mathbf{Q}}u_{-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{Q},\bar{\alpha},e_{Y},{\eta}}\implies

\displaystyle-ie_{Y}e^{-i\phi_{-\mathbf{k}}}\eta=-ie_{Y}e^{i\phi_{\mathbf{k}}}\eta\implies e^{i(\phi_{\mathbf{k}}+\phi_{-\mathbf{k}})}=1

(272)

Thus, we have

F^{\eta e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=F^{\eta e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}},

(273)

which corresponds to the constraint equation for
V
potential from
\hat{P}
. Hence, the derivation here show that no sign change occurs because of the
\hat{P}
sewing matrix.

•

We now analyze the constraint from
\hat{C}_{3z}
. First we put
\mathbf{k}^{\prime}=0
and we have

V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},0}=e^{ie_{Y}(\theta(\mathbf{k})-2\theta(0))+ie_{Y}^{\prime}\theta(-\mathbf{k})}V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\hat{C}_{3}\mathbf{k},0}

(274)

Now notice that if we put
\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{K}_{M}
then
-\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{K}_{M}^{\prime}
but
\theta(\mathbf{K}_{M})=\theta(\mathbf{K}_{M}^{\prime})=2\pi/3\mod 2\pi
,
and we have (
\theta_{0}=0\mod 2\pi
)

V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{K}_{M},0}=e^{i(e_{Y}+e_{Y}^{\prime})\frac{2\pi}{3}}V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{K}_{M},0}

(275)

(one notes that
V_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=V_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{G}}=V_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}
).
Hence, there are nodes for the channel with
e_{Y}=e_{Y}^{\prime}
, but not for
e_{Y}=-e_{Y}^{\prime}
, as shown below.

e_{Y}=e_{Y}^{\prime}:\;\;V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}}_{\mathbf{K}_{M},0}=e^{ie_{Y}\frac{4\pi}{3}}V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}}_{\mathbf{K}_{M},0}\implies V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}}_{\mathbf{K}_{M},0}=0.

(276)

VI.5
Continuous Symmetry Analysis of the Intra-layer and Inter-valley Electron-Phonon Interaction and the phonon-induced electron-electron interaction

It is known that the projected Coulomb interaction Hamiltonian into the flat bands, in the chiral limit, enjoys a large
U(4)\times U(4)
symmetry with generators

S^{ab}_{e_{Y}}=\sum_{\mathbf{k}\eta s\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}e_{Y}\eta s}\tau^{a}_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}s^{b}_{ss^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}e_{Y}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}},\;\;\;\ e_{Y}=\pm 1,\;a,b=0,1,2,3

(277)

However, this enlarged symmetry will be broken by the electron-phonon coupling. To see that, we calculate
[H_{el-el},S^{ab}_{e_{Y_{2}}}]
and with
some tedious calculation, we find

\displaystyle[H_{el-el},S^{ab}_{e_{Y_{2}}}]=2F^{\eta e_{Y},e_{Y_{2}}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}e_{Y}\eta s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}e_{Y}-\eta s}(\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1}e_{Y_{2}}-\eta s_{2}}^{\dagger}\tau^{a}_{\eta,\eta_{2}}s^{b}_{s_{2}s_{1}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}e_{Y_{2}}\eta_{2}s_{1}}-\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1}e_{Y_{2}}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}\tau^{a}_{\eta_{2},-\eta}s^{b}_{s_{2}s_{1}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}e_{Y_{2}}\eta s_{1}})+

\displaystyle+\text{one-body terms},

(278)

from which one can see that the two body terms only vanish for

a=0,\;\;\forall b\implies U_{s}(2)_{e_{Y}=1}\times U_{s}(2)_{e_{Y}=-1}

(279)

where
s
means spin. The electron-phonon coupling breaks the
U(4)\times U(4)
symmetry in the chiral flat-band limit to a
U_{s}(2)_{e_{Y}=1}\times U_{s}(2)_{e_{Y}=-1}
spin
U(2)
symmetry in each Chern sector, with the generators

S^{b}_{e_{Y}}=\sum_{\mathbf{k}\eta s}\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}e_{Y}\eta s}s^{b}_{ss^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}e_{Y}\eta s^{\prime}},\;\;\;\ e_{Y}=\pm 1,\;b=0,1,2,3

(280)

This symmetry is broken away from the chiral flat-band limit, leaving behind just the total
U(2)
spin symmetry.

We also have
a=3,b=0
(Valley
U(1)
charge
per Chern sector
) for which
\tau^{a}_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}=\eta\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}
.
In this case, one can show

\displaystyle[H_{el-el},S^{30}_{e_{Y_{2}}}]=-\frac{2}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},s,s_{1},e_{Y}}\eta

\displaystyle[F^{\eta e_{Y},e_{Y_{2}}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},e_{Y},-\eta s}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1},e_{Y_{2}},-\eta,s_{1}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},e_{Y_{2}},\eta,s_{1}}-F^{\eta e_{Y_{2}},e_{Y}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime};\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y_{2}},\eta s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},e_{Y_{2}},-\eta,s}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1},e_{Y},-\eta,s_{1}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},e_{Y},\eta,s_{1}}.

(281)

And we can see that Valley
U(1)
charge per Chern band sector is
not
conserved. However, the
total (summed over both chern sectors)

U(1)
Valley charge

Q_{v}=\sum_{\mathbf{k}e_{Y}\eta s}\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}e_{Y}\eta s}\tau^{z}_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}e_{Y}\eta s}

(282)

is a good quantum number; it remains so away from the chiral flat-band limit.

VI.6
Numerical results for the phonon-induced electron-electron interaction

In this section, we will discuss our numerical results for the electron-phonon interaction and
the phonon-induced electron-electron interaction in TBG and compare the results
with the symmetry analysis of
V^{\eta e_{1}e_{2}}_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}
in the previous section.

Our numerical calculation is based on the expression (
136
) for electron-phonon coupling
g^{s}_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})

in one single layer and then project it into the flat band basis in TBG as

\displaystyle G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},Q_{-l\eta}}^{\eta nn^{\prime}ls}=\sum_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta},\alpha\beta}u^{n\star}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta},\alpha,\eta}g^{s,l}_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})u^{n^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta}-\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta},\beta,-\eta},

(283)

with
\mathbf{p}=\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta}
and
\mathbf{p}^{\prime}=\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta}+\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}
and
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
is chosen within the Moiré Brillouin zone.
The corresponding inter-valley electron-phonon interaction is then given by

\displaystyle H_{inter-vall}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{G}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\in MBZ}\sum_{n,n^{\prime}=1,2}\sum_{\eta,s}\sum_{l=\pm}\sum_{Q_{-l\eta}}G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta nn^{\prime}ls}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta,s}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n^{\prime},-\eta,s}

\displaystyle(b_{-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta},l,A_{1}}+b_{\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}-\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta},l,A_{1}}^{\dagger})

(284)

Since the Moiré Hamiltonian is expanded around
\mathbf{K}_{D}
, namely
|\mathbf{K}_{D}|\gg k,k^{\prime},|\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta}|
and
g^{s}_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})
is a smooth
function of
\mathbf{p}
and
\mathbf{p}^{\prime}
, we can approximate
g^{s}_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})
by
g^{s}_{\alpha\beta}(\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})
in our numerical calculations.
We test this approximation by directly evaluating
g^{s}_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})
and find that the derivation of
g^{s}_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{\prime})
from

g^{s}_{\alpha\beta}(\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})
is only within 5% at the relevant momentum scale of Moiré reciprocal lattice vectors.
For
g^{s}_{\alpha\beta}(\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})
, only the phonon mode
s=A1
give non-zero contribution, so we only keep
s=A1
below and drop the
s-index in calculating coupling strength

\displaystyle G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta nn^{\prime}l}=\sum_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta},\alpha\beta}u^{n\star}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta},\alpha,\eta}g^{l}_{\alpha\beta}(\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})u^{n^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta}-\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta},\beta,-\eta}.

(285)

A similar expression of electron-phonon coupling parameter for the Chern band basis is given by

\displaystyle G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta e_{Y}e_{Y}^{\prime}l}=\sum_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta},\alpha\beta}u^{\star}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta},\alpha,e_{Y},\eta}g^{l}_{\alpha\beta}(\eta\mathbf{K}_{D},-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D})u_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{l\eta}-\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta},\beta,e_{Y}^{\prime},-\eta}.

(286)

The corresponding phonon-induced electron-electron interaction in the Cooper pair channels is given by

H_{el-el}=-\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},s_{1},s_{2},e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s_{1}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k},e_{Y}^{\prime},-\eta,s_{2}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},e_{Y}^{\prime},\eta,s_{2}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},e_{Y},-\eta,s_{1}}

(287)

where

V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{N_{0}}\frac{1}{\omega_{A_{1}}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}_{M}}\sum_{l}G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}+\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{\eta e_{Y}l}G_{-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},l\eta\mathbf{q}_{2}-\mathbf{G}_{M}}^{-\eta e_{Y}^{\prime}l}.

(288)

Here we have already added the required factors
N_{0}
and
N_{M}
and divided the expression by
\omega_{A_{1}}
so

V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}
has the correct energy unit.

In the above expression, the phonon momentum is
\mathbf{q}=-\eta\mathbf{K}_{D}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}
and the e-ph coupling parameter

G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}}^{\eta e_{Y}e_{Y}^{\prime}l}
depends on the Moiré momentum points
\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}
. In Fig.
11

and
12
, we plot
|G|
as a function of different values of
|\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}|
for the non-chiral and chiral limit, separately, on the eigen-state basis.
In Fig.
13
, we plot
|G|
as a function of different values of
|\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}|
in the chiral limit on the Chern-band basis.
The corresponding lattice of
\mathbf{Q}_{-l\eta}
and different shells are shown in Fig.
10
(b).
One can see that while
|G|
generally decays away for large
|G|
’s in both cases, the decay rate is faster for the chiral limit.
Furthermore, we notice that in the chiral limit on the Chern-band basis (Fig.
13
),
|G^{e_{Y}e_{Y}^{\prime}}|
is zero if
e_{Y}\neq e_{Y}^{\prime}
, which has been derived in our analytical results, see Eq.
•
‣
VI.4
.
The detailed form of
G
function is quite complicated and thus, we, instead, show
V^{\eta,e_{Y},e_{Y}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}
as a function of
\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
in
Fig.
14
for
\mathbf{k}=\Gamma_{M},\mathbf{K}_{M},M_{M}
. Several features are noticed: (1) Intra-Chern-band channel
(
e_{Y}=e_{Y}^{\prime}=\pm
) shows nodes at
\mathbf{k}=\Gamma_{M},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}=\mathbf{K}_{M}
, which is consistent with Eq. (
276
) from the symmetry analysis.
(2) We notice that the interaction for the inter-Chern-band channels keeps its value over the whole MBZ while that for the intra-Chern-band channels drops
quickly near the MBZ boundary due to the presence of the nodes.

VI.7
Symmetry Classification of Irreducible Pairing Channels

Since the phonon energy and the electron-phonon coupling are strong, the eige-state basis might not be the best basis to work in here.
We hence work in the Chern band basis, for which the symmetry transformation properties are summarized as follows.

\displaystyle\hat{g}\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}e_{Y}\eta^{\prime}s}\hat{g}^{-1}=\sum_{e_{Y_{1}}\eta}B^{g}_{\eta e_{Y_{1}};e_{Y}\eta^{\prime}}\gamma^{\dagger}_{\hat{g}\mathbf{k},e_{Y_{1}}\eta s}

\displaystyle\hat{C}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}^{\dagger}\hat{C}^{-1}=e_{Y}\eta\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{P}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}^{\dagger}(\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{P})^{-1}=ie_{Y}\eta\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},-\eta,s}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{T}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}^{\dagger}(\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{T})^{-1}=\gamma_{\mathbf{k},-e_{Y},\eta,s}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\hat{P}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}^{\dagger}\hat{P}^{-1}=ie_{Y}\eta e^{i\phi_{\mathbf{k}}}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{3z}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}^{\dagger}\hat{C}_{3z}^{-1}=e^{-ie_{Y}\theta(\mathbf{k})}\gamma_{\hat{C}_{3z}\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{2x}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}^{\dagger}\hat{C}_{2x}^{-1}=e^{ie_{Y}\alpha(\mathbf{k})}\gamma_{\hat{C}_{2x}\mathbf{k},-e_{Y},\eta,s}^{\dagger}

(289)

VI.7.1
Discrete Symmetry analysis of the Gap Function

The mean-field Bogoliubov-de Gennes (BdG) Hamiltonian for the inter-valley pairing can be written as

H_{\Delta}=\sum_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y_{1}},e_{Y_{2}},s_{1},s_{2},\eta}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y_{1}},\eta,s_{1}}^{\dagger}\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{1}}s_{1},e_{Y_{2}}s_{2}}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k},e_{Y_{2}},-\eta,s_{2}}^{\dagger}+h.c.

(290)

First we notice the reshuffling symmetry of the gap function,

\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{1}}s_{1},e_{Y_{2}}s_{2}}=-\Delta^{-\eta}_{-\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{2}}s_{2},e_{Y_{1}}s_{1}},

(291)

which can be obtained by interchanging two
\gamma^{\dagger}
operators and then relabelling the indices.

Because of the
SU(2)
spin symmetry of the system, We can define

\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{1}}s_{1},e_{Y_{2}}s_{2}}=\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{1}},e_{Y_{2}}}{\mathcal{S}}_{s_{1},s_{2}}.

(292)

Where
{\mathcal{S}}
is the spin matrix, which takes the form

\text{Singlet: }{\mathcal{S}}_{s_{1}s_{2}}=(is_{y})_{s_{1}s_{2}};\;\;\;\text{Triplet: }{\mathcal{S}}_{s_{1}s_{2}}=((\mathbf{d}\cdot{\bf s})is_{y})_{s_{1}s_{2}};

(293)

where
\mathbf{d}
is the three component d-vector for triplet pairing. We note that

\text{Singlet: }{\mathcal{S}}_{s_{1}s_{2}}=-{\mathcal{S}}_{s_{2}s_{1}};\;\;\;\text{Triplet: }{\mathcal{S}}_{s_{1}s_{2}}={\mathcal{S}}_{s_{2}s_{1}};

(294)

, or

E_{1}

(x,y)

2

-\sigma_{0}

\sigma_{z}

\exp(-i2\pi\sigma_{y}/3)

E_{2}

(
\frac{x^{2}-y^{2}}{2},xy
)

2

\sigma_{0}

\sigma_{z}

\exp(i2\pi\sigma_{y}/3)

Table 4:
Irreducible pairing channels.

We now write down the basis function of the irreducible representation (irrep) of the
C_{6v}
group,
which is the symmetry group of the TBG Hamiltonian with two valleys,
in the table
4
. The pairing Hamiltonian
H_{\Delta}
can transform as a representation of the
C_{6v}
group.
Thus, based on the table
4
, we next consider the symmetry classification of the irreducible pairing channels
Savary et al. 2017
;
Sigrist and Ueda 1991
.

Let’s first consider (spinless)
\hat{C}_{2z}
symmetry, which generally plays the role of inversion and allows us to identify the parity of different pairing channels. For
\hat{C}_{2z}
, the electron operator is transformed as

\hat{C}_{2z}\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta s}\hat{C}_{2z}^{-1}=e^{i\phi_{\mathbf{k}}}\gamma^{\dagger}_{-\mathbf{k},e_{Y},-\eta s}

(295)

From the table
4
, We expect the pairing Hamiltonian
H_{\Delta}
should transform as certain representations under
C_{2z}
,
with the eigenvalue
\lambda_{C_{2z}}=\pm 1
,

\hat{C}_{2z}H_{\Delta}\hat{C}_{2z}^{-1}=\lambda_{C_{2z}}H_{\Delta}.

(296)

In the case of
\lambda_{C_{2z}}=1
, the Hamiltonian is invariant under
\hat{C}_{2z}
. For the case of
\lambda_{C_{2z}}=-1
, the Hamiltonian is not invariant under
\hat{C}_{2z}
, as the gap term changes sign. However, it is invariant under
\hat{C}_{2z}
followed by a gauge transformation
c\rightarrow ic
, i.e. a projective representation. Hence, the spectrum is still
\hat{C}_{2z}
symmetric.
With
\exp(i\phi_{\mathbf{k}}+i\phi_{-\mathbf{k}})=1
, the gap function then follows the relation

\displaystyle\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{1}s_{1},e_{2}s_{2}}=\lambda_{C_{2z}}\Delta^{-\eta}_{-\mathbf{k};e_{1}s_{1},e_{2}s_{2}}.

(297)

Together with the reshuffling symmetry (
291
), we have

\displaystyle\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{1},s_{1},e_{2},s_{2}}=-\lambda_{C_{2z}}\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{2},s_{2},e_{1},s_{1}},

(298)

which leads to

\text{Singlet: }\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}=\lambda_{C_{2z}}\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{2},e_{1}};\;\;\;\text{Triplet: }\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}=-\lambda_{C_{2z}}\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{2},e_{1}}.

(299)

Next let’s consider the (spinless)
\hat{C}_{3z}
symmetry. Since the
1
-D irrep of
C_{6v}
have only eigenvalue
1
for
\hat{C}_{3z}
,
the transformation of the pairing Hamiltonian is given by

\hat{C}_{3z}H_{\Delta}\hat{C}_{3z}^{-1}=H_{\Delta},

(300)

which leads to

\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}=e^{-i(e_{1}\theta_{\mathbf{k}}+e_{2}\theta_{-\mathbf{k}})}\Delta^{\eta}_{\hat{C}_{3z}^{-1}\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}

(301)

irrespective of the triplet and singlet paring channels.
At the
\mathbf{K}_{M}
or
\mathbf{K}_{M}^{\prime}
points, this gap then becomes

\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{K}_{M};e_{1},e_{2}}=e^{-i(e_{1}\theta_{\mathbf{K}_{M}}+e_{2}\theta_{-\mathbf{K}_{M}})}\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{K}_{M};e_{1},e_{2}}=e^{-i(e_{1}+e_{2})\theta_{\mathbf{K}_{M}}}\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{K}_{M},e_{1},e_{2}}

(302)

where we have used
\theta_{\mathbf{K}_{M}}=\theta_{-\mathbf{K}_{M}}=2\pi/3
for the TBG flat bands, as we previously showed.
This means that any
1D
irrep will have, for the pairing between the bands with equal Chern number (called ”intra-Chern-band pairing” below),

\Delta_{\mathbf{K}_{M};e_{Y},e_{Y}}=0

(303)

No such condition exists for pairing between opposite Chern number bands
e_{Y_{1}}=-e_{Y_{2}}
(called ”inter-Chern-band pairing” below).
Hence we have the following theorem:
The gap function of the 1D irrep pairing channel between two bands with the equal Chern number
\pm 1
(intra-Chern-band pairing) of the
\hat{C}_{6v}
symmetry group has nodes at
\mathbf{K}_{M}
,
\mathbf{K}_{M}^{\prime}
.

Based on the above discussion, we can summarize the 1D irrep pairing channels as the following.

•

A_{1},A_{2}
irrep channels:

\text{Singlet: }\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}=\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{2},e_{1}};\;\;\;\text{Triplet: }\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}=-\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{2},e_{1}};\;\;\;\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}=e^{-i(e_{1}\theta_{\mathbf{k}}+e_{2}\theta_{-\mathbf{k}})}\Delta^{\eta}_{\hat{C}_{3z}^{-1}k,e_{1},e_{2}}.

(304)

For these irrep channels, the singlet pairing between the bands with the same Chern number has a node at
\mathbf{K}_{M}
, while the triplet pairing between the bands
with the same Chern number vanishes identically as
\lambda_{C_{2z}}=1
for the
A_{1},A_{2}
pairing channels.

•

B_{1},B_{2}
irrep channels:

\text{Singlet: }\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}=-\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{2},e_{1}};\;\;\;\text{Triplet: }\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}=\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{2},e_{1}};\;\;\;\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}=e^{-i(e_{1}\theta_{\mathbf{k}}+e_{2}\theta_{-\mathbf{k}})}\Delta_{\hat{C}_{3z}^{-1}\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}

(305)

For these representations, the triplet pairing between same Chern number bands has a zero at
K_{M}
, while the singlet pairing between the same Chern numbers vanishes identically as
\lambda_{C_{2z}}=-1
for the
B_{1},B_{2}
pairing channels.

We now move to the
2D
irrep pairing channels, which necessarily require two pairing terms
(H_{\Delta_{1}},H_{\Delta_{2}})
and two different gaps
\Delta_{1;\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}},\Delta_{2;\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}
. The pairing Hamiltonian is written as

\displaystyle H_{\Delta}=H_{\Delta_{1}}+H_{\Delta_{2}}+H^{\dagger}_{\Delta_{1}}+H^{\dagger}_{\Delta_{2}}

(306)

\displaystyle H_{\Delta_{1}}=\sum_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y_{1}},e_{Y_{2}},\eta}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y_{1}},\eta}^{\dagger}\Delta^{\eta}_{1;\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{1}},e_{Y_{2}}}{\mathcal{S}}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k},e_{Y_{2}},-\eta}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle H_{\Delta_{2}}=\sum_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y_{1}},e_{Y_{2}},\eta}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y_{1}},\eta}^{\dagger}\Delta^{\eta}_{2;\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{1}},e_{Y_{2}}}{\mathcal{S}}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k},e_{Y_{2}},-\eta}^{\dagger}.

Here we have suppressed the spin index and all the spin information is included in the
{\mathcal{S}}
function (singlet or triplet).
According to the table
4
, the
\hat{C}_{2z}
symmetry should give the constraints

\hat{C}_{2z}H_{\Delta_{1}}\hat{C}_{2z}^{-1}=\lambda_{C_{2z}}H_{\Delta_{1}},\;\;\hat{C}_{2z}H_{\Delta_{2}}\hat{C}_{2z}^{-1}=\lambda_{C_{2z}}H_{\Delta_{2}};\;\;\;\Delta^{\eta}_{1;\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}=\pm\lambda_{C_{2z}}\Delta^{\eta}_{1;\mathbf{k};e_{2},e_{1}};\;\;\Delta^{\eta}_{2;\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}=\pm\lambda_{C_{2z}}\Delta^{\eta}_{2;\mathbf{k};e_{2},e_{1}}

(307)

with
+
for singlet pairing and
-
for triplet pairing (and
\lambda_{C_{2z}}=-(+)
for
E_{1}(E_{2})
irrep channels, respectively).
One notices that
(H_{\Delta_{1}}
and
H_{\Delta_{2}})
have to be both singlet or both triplet. The actual order parameter can be a combination of
aH_{\Delta_{1}}+bH_{\Delta_{2}}
, where
a
and
b
can be complex numbers. This breaks
\hat{C}_{3z}
symmetry and is hence nematic. Another possibility would be a time-reversal breaking order parameter, which can respect
\hat{C}_{3z}
. We now treat each of the two
2D
irrep channels separately as follows.

•

E_{2}
irrep channels:
We have

\hat{C}_{3z}H_{\Delta_{\alpha}}\hat{C}_{3z}^{-1}=H_{\Delta_{\beta}}(e^{-i\frac{2\pi}{3}\sigma_{y}})_{\beta\alpha}\implies e^{-i(e_{1}\theta_{\mathbf{k}}+e_{2}\theta_{-\mathbf{k}})}\Delta^{\eta}_{\alpha;\hat{C}_{3z}^{-1}\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}=\Delta^{\eta}_{\beta;\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}(e^{-i\frac{2\pi}{3}\sigma_{y}})_{\beta\alpha},

(308)

where
\alpha,\beta=1,2
indices represent the different pairing channels and
\sigma
is the corresponding Pauli matrix. For
\mathbf{k}=\Gamma_{M}
with
\theta_{\Gamma_{M}}=0
, the equation above becomes

\Delta^{\eta}_{2;\Gamma_{M};e_{1},e_{2}}=\sqrt{3}\Delta^{\eta}_{1;\Gamma_{M};e_{1},e_{2}};\;\;\Delta^{\eta}_{1;\Gamma_{M};e_{1},e_{2}}=-\sqrt{3}\Delta^{\eta}_{2;\Gamma_{M};e_{1},e_{2}}\implies\Delta^{\eta}_{1;\Gamma_{M};e_{1},e_{2}}=\Delta^{\eta}_{2;\Gamma_{M};e_{1},e_{2}}=0

(309)

Hence the nematic
2D
irrep
E_{2}
channel has a zero in the gap function at the
\Gamma_{M}
point for both the same and different Chern number pairing.
This is different from the
1D
irrep pairing channels. At
\mathbf{K}_{M}
, we have

\Delta^{\eta}_{1;\mathbf{K}_{M};e_{1},-e_{1}}=\Delta^{\eta}_{2;\mathbf{K}_{M};e_{1},-e_{1}}=0;\;\;\;\;ie_{1}\Delta^{\eta}_{1;\mathbf{K}_{M};e_{1},e_{1}}=\Delta^{\eta}_{2;\mathbf{K}_{M};e_{1},e_{1}}

(310)

Hence the pairing between the bands with opposite Chern number is zero at
\mathbf{K}_{M}
, while the pairing between the bands with the same Chern number is nonzero and satisfies the constraint above.
From
\hat{C}_{2z}
, the
\lambda_{C_{2z}}=1
for the
E_{2}
representation and we have

\Delta^{\eta}_{1;\mathbf{k}e_{1},e_{2}}=\pm\Delta_{1;\mathbf{k}e_{2},e_{1}};\;\;\Delta_{2;\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}=\pm\Delta_{2;\mathbf{k};e_{2},e_{1}},

(311)

where, again, we have
\pm
for singlet/triplet. We can then see that for same chern number pairing, there cannot be a triplet component.

•

E_{1}
irrep channels:
We have

\hat{C}_{3z}H_{\Delta_{\alpha}}\hat{C}_{3z}^{-1}=H_{\Delta_{\beta}}(e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}\sigma_{y}})_{\beta\alpha}\implies e^{-i(e_{1}\theta_{\mathbf{k}}+e_{2}\theta_{-\mathbf{k}})}\Delta^{\eta}_{\alpha;\hat{C}_{3z}^{-1}\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}=\Delta^{\eta}_{\beta;\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}(e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}\sigma_{y}})_{\beta\alpha}.

(312)

For
\mathbf{k}=\Gamma_{M}
and
\theta_{\Gamma_{M}}=0
, the equation above becomes

\Delta^{\eta}_{2;\Gamma_{M};e_{1},e_{2}}=-\sqrt{3}\Delta^{\eta}_{1;\Gamma_{M};e_{1},e_{2}};\;\;\Delta^{\eta}_{1;\Gamma_{M};e_{1},e_{2}}=\sqrt{3}\Delta^{\eta}_{2;\Gamma_{M};e_{1},e_{2}}\implies\Delta^{\eta}_{1;\Gamma_{M};e_{1},e_{2}}=\Delta^{\eta}_{2;\Gamma_{M};e_{1},e_{2}}=0.

(313)

Hence, similar as the
E_{2}
irrep case, the nematic
2D
irrep
E_{1}
channel also has a zero in the gap function at the
\Gamma_{M}
point for both the same and different Chern number pairing. At
K_{M}
we have

\Delta^{\eta}_{1;\mathbf{K}_{M};e_{1},-e_{1}}=\Delta^{\eta}_{2;\mathbf{K}_{M};e_{1},-e_{1}}=0;\;\;\;\;ie_{1}\Delta_{1;\mathbf{K}_{M};e_{1},e_{1}}=-\Delta_{2;\mathbf{K}_{M};e_{1},e_{1}}

(314)

Hence, the pairing between the bands with different Chern numbers is zero at
\mathbf{K}_{M}
while the pairing between the bands with the same Chern number is nonzero and satisfies the constraint above. From
\hat{C}_{2z}
, the
\lambda_{C_{2z}}=-1
for the
E_{2}
representation and we have

\Delta^{\eta}_{1;\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}=\mp\Delta^{\eta}_{1;\mathbf{k};e_{2},e_{1}};\;\;\Delta^{\eta}_{2;\mathbf{k};e_{1},e_{2}}=\mp\Delta^{\eta}_{2;\mathbf{k};e_{2},e_{1}}

(315)

with
\mp
for singlet/triplet. We can then see that for the pairing between the bands with the same Chern number, there cannot be a singlet component.

Finally, let’s discuss the constraint due to the presence of time reversal (TR) symmetry. It should be noted that TR may be spontaneously broken by the gap function,
so the constraint derived below can only be applied to the TR invariant pairing.
We can combine the spinless
\hat{C}_{2z}
with the
spinful
TR
\hat{T}
(both are independent symmetries of the TBG system), which gives

\hat{C}_{2z}T\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,s}(\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{T})^{-1}=\sum_{s^{\prime}}\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},-e_{Y},\eta,s^{\prime}}(i{\mathcal{S}}_{y})_{s^{\prime}s}.

(316)

Applying this symmetry to the pairing Hamiltonian (
290
) implies that

\epsilon_{s_{1}^{\prime}s_{1}}(\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};-e_{Y_{1}}s_{1},-e_{Y_{2}}s_{2}})^{\star}\epsilon_{s_{2}^{\prime}s_{2}}=\Delta_{k;e_{Y_{1}}s_{1}^{\prime},e_{Y_{2}}s_{2}^{\prime}},

(317)

where
\epsilon_{s_{1}^{\prime}s_{1}}
is the Levi-Civita symbol. Hence, for spin singlet and triplet, we have two transformations for the gap function,

\displaystyle\text{Singlet: }\epsilon_{s_{1}^{\prime}s_{1}}(\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};-e_{Y_{1}},-e_{Y_{2}}})^{\star}\epsilon_{s_{1},s_{2}}\epsilon_{s_{2}^{\prime}s_{2}}=\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{1}},e_{Y_{2}}}\epsilon_{s_{1}^{\prime},s_{2}^{\prime}}\implies\boxed{(\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};-e_{Y_{1}},-e_{Y_{2}}})^{\star}=\Delta_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{1}},e_{Y_{2}}}}

\displaystyle\text{Triplet: }(\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};-e_{Y_{1}},-e_{Y_{2}}})^{\star}(\mathbf{d}\cdot\ss)^{\star}_{s_{1}s_{2}}=\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{1}},e_{Y_{2}}}(\mathbf{d}\cdot\ss)_{\bar{s}_{1}\bar{s}_{2}}(-1)^{s_{1}+s_{2}}\implies\boxed{(\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};-e_{Y_{1}},-e_{Y_{2}}})^{\star}=-\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{Y_{1}},e_{Y_{2}}}}

(318)

where
s=1,2
are
\uparrow,\downarrow
while
\bar{1}=2
and vice versa.

VI.7.2
Continuous Symmetry Analysis of Irreducible Pairing Channels

Now let’s consider the constraint from the continuous symmetry in the chiral limit on the phonon-mediated electron-electron interaction in Eq. (
287
) when taking the mean field decomposition for different pairing channels.

In the chiral limit, we have an
SU(2)
for each chern number sector and we now separate the two
SU(2)
operators per chern number sector into

S^{+}_{e_{Y}}=\sum_{\mathbf{k}\eta}\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,\uparrow}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,\downarrow},\;\;\;\;S^{-}_{e_{Y}}=\sum_{\mathbf{k}\eta}\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,\downarrow}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{Y},\eta,\uparrow},

(319)

which can be further linearly combined to form the total (tot) spin and relative (rel) spin raising and lowering operators,

\displaystyle S^{+}_{tot}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(S^{+}_{+}+S^{+}_{-}),\;\;\;S^{-}_{tot}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(S^{-}_{+}+S^{-}_{-})

\displaystyle S^{+}_{rel}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(S^{+}_{+}-S^{+}_{-}),\;\;\;S^{-}_{rel}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(S^{-}_{+}-S^{-}_{-}).

(320)

We first separate the gap function based on the total spin - singlet and triplet components. This will be the same even away from the chiral limit.

We now define the two-fermion pairing operators

\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\eta;e_{1}s_{1};e_{2},s_{2}}=\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{1},\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}\gamma^{\dagger}_{-\mathbf{k},e_{2},-\eta s_{2}}

(321)

and decompose them into singlet and triplet channels,

\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\eta,e_{1}s_{1};e_{2},s_{2}}=\sum_{S=0,1;M=-S\ldots S}C_{s_{1}s_{2}}^{SM}\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\eta;e_{1},e_{2},S,M}

(322)

where
C_{s_{1}s_{2}}^{SM}
are the Clebsh-Godron Coefficients. The operator form of the
S,M
pairing channel is then given by

\displaystyle\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+;e_{1},e_{2},0,0}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{1},+,\uparrow}^{\dagger}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k},e_{2},-,\downarrow}^{\dagger}-\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{1},+,\downarrow}^{\dagger}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k},e_{2},-,\uparrow}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+;e_{1},e_{2},1,0}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{1},+\uparrow}^{\dagger}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k},e_{2},-\downarrow}^{\dagger}+\gamma_{\mathbf{k},e_{1},+,\downarrow}^{\dagger}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k},e_{2},-,\uparrow}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+;e_{1},e_{2},1,1}=\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},e_{1},+,\uparrow}\gamma^{\dagger}_{-\mathbf{k},e_{2},-,\uparrow}

\displaystyle\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+;e_{1},e_{2},1,-1}=\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},e_{1},+,\downarrow}\gamma^{\dagger}_{-\mathbf{k},e_{2},-,\downarrow}.

(323)

Besides the usual rotations within spin
1
by the raising and lowering total spin operators

\displaystyle S_{tot}^{-}\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+;e_{1},e_{2},1,1}\left|0\right\rangle=\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+;e_{1},e_{2},1,0}\left|0\right\rangle,\;\;\;(S_{tot}^{-})^{2}\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+;e_{1},e_{2},1,1}\left|0\right\rangle=\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+;e_{1},e_{2},1,-1}\left|0\right\rangle,

(324)

we also have, for
e_{1}=e_{2}
,

\displaystyle S_{rel}^{-}\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+;e_{1},e_{1},1,1}\left|0\right\rangle=e_{1}\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+;e_{1},e_{2},1,0}\left|0\right\rangle;\;\;\;\;(S_{rel}^{-})^{2}\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+;e_{1},e_{1},1,1}\left|0\right\rangle=\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+;e_{1},e_{2},1,-1}\left|0\right\rangle

(325)

As such, we can see that the action of
S_{rel}^{-}
and
S_{tot}^{-}
on the pairing states with the same Chern number is identical (albeit some signs).

However
, for
e_{1}\neq e_{2}
, we have that the singlet and the triplet states are related, due to the relative (rel)
SU(2)
we find:

\displaystyle S_{rel}^{-}\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+;e_{1},-e_{1},1,1}\left|0\right\rangle=-e_{1}\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+;e_{1},-e_{1},0,0}\left|0\right\rangle;\;\;\;\;

\displaystyle S_{rel}^{-}\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+;e_{1},-e_{1},0,0}\left|0\right\rangle=e_{1}\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+;e_{1},-e_{1},1,-1}\left|0\right\rangle;\;\;\;\;

(326)

with similar relations for
\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},-;e_{1},-e_{1},S,M}
and hence the triplet pairing is actually related to the singlet pairing of the inter-Chern-band pairing, which implies the
inter-Chern-band singlet and triplet pairings to be degenerate
, in the chiral flat-band limit; while the intra-Chern-band triplet and singlet pairings do not need this degeneracy.

We now can rewrite the Hamiltonian (
287
) into the form

H_{el-el}=-\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\sum_{\eta,e_{1},e_{2},s_{1},s_{2}}V_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}^{\eta e_{1}e_{2}}\sum_{S=0,1}\sum_{M=-S\ldots S}\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\eta;e_{1},e_{2},S,M}\Pi_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-\eta;e_{1},e_{2},S,M}.

(327)

With

\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\eta e_{1}s_{1};e_{2},s_{2}}=-\Pi^{\dagger}_{-\mathbf{k},-\eta;e_{2}s_{2};e_{1},s_{1}}

(328)

we have

\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\eta;e_{1},e_{2},0,0}=\Pi^{\dagger}_{-\mathbf{k},-\eta;e_{2},e_{1},0,0};\;\;\;\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\eta;e_{1},e_{2},1,M}=-\Pi^{\dagger}_{-\mathbf{k},-\eta;e_{2},e_{1},1,M},\;\;M=-1,0,1

(329)

Using the reshuffling property, we find that

H_{el-el}=-\frac{2}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\sum_{e_{1},e_{2},s_{1},s_{2}}V_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}^{+e_{1}e_{2}}\sum_{S=0,1}\sum_{M=-S\ldots S}\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+;e_{1},e_{2},S,M}\Pi_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-;e_{1},e_{2},S,M}

(330)

VI.8
Gap equation and Possible Pairing Channels for the Flat Bands

VI.8.1
Linearized gap equation

In this section, we will first drive the linearized gap equation for the TBG Hamiltonian and discuss the properties of the linearized gap equation.

Now let us consider the general interaction

H_{el-el}=-\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\sum_{\alpha\beta\mu\nu}V_{\alpha,\beta,\mu,\nu}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\gamma_{\mathbf{k},\alpha s_{1}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k},\beta s_{2}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\mu s_{2}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\nu s_{1}}

(331)

with composite index
\alpha=e_{1},\eta;\beta=e_{2},-\eta;\mu=e_{2},\eta;\nu=e_{1},-\eta
, and the interaction function
V
is defined as

V_{\alpha,\beta,\mu,\nu}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})=V_{e_{1},\eta;e_{2},-\eta;e_{2},\eta;e_{1},-\eta}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})=V_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}^{\eta e_{1}e_{2}}.

(332)

Next we define the gap function as

\Delta^{\eta}_{\mathbf{k};e_{1}s_{1},e_{2}s_{2}}=-\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}V_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}^{\eta e_{1}e_{2}}\langle\gamma_{-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}e_{2}\eta s_{2}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}e_{1}-\eta s_{1}}\rangle,

(333)

or

\Delta_{\mathbf{k};\alpha s_{1},\beta s_{2}}=-\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}V_{\alpha\beta\mu\nu}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\langle\gamma_{-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\mu s_{2}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\nu s_{1}}\rangle,

(334)

in terms of the notation
\alpha,\beta,\mu,\nu
. With the above form of the gap function, we can perform the mean field decomposition of the
interaction Hamiltonian,

H_{el-el}=\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\sum_{\alpha\beta\mu\nu}\left(\Delta_{\mathbf{k};\alpha s_{1},\beta s_{2}}\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\alpha s_{1}}\gamma^{\dagger}_{-\mathbf{k},\beta s_{2}}+\Delta^{*}_{\mathbf{k};\alpha s_{1},\beta s_{2}}\gamma_{-\mathbf{k},\beta s_{2}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},\alpha s_{1}}-\Delta_{\mathbf{k};\alpha s_{1},\beta s_{2}}\langle\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\alpha s_{1}}\gamma^{\dagger}_{-\mathbf{k},\beta s_{2}}\rangle\right).

(335)

We introduce the anomalous Green’s function in the imaginary time
\tau
as

{\mathcal{F}}_{\nu s_{1},\mu s_{2}}(\mathbf{k},\tau)=\langle T_{\tau}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}\nu s_{1}}(\tau)\gamma_{-\mathbf{k}\mu s_{2}}(0)\rangle,

(336)

where
\langle...\rangle
represents the thermal average, and its Fourier transform

{\mathcal{F}}_{\nu s_{1},\mu s_{2}}(\mathbf{k},\tau)=\frac{1}{\beta}\sum_{i\omega_{n}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\nu s_{1},\mu s_{2}}(\mathbf{k},i\omega_{n})e^{-i\omega_{n}\tau},

(337)

where
\beta=1/k_{B}T
and
i\omega_{n}=(2n+1)\pi/\beta
is the Matsubara frequency.

The gap function can be related to the anomalous Green’s function by

\Delta_{\mathbf{k};\alpha s_{1},\beta s_{2}}=\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mu\nu}V_{\alpha\beta\mu\nu}(\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}){\mathcal{F}}_{\nu s_{1},\mu s_{2}}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},0)=\frac{1}{\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},i\omega_{n},\mu\nu}V_{\alpha\beta\mu\nu}(\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}){\mathcal{F}}_{\nu s_{1},\mu s_{2}}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},i\omega_{n}).

(338)

With the above definition, we can derive the equation of motion for the anomalous Green’s function, given by

\frac{d{\mathcal{F}}_{\nu s_{1},\mu s_{2}}(\mathbf{k},\tau)}{d\tau}=-\sum_{\mu^{\prime}}h_{\mathbf{k},\nu\mu^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}s_{1},\mu s_{2}}(\mathbf{k},\tau)-2\sum_{\beta s_{1}^{\prime}}\Delta_{\mathbf{k},\nu s_{1},\beta s_{1}^{\prime}}G_{\mu s_{2},\beta s_{1}^{\prime}}(-\mathbf{k},-\tau),

(339)

where
h_{\mathbf{k},\nu\mu^{\prime}}
is the single-particle Hamiltonian

H_{0}=\sum_{\mathbf{k},\alpha\beta,s}h_{\mathbf{k},\alpha\beta}\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\alpha,s}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},\beta s}

(340)

and the Green’s function
G
is defined as

G_{\alpha s_{1},\beta s_{2}}(\mathbf{k},\tau)=-\langle T_{\tau}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},\alpha s_{1}}(\tau)\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\beta s_{2}}(0)\rangle

(341)

and its Fourier transform is

G_{\alpha s_{1},\beta s_{2}}(\mathbf{k},\tau)=\frac{1}{\beta}\sum_{i\omega_{n}}G_{\alpha s_{1},\beta s_{2}}(\mathbf{k},i\omega_{n})e^{-i\omega_{n}\tau}.

(342)

In the frequency space, the equation of motion for the anomalous Green’s function then reads

\sum_{\mu^{\prime}}(i\omega_{n}\delta_{\nu\mu^{\prime}}-h_{\mathbf{k},\nu\mu^{\prime}}){\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}s_{1},\mu s_{2}}(\mathbf{k},i\omega_{n})=2\sum_{\mu^{\prime}s_{1}^{\prime}}\Delta_{\mathbf{k},\nu s_{1},\mu^{\prime}s_{1}^{\prime}}G_{\mu s_{2},\mu^{\prime}s_{1}^{\prime}}(-\mathbf{k},-i\omega_{n}).

(343)

Next we can make the approximation of replacing the full Green’s function
G
by the zero-order Green’s function
G_{0}
, in which
the thermal average is only for the single-particle Hamiltonian
H_{0}
. From the equation of motion,
G_{0}
can be solved analytically
and given by

G_{0;\alpha s_{1},\beta s_{2}}(\mathbf{k},i\omega_{n})=\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}[(i\omega_{n}-h_{\mathbf{k}})^{-1}]_{\alpha\beta}

(344)

Thus, up to the lowest order in the gap function
\Delta
, we find the anomalous Green’s function is given by

{\mathcal{F}}_{\nu s_{1},\mu s_{2}}(\mathbf{k},i\omega_{n})=2\sum_{\alpha\beta,s_{1}^{\prime}s_{2}^{\prime}}G_{0;\nu s_{1},\alpha s_{1}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k},i\omega_{n})\Delta_{\mathbf{k},\alpha s_{1}^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}s_{2}^{\prime}}G_{0;\mu s_{2},\beta^{\prime}s_{2}^{\prime}}(-\mathbf{k},-i\omega_{n}).

(345)

Substituting the above form of
{\mathcal{F}}
into Eq. (
338
), we obtain the linearized gap equation

\Delta_{\alpha s_{1}\beta s_{2}}(\mathbf{k})=\frac{2}{\beta N_{M}}\sum_{i\omega_{n},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mu,\nu}V_{\alpha,\beta,\mu,\nu}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})[G_{0}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},i\omega_{n})\Delta(\mathbf{k}^{\prime})G_{0}^{T}(-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-i\omega_{n})]_{\nu s_{1},\mu s_{2}},

(346)

It should be noted that in the Chern-band basis, the single-particle Green’s function is
not
diagonal, and the explicit form of the linearized gap equation
on the Chern-band basis is written as

\Delta_{e_{1}s_{1},e_{2}s_{2};\mathbf{k}}^{\eta}=\frac{2}{\beta N_{M}}\sum_{i\omega_{n},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},e_{1}^{\prime},e_{2}^{\prime}}V^{\eta,e_{1}e_{2}}_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}G_{0,e_{1}e_{1}^{\prime}}^{-\eta}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},i\omega_{n})\Delta^{-\eta}_{e_{1}^{\prime}s_{1},e_{2}^{\prime}s_{2};\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}G^{\eta}_{0,e_{2}e_{2}^{\prime}}(-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-i\omega_{n}),

(347)

It should be noted that the left hand side of the above equation is for gap function
\Delta_{e_{1}s_{1},e_{2}s_{2};\mathbf{k}}^{\eta}=\Delta_{e_{1}\eta s_{1},e_{2}-\eta s_{2}}
,
which has the opposite
\eta
value from
\Delta^{-\eta}_{e_{1}^{\prime}s_{1},e_{2}^{\prime}s_{2};\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}=\Delta_{e_{1}^{\prime}-\eta s_{1},e_{2}^{\prime}\eta s_{2}}
on the right hand side of the equation.
On the Chern-band basis, the single particle Hamiltonian reads

h_{\eta,\mathbf{k}}=(d_{0,\eta,\mathbf{k}}-\mu)\zeta^{0}+d_{x,\eta,\mathbf{k}}\zeta^{x},

(348)

with

d_{0,\eta,\mathbf{k}}=(\epsilon_{+,\eta,\mathbf{k}}+\epsilon_{-,\eta,\mathbf{k}})/2;\;\;d_{x,\eta,\mathbf{k}}=(\epsilon_{+,\eta,\mathbf{k}}-\epsilon_{-,\eta,\mathbf{k}})/2,

(349)

and
\epsilon_{n,\eta,\mathbf{k}}
are the single-particle eigen-energies of the BM model
\hat{H}_{0}
so they should be real.
Time reversal symmetry requires

\epsilon_{n,\eta,\mathbf{k}}=\epsilon_{n,-\eta,-\mathbf{k}}\implies d_{0,-\eta,-\mathbf{k}}=d_{0,\eta,\mathbf{k}};\;d_{x,-\eta,-\mathbf{k}}=d_{x,\eta,\mathbf{k}}\implies h_{-\eta,-\mathbf{k}}=h_{\eta,\mathbf{k}}.

(350)

The corresponding single-particle Green function is given by

G_{0}^{\eta}(\mathbf{k},i\omega_{n})=(i\omega_{n}-h_{\eta,\mathbf{k}})^{-1}=\sum_{m}\frac{P_{m,\mathbf{k},\eta}}{i\omega_{n}-\xi_{m,\eta,\mathbf{k}}},

(351)

where
m=\pm
,
\xi_{m,\eta,\mathbf{k}}=d_{0,\eta,\mathbf{k}}-\mu+m|d_{x,\eta,\mathbf{k}}|
and the projection operator is defined as

P_{m,\mathbf{k},\eta}=\frac{1}{2}\left(1+m\frac{d_{x,\eta,\mathbf{k}}}{|d_{x,\eta,\mathbf{k}}|}\zeta^{x}\right).

(352)

With such form of the single-particle Green’s function, the linearized gap equation can be written as

\Delta_{e_{1}s_{1},e_{2}s_{2};\mathbf{k}}^{\eta}=\frac{2}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},m_{1}m_{2}}V^{\eta,e_{1}e_{2}}_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\left[P_{m_{1},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},-\eta}\Delta^{-\eta}_{s_{1}s_{2},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}P^{T}_{m_{2},-\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\eta}\right]_{e_{1}e_{2}}T^{\eta}_{m_{1}m_{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}},

(353)

where the Mastubara frequency summation can be performed
